. If the reduced mass ratio
\eta^{*}(u_{0})
satisfies the lower-bound condition

(4.4)

\eta^{*}(u_{0})>C_{24}\frac{\delta(u_{0})}{1+\delta(u_{0})}\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta(u_{0})}\right)+C_{23}\left(\frac{\delta(u_{0})r_{1}(u_{0})}{1-\mu^{*}(u_{0})}\right),

with
\delta(u_{0})\in(0,1)
and with constants
C_{23}
and
C_{24}
explicitly given in (
4.35
) and (
4.40
), then a marginally outer trapped surface (MOTS) or a trapped surface is guaranteed to form along the incoming null curve
[u_{0},u_{*}]\times\{v_{2}\}
with
u_{*}\in(u_{0},v_{0})
.

Remark 9
.

In the proof of the later instability theorems, by taking into account the blueshift mechanism, for
u_{0}
sufficiently close to
v_{1}
, we will see that
\frac{1}{1-\mu^{*}(u_{0})}
is of order
\log(\frac{1}{\delta(u_{0})})
and the gained factor
r_{1}(u_{0})
will play a crucial role.

Proof.

With
v_{2}>v_{1}
,
u_{0}<v_{1}
and
u_{*}\in[u_{0},v_{1})
being an arbitrary constant such that
(u_{*},v_{2})\in\mathcal{Q}
, we denote
R^{\prime}:=[u_{0},u_{*}]\times[v_{1},v_{2}]
. Under condition (
4.4
), we explain a mechanism in the regular (untrapped) region, that would lead to MOTS or trapped surface formation.

If there exists a MOTS or a trapped surface within
R^{\prime}
, then the conclusion of Theorem
4.1
is achieved. We then proceed to assume the absence of a MOTS or a trapped surface in
R^{\prime}
. By Remark
8
, if
v_{2}
and
u_{0}
are picked sufficiently close to
v_{1}
, for each point
(u,v)\in R^{\prime}
, the hypothesis of Proposition
3.3
is satisfied. Consequently, we have that (
3.8
) holds for any
\chi\in(0,1)
and we obtain

(4.5)

\mu-\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}(u,v)\geq\left(1-C_{1}^{2}(\overline{u},v;\chi)r^{1-\chi}(u,v)r^{\chi}(\overline{u},v)(v-u)\right)\mu(u,v).

Here,
C_{1}(\overline{u},v;\chi)
is defined in (
3.20
). By the expression there, we can also see that
C_{1}(\overline{u},v;\chi)
is monotonically non-decreasing in
v
for
v\in(u,v_{3}]
. Hence, we obtain

(4.6)

\mu-\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}(u,v)\geq\left(1-C_{1}^{2}(\overline{u},v_{3};\chi)r(\overline{u},v_{3})(v-u)\right)\mu(u,v).

Using
v-u=v-v_{1}+v_{1}-u\leq(v_{2}-v_{1})+(v_{1}-u_{0})
for all
u\in[u_{0},v_{1}]
and
v\in[v_{1},v_{2}]
, if we pick
v_{2}
and
u_{0}
sufficiently close to
v_{1}
such that

(4.7)

(v_{2}-v_{1})+(v_{1}-u_{0})\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}^{2}(\overline{u},v_{3};\chi)r(\overline{u},v_{3})},

then we deduce that
\mu-\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}\geq 0
on
R^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{R}
.

With these preparations, we are ready to prove the main theorem. Introducing a scale-free length
x
by

(4.8)

x(u)=\frac{r_{2}(u)}{r_{2}(u_{0})},

we then consider the evolution of
\eta^{*}(x)
along incoming null curves lying within
R^{\prime}
. For
x\in(0,1]
, we have

(4.9)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\eta^{*}}{\mathrm{d}x}=

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{\mathrm{d}\eta^{*}}{\mathrm{d}u}}{\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{\mathrm{d}u}}=\frac{\partial_{u}\left(\frac{2(m_{2}-m_{1})}{r_{2}}-\frac{2}{r_{2}}\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)}{\frac{\partial_{u}r_{2}}{r_{2}(u_{0})}}

\displaystyle=\;\frac{r_{2}(u_{0})}{\partial_{u}r_{2}}\left(-\frac{\partial_{u}r_{2}}{r_{2}}\left(\frac{2(m_{2}-m_{1})}{r_{2}}+\frac{2}{r_{2}}\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)+\frac{2}{r_{2}}\partial_{u}(m_{2}-m_{1})-\frac{2}{r_{2}}\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}\partial_{u}\left(\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\;-\frac{\eta^{*}}{x}-\frac{2}{x(\partial_{u}r_{2})}\left(\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}m+\partial_{u}\left(\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right).

Note that the term
\partial_{u}\left(\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{u}r}{2r^{2}}\right)
comes from the definition of
\eta^{*}(u)
in (
4.4
), and it is designed to have a precise cancellation with terms in
-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}m
. This is illustrated using (
1.26
), where we see that

(4.10)

-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}m+\partial_{u}\left(\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}\right)=-\partial_{u}\left(\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}\right).

Hence, with the help of (
1.20
), the integrand in the last line of (
4.9
) can be simplified as

(4.11)

\displaystyle-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}m+\partial_{u}\left(\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\partial_{u}\left(\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}\right)

\displaystyle=-2\pi\left(\partial_{u}\left(\frac{1-\mu}{\partial_{v}r}\right)r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}+\left(\frac{1-\mu}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\partial_{u}(r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2})\right)

\displaystyle=8\pi^{2}r^{3}(1-\mu)(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)\frac{|D_{u}\phi|^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{(\partial_{u}r)^{2}(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}-2\pi\left(\frac{1-\mu}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\partial_{u}\left(r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}\right).

For
\partial_{u}(r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2})
, from (
1.24
) and the definition of
D_{u}
, we have

(4.12)

\partial_{u}(r\partial_{v}\phi)=-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi-\partial_{v}rD_{u}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r(1-\mu)}

and thus

(4.13)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}(r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2})=

\displaystyle\partial_{u}(r\partial_{v}\phi)(r\partial_{v}\phi)^{\dagger}+\partial_{u}((r\partial_{v}\phi)^{\dagger})(r\partial_{v}\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r(\partial_{v}\phi)^{\dagger}\left(-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi-\partial_{v}rD_{u}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)

\displaystyle+r(\partial_{v}\phi)\left(-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi-\partial_{v}rD_{u}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2r\partial_{v}r\re{\left((\partial_{v}\phi)^{\dagger}D_{u}\phi\right)}+2\mathfrak{e}Q\frac{(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{1-\mu}\im{(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)}.

Combining these calculations, we obtain

(4.14)

\displaystyle-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}m+\partial_{u}\left(\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\partial_{v}r)(-\partial_{u}r)\left(-8\pi^{2}r^{3}(1-\mu)\left(\frac{|D_{u}\phi||\partial_{v}\phi|}{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}\right)^{2}+4\pi r(1-\mu)\frac{\re{\left((\partial_{v}\phi)^{\dagger}(D_{u}\phi)\right)}}{\partial_{v}r(-\partial_{u}r)}\right)

\displaystyle+4\pi\mathfrak{e}Q(-\partial_{u}r)\im{(\phi^{\dagger}(\partial_{v}\phi))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\pi^{2}r^{3}(\partial_{v}r)(-\partial_{u}r)(1-\mu)\left(-\left(\frac{|D_{u}\phi||\partial_{v}\phi|}{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2\pi r^{2}}\frac{|D_{u}\phi||\partial_{v}\phi|}{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}\right)+4\pi\mathfrak{e}|Q|(-\partial_{u}r)|\phi||\partial_{v}\phi|.

Using the fact that
\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r\leq 0
in
R^{\prime}
and equation (
4.14
), the integral in (
4.9
) can be simplified to

(4.15)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\partial_{u}r_{2}}\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}m+\partial_{u}\left(\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}J_{4}(u,v^{\prime})+J_{5}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

with

(4.16)

J_{4}=8\pi^{2}r^{3}(\partial_{v}r)(1-\mu)\left(-\left(\frac{|D_{u}\phi||\partial_{v}\phi|}{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2\pi r^{2}}\frac{|D_{u}\phi||\partial_{v}\phi|}{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}\right)

and

(4.17)

J_{5}=4\pi\mathfrak{e}|Q||\phi||\partial_{v}\phi|.

For
J_{4}
, by using an elementary inequality
-x^{2}+ax\leq\frac{a^{2}}{4}
, we have

(4.18)

\displaystyle\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}8\pi^{2}r^{3}(\partial_{v}r)(1-\mu)\left(-\left(\frac{|D_{u}\phi||\partial_{v}\phi|}{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2\pi r^{2}}\frac{|D_{u}\phi||\partial_{v}\phi|}{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}\right)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}2\pi^{2}r^{3}(\partial_{v}r)(1-\mu)\left(\frac{1}{2\pi r^{2}}\right)^{2}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\;\frac{1}{2}\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\;\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r(u,v_{2})}{r(u,v_{1})}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(1+\frac{r(u,v_{2})-r(u,v_{1})}{r(u,v_{1})}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta(u).

Note that via our new approach,
J_{4}
is bounded by
\frac{1}{2}\delta(u)
independent of
\phi
through a simple computation.

For
J_{5}
, with (
1.26
) and the definition in (
4.2
), we proceed to deduce that

(4.19)

\displaystyle\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}J_{5}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}4\pi\mathfrak{e}|Q||\phi||\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{8\pi}\mathfrak{e}\left(\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{|Q|^{2}|\phi|^{2}}{r^{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{8\pi}\mathfrak{e}\left(\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}\partial_{v}m-\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{|Q|^{2}|\phi|^{2}}{r^{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{4\pi}\mathfrak{e}{\eta^{*}}^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}(u)\left(\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{|Q|^{2}|\phi|^{2}}{r^{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Employing (
3.9
) of Proposition
3.3
with
\xi_{1}=2
and
\xi_{2}=\chi
for
\chi\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right)
, we obtain the bound

(4.20)

|Q\phi|^{2}(u,v)\leq C_{5}(\overline{u},v_{2};\chi,2,\chi)^{2}r^{\frac{5}{2}-\frac{3\chi}{2}}(u,v),

where we have used the fact that
\mu<1
. Returning to (
4.19
), with (
4.20
) and
\chi\in\left(0,\frac{1}{3}\right]
, we thus have

(4.21)

\displaystyle\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}J_{5}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{4\pi}\mathfrak{e}{\eta^{*}}^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}(u)\left(\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{|Q|^{2}|\phi|^{2}}{r^{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{4\pi}\mathfrak{e}C_{5}{\eta^{*}}^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}(u)\left(\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}(u_{0},v^{\prime})r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\chi}{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{4\pi}\mathfrak{e}C_{5}{\eta^{*}}^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{2}^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{3\chi}{4}}(u)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}^{*}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}\partial_{v}r(u_{0},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{4\pi}\mathfrak{e}C_{5}}{(1-\mu_{0}^{*})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\eta^{*}}^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{2}^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{3\chi}{4}}(u)r_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}(u_{0})\delta(u_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Utilizing (
4.15
), (
4.18
), (
4.21
) and the fact that
x\in(0,1]
, the main inequality (
4.9
) simplifies to

(4.22)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\eta^{*}}{\mathrm{d}x}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\eta^{*}}{x}-\frac{2}{x(\partial_{u}r_{2})}\left(\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}m+\partial_{u}\left(\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\eta^{*}}{x}+\frac{\delta}{x}+\frac{1}{x}\frac{4\sqrt{\pi}\mathfrak{e}C_{5}}{(1-\mu_{0}^{*})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\eta^{*}}^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{2}^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{3\chi}{4}}(u)r_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}(u_{0})\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}(u_{0})

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\eta^{*}}{x}+\frac{\delta}{x}+C_{21}x^{-\frac{1}{4}-\frac{3\chi}{4}}\left(\frac{2{\eta^{*}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(r_{1})_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(1-\mu_{0}^{*})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\eta^{*}}{x}+\frac{\delta}{x}+\frac{C_{21}}{x^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\eta^{*}+\frac{\delta_{0}(r_{1})_{0}}{1-\mu_{0}^{*}}\right).

Furthermore, the constant
C_{21}
is defined as

(4.23)

C_{21}=2\sqrt{\pi}\mathfrak{e}C_{5}r(\overline{u},v_{3})^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{3\chi}{4}}

with
\chi\in\left(0,\frac{1}{3}\right]
. Meanwhile, for all
(u,v)\in R^{\prime}
, using
\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r\leq 0
in
R^{\prime}
, we get

(4.24)

\partial_{v}r(u,v)\leq\partial_{v}r(u_{0},v).

For
u\in[u_{0},v_{1})
, this implies

(4.25)

r_{2}(u)-r_{1}(u)\leq r_{2}(u_{0})-r_{1}(u_{0}).

Thus, one can establish the following inequality

(4.26)

\displaystyle\delta(u)=\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}-1

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{r_{2}-(r_{2}-r_{1})}\leq\frac{r_{2}(u_{0})-r_{1}(u_{0})}{r_{2}(u)-(r_{2}(u_{0})-r_{1}(u_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta(u_{0})}{\frac{r_{2}(u)}{r_{1}(u_{0})}-\delta(u_{0})}=\frac{\delta_{0}}{x(u)(1+\delta_{0})-\delta_{0}}.

Rewriting (
4.22
), we hence arrive at

(4.27)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\eta^{*}}{\mathrm{d}x}+\left(\frac{1}{x}-\frac{C_{21}}{x^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\eta^{*}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{0}\left(\frac{1}{x(x(1+\delta_{0})-\delta_{0})}+\frac{C_{21}(r_{1})_{0}}{x^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\mu_{0}^{*})}\right).

This can be rewritten as

(4.28)

\frac{\mathrm{d}\eta^{*}}{\mathrm{d}x}+f(x)\eta^{*}\leq\delta_{0}g(x)

with

(4.29)

f(x)=\frac{1}{x}-\frac{C_{21}}{x^{\frac{1}{2}}}\quad\text{ and }\quad g(x)=\frac{1}{x(x(1+\delta_{0})-\delta_{0})}+\frac{C_{21}(r_{1})_{0}}{x^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\mu_{0}^{*})}.

We then solve the differential inequality (
4.28
) and get

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\left(e^{-\int^{1}_{x}f(s)\mathrm{d}s}\eta^{*}\right)\leq\delta_{0}g(x)e^{-\int^{1}_{x}f(s)\mathrm{d}s},

which implies that, for any
x\in(x(u_{*}),1)
, we obtain

\eta^{*}_{0}-e^{-\int^{1}_{x}f(s)\mathrm{d}s}\eta^{*}(x)\leq\delta_{0}\int^{1}_{x}g(s)e^{-\int^{1}_{s}f(s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}s.

Recall that in the regular region definition (
4.2
) implies
\eta_{*}(x)\leq\mu_{2}(x)
. Rearranging the above inequality, we get

(4.30)

\mu_{2}(x)\geq\eta^{*}(x)\geq e^{\int_{x}^{1}f(s)\mathrm{d}s}\left(\eta_{0}^{*}-\delta_{0}\int_{x}^{1}g(s)e^{-\int_{s}^{1}f(s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}}\right).

We further evaluate the integrals in (
4.30
) as follows. We first calculate

(4.31)

\displaystyle\int^{1}_{x}f(s)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\int^{1}_{x}\frac{1}{s}-\frac{C_{21}}{s^{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathrm{d}s=-\log(x)-2C_{21}\left(1-x^{\frac{1}{2}}\right),

which implies

(4.32)

\displaystyle e^{-\int^{1}_{x}f(s)\mathrm{d}s}=xe^{2C_{21}(1-x^{\frac{1}{2}})}\leq xe^{2C_{21}}.

The second integral in (
4.30
) obeys

(4.33)

\displaystyle\int^{1}_{x}g(s)e^{-\int^{1}_{s}f(s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int^{1}_{x}\left(\frac{1}{s(s(1+\delta_{0})-\delta_{0})}+\frac{C_{21}(r_{1})_{0}}{s^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\mu_{0}^{*})}\right)se^{2C_{21}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq e^{2C_{21}}\left(\frac{\log\left(\frac{1}{x(1+\delta_{0})-\delta_{0}}\right)}{1+\delta_{0}}+\frac{2C_{21}(r_{1})_{0}}{3(1-\mu_{0}^{*})}\left(1-x^{\frac{3}{2}}\right)\right)

if we impose
x>\frac{\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}
. For our following arguments, we restrict ourselves to
x\in\left(\frac{\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}},1\right]
. Back to (
4.30
), with (
4.32
) and (
4.33
), we now obtain

(4.34)

\mu_{2}(x)\geq\frac{1}{C_{22}x}\left(\eta_{0}^{*}-C_{22}\frac{\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}\log\left(\frac{1}{x(1+\delta_{0})-\delta_{0}}\right)-C_{23}\frac{\delta_{0}(r_{1})_{0}}{1-\mu_{0}^{*}}\right)

with

(4.35)

C_{22}=e^{2C_{21}}\quad\text{ and }\quad C_{23}=\frac{2C_{21}e^{2C_{21}}}{3}.

Next, we consider the following two cases:

•

Suppose that
|\mathcal{B}_{0}|\geq v_{2}-v_{1}
. This scenario corresponds to the picture on the left of Figure
6
. As seen from the picture, this allows us to set
u_{*}
to be arbitrarily close to
v_{1}
. Furthermore, observe that at
(u_{*},v_{2})
, we have

(4.36)

x(u_{*})\leq\frac{r_{1}(u_{*})}{r_{2}(u_{0})}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{1+\frac{r_{2}(u_{0})-r_{1}(u_{0})}{r_{1}(u_{0})}}=\frac{r_{1}(u_{*})}{r_{2}(u_{0})}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}.

Henceforth, by (
4.36
) and the fact
that
r_{1}(u_{*})\rightarrow 0
as
u_{*}\rightarrow v_{1}^{-}
, we can have
x(u_{*})\leq\frac{p\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}
for any
p>1
.

•

Assume that
|\mathcal{B}_{0}|<v_{2}-v_{1}
. We encounter the scenario portrayed in the right picture of Figure
6
. As observed from the picture, we might not be able to pick
u_{*}
to be arbitrarily close to
v_{1}
due to the possibly spacelike nature of
\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}
. Nonetheless,
r_{2}(u)\rightarrow 0
as
(u,v_{2})
approaches any point on
\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}
. This implies that it suffices to pick
u_{*}
along
v=v_{2}
to be sufficiently close to
\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}
and we conclude
x(u_{*})\leq\frac{p\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}
for any
p>1
as in the previous case.

For both scenarios, since
x(u)
is a decreasing continuous function on
[u_{0},u_{*}]
, there exists
\tilde{u}(k)
such that
x(\tilde{u}(k))=\frac{k\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}
for any
k>1
. We then evaluate (
4.34
) at the point
(\tilde{u}(k),v_{2})
. Further applying (
4.4
) we then obtain

(4.37)

\mu(\tilde{u}(k),v_{2})\geq\frac{1}{C_{22}k}\left((C_{24}-C_{22})\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{0}}\right)+C_{22}\log\left(k-1\right)\right).

Hence, we would have
\mu(\tilde{u}(k),v_{2})\geq 1
if

(4.38)

(C_{24}-C_{22})\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{0}}\right)\geq C_{22}\left(k-\log(k-1)\right).

Note that with
k>1
the function
k-\log(k-1)
is minimized at
k=2
. Henceforth, we get
\mu(\tilde{u}(2),v_{2})\geq 1
if

(4.39)

(C_{24}-C_{22})\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{0}}\right)\geq 2C_{22}.

This requirement is easily satisfied if we set

(4.40)

C_{24}=3C_{22},

since we have
\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{0}}\right)\geq 1
for
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
. With this choice of
C_{24}
, we then obtain
\mu(\tilde{u}(2),v_{2})\geq 1
, which guarantees that a MOTS or a trapped surface is formed in
R^{\prime}
. This concludes the proof of Theorem
4.1
.
∎

5.
BV Area Estimates

In this section, we establish scale-critical BV estimates for
C^{1}
solutions with BV initial data. To begin, we first define the concept of area for maps of two Lipschitz functions. Let
\mathcal{U}
be a domain in the
(u,v)
plane and let
f,g
be a pair of Lipschitz functions defined on it. The area
A(\mathcal{U})[f,g]
for the image of
\mathcal{U}
lying in a plane by the mapping
(u,v)\rightarrow(f(u),g(v))
is given by

(5.1)

A(\mathcal{U})[f,g]:=\int_{\mathcal{U}}\left|\frac{\partial(f,g)}{\partial(u,v)}\right|\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

with

(5.2)

\frac{\partial(f,g)}{\partial(u,v)}=\frac{\partial f}{\partial u}\frac{\partial g}{\partial v}-\frac{\partial f}{\partial v}\frac{\partial g}{\partial u}

being the Jacobian of the mapping
(u,v)\rightarrow(f(u,v),g(u,v))
.

A major difference between our charged system (
1.11
)-(
1.18
) and the uncharged scalar-field system investigated in
[
18
]
-
[
21
]
is that the charged system is
not
invariant under translations of
\phi
. In other words, for any constant
c
, the following transformation

r\rightarrow r,\quad\Omega\rightarrow\Omega,\quad\phi\rightarrow\phi+c,

does not necessarily leaves all equations from (
1.11
) - (
1.18
) invariant. This means that we will have to modify the area estimates obtained in
[
19
]
, in which it was assumed without loss of generality that
\phi(0,0)=0
. In our case, we denote

(5.3)

\phi_{0}:=\phi(0,0),\quad\text{ and }\quad\tilde{\phi}:=\phi-\phi_{0}.

Correspondingly, we define

(5.4)

\mathfrak{a}:=\partial_{v}(r\phi),\quad\quad\alpha:=\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}=\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\partial_{v}r},

and

(5.5)

\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}:=\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi}),\quad\quad\tilde{\alpha}:=\frac{\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})}{\partial_{v}r}=\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}}{\partial_{v}r}.

Analogously, we set

(5.6)

\mathfrak{b}:=\partial_{u}(r\phi),\quad\quad\beta:=\frac{\partial_{u}(r\phi)}{\partial_{u}r}=\frac{\mathfrak{b}}{\partial_{u}r},

and

(5.7)

\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}:=\partial_{u}(r\tilde{\phi})\quad\quad\tilde{\beta}:=\frac{\partial_{u}(r\tilde{\phi})}{\partial_{u}r}=\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}}{\partial_{u}r}.

For convenience, we will also denote

(5.8)

\lambda:=\partial_{v}r,\quad\text{ and }\nu:=\partial_{u}r.

Remark 10
.

The above definitions of
\alpha
and
\beta
are slightly different from
[
19
]
, where
\alpha
and
\beta
are defined to be just
\partial_{v}(r\phi)
and
\partial_{u}(r\phi)
. To be consistent with the notations adopted in
[
21
]
, we will adopt the definitions presented in this paper.

From the above definitions, it is not difficult to see that

(5.9)

\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{C_{0}^{+}}=\mathfrak{a}|_{C_{0}^{+}}-\frac{1}{2}\phi_{0}.

Therefore, the total variations of
\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}
and
\mathfrak{a}
are the same. Hence, we define

(5.10)

D:=TV_{\{0\}\times(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}]=TV_{\{0\}\times(0,v_{0})}[\mathfrak{a}],

which measures the size of the initial data in the TV (total variation) norm. In addition, we define the following quantities

(5.11)

\displaystyle X(v_{0})

\displaystyle=\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}\int^{v_{0}}_{u}|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}\int^{v_{0}}_{u}|\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime},

\displaystyle Y(v_{0})

\displaystyle=\sup_{v\in(0,v_{0})}\int^{v}_{0}|\partial_{u}\phi|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}=\sup_{v\in(0,v_{0})}\int^{v}_{0}|\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

For ease of computation, we pick
v_{0}
sufficiently small, such that
\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}\geq 0
in
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
. We assume that we have a
C^{1}
solution on
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
and
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})\subset\mathcal{R}.
This means that we can apply Proposition
3.3
with
\overline{u}=0
. In particular, by (
3.8
), we have that
\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}\geq 0
only if

(5.12)

C_{1}^{2}(0,v;\chi)r^{1-\chi}(u,v)r^{\chi}(0,v)(v-u)\leq 1

for all
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0}).
By restricting our choice of
v_{0}
to stay in
(0,1)
, it suffices to demand that

(5.13)

C_{1}^{2}(0,1;\chi)r^{1-\chi}(u,v)r^{\chi}(0,v)(v-u)\leq 1

since by (
3.20
), we know that
C_{1}(0,\cdot;\chi)
is a non-decreasing function. In particular, from (
2.6
) and the fact that
\partial_{u}r<0
,
\partial_{v}r>0
, and
u\geq 0
, if we pick
\chi=\frac{1}{2}
, it then suffices to demand that

(5.14)

v_{0}^{2}\leq\frac{2}{C_{1}^{2}\left(0,1;\frac{1}{2}\right)}.

For instance, by choosing

(5.15)

v_{0}=\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\sqrt{2}}{C_{1}\left(0,1;\frac{1}{2}\right)}\right\},

we then have
v_{0}<1
and (
5.14
) are satisfied. Note that such a choice is
independent
of
D
, which is important in this section. In the arguments that follow, we will refine
v_{0}
to be even smaller if necessary, as long as it remains to be independent of
D
.

Last but not least, we work with the following assumptions for solutions in
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
:

(5.16)

\inf_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}\partial_{v}r\geq\frac{5}{12},\quad\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}(-\partial_{u}r)\leq\frac{2}{3},\quad\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}\mu\leq\frac{1}{3}.

These assumptions can be verified directly with the same type of arguments as in Section 6 of
[
19
]
.

With the above preparation, we now state the main theorem of this section.

Theorem 5.1
.

(
BV
Area Estimates.)
Assume that (
5.16
) holds with our choice of
v_{0}
to be given in (
5.98
). Then there exists a constant
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1]
such that if the initial data satisfy

(5.17)

Z(v_{0})=\max\{X(v_{0}),Y(v_{0})\}\leq\varepsilon_{0},\quad\text{ and }\quad D:=TV_{\{0\}\times(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}]\leq\varepsilon_{0},

then we have

(5.18)

Z(v_{0})\lesssim D,

(5.19)

\begin{aligned} \sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}]&\lesssim D,\\
\sup_{v\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{(0,v)\times\{v\}}[\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}]&\lesssim D,\\
TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{\Gamma}]&\lesssim D,\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} \sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\log\lambda]&\lesssim D^{2},\\
\sup_{v\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{(0,v)\times\{v\}}[\log|\nu|]&\lesssim D^{2},\\
TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\log(\lambda|_{\Gamma})]&\lesssim D^{2},\\
\end{aligned}

and

(5.20)

\begin{aligned} A[\tilde{\phi}^{\dagger},\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}/\lambda]&\lesssim D^{2},\\
A[\tilde{\phi}^{\dagger},\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}/\nu]&\lesssim D^{2},\\
\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} A[\lambda,\tilde{\phi}]&\lesssim D^{3},\\
A[\nu,\tilde{\phi}]&\lesssim D^{3}.\\
\end{aligned}

Remark 11
.

For a quantity
H
, we write
H\lesssim D
to mean that there exists a non-negative constant
c
(possibly depending on
\mathfrak{e}
and
|\phi_{0}|
) independent of
D
such that
H\leq cD
. For brevity, we also write
A[f,g]
to mean
A(\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0}))[f,g]
for any pair of Lipschitz functions
f
and
g
.

To prove this theorem, we first state and prove several lemmas.

Lemma 5.2
.

For any
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
, we have

(5.21)

\displaystyle\int^{v_{0}}_{u}|\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\tilde{\alpha}],\quad\int^{v}_{0}|\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq TV_{(0,v)\times\{v\}}[\tilde{\beta}].

The proof of this lemma is similar to the corresponding one in
[
19
]
and we outline the approach.

Proof.

Using the fact that
r\phi|_{\Gamma}=0
and
r\phi\in C^{1}
, for each
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
, we have

(5.22)

\phi(u,v)=\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\partial_{v}(r\phi)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

This implies

(5.23)

\displaystyle(\partial_{v}\phi)(u,v)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\partial_{v}r(u,v)}{r^{2}(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\partial_{v}(r\phi)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}+\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)(u,v)}{r(u,v)}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\partial_{v}r)(u,v)}{r^{2}(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\left(\alpha(u,v)-\alpha(u,v^{\prime})\right)(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Next, since
\phi
,
r\partial_{v}\phi
,
\partial_{v}r
are
C^{1}
functions on
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
, this implies that
\alpha\in C^{1}
on
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
. Consequently, we have

(5.24)

\lim_{v\rightarrow u+}\frac{\int^{v}_{u}\left(\alpha(u,v)-\alpha(u,v^{\prime})\right)(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}}{r(u,v)}=0.

We further denote

(5.25)

I_{\alpha}(u,v):=\int^{v}_{u}\left(\alpha(u,v)-\alpha(u,v^{\prime})\right)(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

and it satisfies

(5.26)

\partial_{v}I_{\alpha}=r\partial_{v}\alpha.

From (
5.23
), integrating along
v\in(u,v_{0})
and applying integration by parts, we obtain

(5.27)

\displaystyle\int_{u}^{v_{0}}|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\int_{u}^{v_{0}}|I_{\alpha}(u,v^{\prime})|\mathrm{d}\left(\frac{1}{r(u,v^{\prime})}\right)=-\frac{|I_{\alpha}|(u,v_{0})}{r(u,v_{0})}+\int^{v_{0}}_{u}\frac{\partial|I_{\alpha}(u,v^{\prime})|}{\partial v}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{r(u,v^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq\int^{v_{0}}_{u}\left|\partial_{v}I_{\alpha}(u,v^{\prime})\right|\frac{1}{r(u,v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=\int^{v_{0}}_{u}|\partial_{v}\alpha|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\alpha].

Together with the fact that
\partial_{v}\phi=\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}
and
TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\alpha]=TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\tilde{\alpha}]
, inequality (
5.27
) implies the first half of (
5.21
).

For the second half of (
5.21
), we begin by writing

(5.28)

\phi(u,v)=-\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\partial_{u}(r\phi)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

We proceed as above and compute

(5.29)

(\partial_{u}\phi)(u,v)=\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(u,v)}{r^{2}(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\left(\beta(u,v)-\beta(u^{\prime},v)\right)(\partial_{u}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Next, we denote

(5.30)

I_{\beta}(u,v):=\int^{v}_{u}\left(\beta(u,v)-\beta(u^{\prime},v)\right)(\partial_{u}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

and observe that

(5.31)

\lim_{u\rightarrow v^{-}}\frac{I_{\beta}(u,v)}{r(u,v)}=0\quad\text{ and }\quad\partial_{u}I_{\beta}=-r\partial_{u}\beta.

Integrating (
5.29
) along
u\in(0,v)
, we obtain

(5.32)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{0}|\partial_{u}\phi|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\int^{v}_{0}|I_{\beta}(u^{\prime},v)|\mathrm{d}\left(\frac{1}{r(u^{\prime},v)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\int^{v}_{0}|\partial_{u}\beta|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}=TV_{(0,v)\times\{v\}}[\beta].

Since
TV_{(0,v)\times\{v\}}[\beta]=TV_{(0,v)\times\{v\}}[\tilde{\beta}]
, we then prove the second inequality in (
5.21
).
∎

We now attempt to derive estimates for the following quantities:

(5.33)

A:=\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|,\quad B:=\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}|,\quad P:=\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}|\tilde{\phi}|.

We summarize the estimates for these three quantities in a lemma below.

Lemma 5.3
.

Suppose that
D\leq 1
. By refining of
v_{0}
to be as described in (
5.66
), we have

(5.34)

A\leq 4D,\quad B\leq 8D,\quad P\leq 12D.

Proof.

We begin by considering the set

(5.35)

\mathcal{\tilde{D}}=\left\{(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0}):\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}\left|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}\right|\leq 4D,\quad\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}\left|\tilde{\phi}\right|\leq 12D\right\}.

Note that
(0,0)\in\mathcal{\tilde{D}}
and
\mathcal{\tilde{D}}
is a closed subset of
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
. It remains to show that
\mathcal{\tilde{D}}
is open.

First, observe that for each
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
, we have

(5.36)

r(u,v)=\int^{v}_{u}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\geq(v-u)\inf_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}(\partial_{v}r)\geq\frac{5}{12}(v-u)\geq\frac{1}{3}(v-u).

Furthermore, analogous to (
5.22
), we get

(5.37)

\tilde{\phi}=\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Applying (
5.36
) to (
5.37
), we hence obtain

(5.38)

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\leq\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}\left|\frac{v-u}{r(u,v)}\right|\leq 3\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|.

Next, within
\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})
, we then compute
\partial_{u}(\partial_{v}(r\phi))
using (
1.4
), (
1.15
), (
1.16
) as follows

(5.39)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\left(\partial_{v}(r\phi)\right)

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi+r\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\phi+\partial_{u}\phi\partial_{v}r+\phi\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r

\displaystyle=\phi\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}\partial_{v}(r\phi)-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}.

The above implies

(5.40)

\partial_{u}\left(\partial_{v}\left(r\tilde{\phi}\right)\right)=\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\phi_{0}A_{u}\partial_{v}r-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}.

By viewing (
5.40
) as a first order ODE for
\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})
in the
u
-direction and noting that the integrating factor
e^{\int^{u}_{0}\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}
only contributes to a phase change, we henceforth derive

(5.41)

\displaystyle e^{\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{0}A_{u}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})(u,v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})(0,v)+\int^{u}_{0}e^{\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\int^{u^{\prime}}_{0}A_{u}(u^{\prime\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}}\left(\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\phi_{0}A_{u}\partial_{v}r-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

which implies that

(5.42)

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})|(u,v)\leq

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})|(0,v)+\int^{u}_{0}|\tilde{\phi}|(-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r(1-\mu)}|Q||\phi|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\mathfrak{e}|\phi_{0}|\int^{u}_{0}|A_{u}|\partial_{v}r(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

In the above estimate, we have used the fact that
\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}\geq 0
from our choice of
v_{0}
in (
5.66
) and its implication
-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r\geq 0
by (
1.19
).

It remains to estimate the integrals in (
5.42
). By employing
\partial_{v}r\geq\frac{5}{12}
and (
2.6
), the first integral obeys

(5.43)

\displaystyle\int^{u}_{0}|\tilde{\phi}|(-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)\left((\partial_{v}r)(0,v)-(\partial_{v}r)(u,v)\right)\leq\frac{1}{12}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right).

For the second integral in (
5.42
), since
|\phi|\leq|\tilde{\phi}|+|\phi_{0}|\leq\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)+|\phi_{0}|
for every
(u^{\prime},v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})
, we have

(5.44)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r(1-\mu)}|Q||\phi|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)\int^{u}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r(1-\mu)}|Q|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{e}|\phi_{0}|\int^{u}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r(1-\mu)}|Q|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

For the two integrals in (
5.44
), we apply two different estimates for
|Q|
. For the first integral, since
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})\subset\mathcal{R}
, we have
\mu\leq 1
and
\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}\geq 0
, which in turn imply
|Q|\leq\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}r\leq r
. In light of Lemma
3.1
and our assumptions in (
5.16
), we thus have

(5.45)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)

\displaystyle\int^{u}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r(1-\mu)}|Q|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\mathfrak{e}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)\int^{u}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{e}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}(0,v)\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathfrak{e}\frac{3v}{8}\right)\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)\leq\left(\mathfrak{e}\frac{3v_{0}}{8}\right)\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)\leq\frac{1}{12}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)

as long as we refine
v_{0}
from (
5.15
) to be

(5.46)

v_{0}=\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\sqrt{2}}{C_{1}\left(0,1;\frac{1}{2}\right)},\frac{2}{9\mathfrak{e}}\right\}.

For the second integral in (
5.44
), we use (
1.18
),
Q|_{\Gamma}=0
and their implication

(5.47)

\displaystyle|Q|(u,v)\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{e}\int^{v}_{u}r^{2}|\phi||\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{e}\left(|\phi_{0}|+\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)r(u,v)\int^{v}_{u}r|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

We further employ (
5.36
) and obtain

(5.48)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{u}r|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int^{v}_{u}r|\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\int^{v}_{u}|\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})|(u,v^{\prime})+|\tilde{\phi}|(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)(v-u)+\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)r(u,v)\leq 6\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)r(u,v).

Plugging (
5.48
) into (
5.47
), we deduce that

(5.49)

|Q|(u,v)\leq 24\pi\mathfrak{e}\left(\left|\phi_{0}\right|+\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)r(u,v)^{2}.

Analogous to (
5.45
), by using (
5.49
), we estimate the second integral in (
5.44
) and get

(5.50)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}|\phi_{0}|\int^{u}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r(1-\mu)}|Q|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 12\pi v_{0}\mathfrak{e}^{2}|\phi_{0}|\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)\left(\left|\phi_{0}\right|+\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)\int^{u}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{9}{2}\pi v_{0}^{2}\mathfrak{e}^{2}|\phi_{0}|\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)\left(\left|\phi_{0}\right|+\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right).

For the remaining integral in (
5.42
), we begin by estimating
|A_{u}|
. For
(u^{\prime},v)\in\mathcal{\tilde{D}}
, employing (
1.31
), (
2.12
), (
5.49
), we deduce

(5.51)

\displaystyle|A_{u}|(u^{\prime},v)

\displaystyle\leq\int^{v}_{u^{\prime}}\frac{2|Q|(-\partial_{u}r)\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 48\pi\mathfrak{e}\left(\left|\phi_{0}\right|+\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,u^{\prime},v)}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)\int^{v}_{u^{\prime}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 48\pi\mathfrak{e}\left(\left|\phi_{0}\right|+\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,u^{\prime},v)}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)r(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq 24\pi v_{0}\mathfrak{e}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)\left(\left|\phi_{0}\right|+\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,u^{\prime},v)}|\tilde{\phi}|\right).

The above further implies

(5.52)

\mathfrak{e}|\phi_{0}|\int^{u}_{0}|A_{u}|(\partial_{v}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq 12\pi v_{0}^{2}\mathfrak{e}^{2}|\phi_{0}|\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)\left(\left|\phi_{0}\right|+\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right).

We now plug (
5.43
), (
5.45
), (
5.50
) into (
5.42
). Together with (
5.38
), we obtain

(5.53)

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})|(u,v)=

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})|(0,v)+\int^{u}_{0}|\tilde{\phi}|(-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r(1-\mu)}|Q||\phi|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{e}|\phi_{0}|\int^{u}_{0}|A_{u}|\partial_{v}r(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle D+\frac{1}{6}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)+\frac{33}{2}\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}|\phi_{0}|v_{0}^{2}\left(\left|\phi_{0}\right|+\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right).

In (
5.53
), we use
D:=TV_{\{0\}\times(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}]
,
\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}(0,0)=0
and the implication
|\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})|(0,v)\leq D
. Taking supremum over
\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})
and applying (
5.35
) and (
5.38
), we deduce that

(5.54)

\displaystyle\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq D+\frac{1}{6}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)+\frac{33}{2}\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}|\phi_{0}|v_{0}^{2}\left(\left|\phi_{0}\right|+\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\right)\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq D+\frac{1}{2}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)+\frac{33}{2}\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}|\phi_{0}|v_{0}^{2}\left(\left|\phi_{0}\right|+12\right)\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq D+\frac{2}{3}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right),

which gives

(5.55)

\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)\leq 3D.

Note that in (
5.54
) we further restrict our choice of
v_{0}
as in (
5.46
) to

(5.56)

v_{0}=\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\sqrt{2}}{C_{1}\left(0,1;\frac{1}{2}\right)},\frac{2}{9\mathfrak{e}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{99\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}|\phi_{0}|\left(|\phi_{0}|+12\right)}}\right\}.

Furthermore, by (
5.38
), inequality (
5.55
) also leads to

(5.57)

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\phi}|\leq 3\left(\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{u},\tilde{v})}|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|\right)\leq 9D.

We thus retrieve our bootstrap estimate in (
5.35
) and deduce that
\mathcal{\tilde{D}}
is an open subset of
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
. Moreover, the derived estimates also extend to the entire region
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
.

We proceed to estimate
B
. In the following arguments, we take
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
and set
\tilde{u}=\tilde{v}=v_{0}
. By viewing (
5.40
) as an ODE in the
v
-direction for
\partial_{u}\left(r\tilde{\phi}\right)
and by applying (
2.13
), we deduce

(5.58)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{u}\left(r\tilde{\phi}\right)\right|(u,v)\leq

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{v}\left(r\tilde{\phi}\right)\right|(u,u)+\int^{v}_{u}|\tilde{\phi}|(-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}+\mathfrak{e}\int^{v}_{u}|A_{u}|\left|\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})\right|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{e}|\phi_{0}|\int^{v}_{u}|A_{u}|(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}+\mathfrak{e}\int^{v}_{u}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{|Q||\phi|}{r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Analogous to the previous case, since
\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r\leq 0
in
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
, we bound the first integral in (
5.58
) by

(5.59)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{u}|\tilde{\phi}|(-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq P\left((-\partial_{u}r)(u,v)-(\partial_{v}r)(u,u)\right)\leq\frac{P}{4}\leq 3D.

Furthermore, by (
5.51
), we get

(5.60)

\displaystyle|A_{u}|(u,v^{\prime})\leq 24\pi\mathfrak{e}(|\phi_{0}|+P)Av_{0}\leq 96\pi\mathfrak{e}(|\phi_{0}|+12)v_{0}D.

With these, we can see that the second and the third integral in (
5.58
) obey

(5.61)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}\int^{v}_{u}|A_{u}|\left|\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})\right|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 96\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}(|\phi_{0}|+12)v_{0}^{2}AD\leq 384\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}(|\phi_{0}|+12)v_{0}^{2}D\leq\frac{D}{4}

and

(5.62)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}|\phi_{0}|\int^{v}_{u}|A_{u}|(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 96\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}|\phi_{0}|(|\phi_{0}|+12)v_{0}^{2}D\leq\frac{D}{4},

if we refine our choice of
v_{0}
from (
5.56
) to

(5.63)

v_{0}=\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\sqrt{2}}{C_{1}\left(0,1;\frac{1}{2}\right)},\frac{2}{9\mathfrak{e}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{384\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}|\phi_{0}|\left(|\phi_{0}|+12\right)}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{1536\pi}\mathfrak{e}\sqrt{|\phi_{0}|+12}}\right\}.

To estimate the last integral in (
5.58
), by (
5.49
), we first note

(5.64)

|Q\phi|\leq 12\pi\mathfrak{e}\left(\left|\phi_{0}\right|+12\right)^{2}Av_{0}r(u,v)\leq 48\pi\mathfrak{e}\left(\left|\phi_{0}\right|+12\right)^{2}Dv_{0}r(u,v).

Henceforth, the last integral in (
5.58
) satisfies

(5.65)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}\int^{v}_{u}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{|Q||\phi|}{r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 48\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}\left(|\phi_{0}|+12\right)^{2}v_{0}D\int^{v}_{u}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 24\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}\left(|\phi_{0}|+12\right)^{2}v_{0}^{2}D\leq\frac{D}{2}

if we restrict our choice of
v_{0}
from (
5.63
) to

(5.66)

v_{0}=\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\sqrt{2}}{C_{1}\left(0,1;\frac{1}{2}\right)},\frac{2}{9\mathfrak{e}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{384\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}|\phi_{0}|\left(|\phi_{0}|+12\right)}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{1536\pi}\mathfrak{e}\sqrt{|\phi_{0}|+12}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{48\pi}\mathfrak{e}(|\phi_{0}|+12)}\right\}.

Plugging our bounds from (
5.59
), (
5.61
), (
5.65
) into (
5.58
), we deduce that

(5.67)

B\leq A+3D+\frac{D}{2}+\frac{D}{2}\leq 8D.

This then completes the proof of Lemma
5.3
.
∎

With the above lemma proven, we return to the proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.3
we can now write

(5.68)

A\lesssim D,\quad B\lesssim D,\quad P\lesssim D.

Furthermore, recall that in the proof of Lemma
5.3
, we chose
v_{0}
such that
\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r\leq 0
. Combined with the fact that
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})\subset\mathcal{R}
and
\partial_{v}r|_{\Gamma}=-\partial_{u}r|_{\Gamma}
, for all
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
, (
5.16
) then implies

(5.69)

\frac{5}{12}\leq\partial_{v}r(u,v)\leq\frac{1}{2},\quad\text{ and }\quad\frac{1}{2}\leq(-\partial_{u}r)(u,v)\leq\frac{2}{3}.

Henceforth, applying

(5.70)

r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}=\partial_{v}\left(r\tilde{\phi}\right)-\tilde{\phi}\partial_{v}r,\quad r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}=\partial_{u}\left(r\tilde{\phi}\right)-\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}r,\quad\phi=\phi_{0}+\tilde{\phi},

and
D\leq\varepsilon_{0}\leq 1
, we then get

(5.71)

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}\left|r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}\right|\lesssim D,\quad\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}\left|r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}\right|\lesssim D,\quad\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}\left|\phi\right|\lesssim 1.

Plugging these estimates into (
5.49
) and (
5.51
), for each
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
, we further have

(5.72)

|Q|(u,v)\lesssim Dr^{2}(u,v)\quad\text{ and }\quad|A_{u}|(u,v)\lesssim Dr(u,v).

Note that if needed, we can also use
r(u,v)\leq r(0,v_{0})=\frac{1}{2}v_{0}\leq 1
. As a consequence, we obtain

(5.73)

|rD_{u}\phi|(u,v)=|r\partial_{u}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}r\phi|(u,v)\lesssim D+Dr(u,v)^{2}\lesssim D.

On top of the estimates in the region
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
, we also derive some asymptotics (in
r
) for
C^{1}
solutions at points sufficiently close to
\Gamma
. This is to justify that various terms vanish as we take the limit along incoming or (backward) outgoing null curves towards points on
\Gamma
. For a
C^{1}
solution, we have that
\phi,r\partial_{v}\phi,\text{ and }r\partial_{u}\phi
are
C^{1}
functions on
\overline{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}
. Since
\partial_{v}\phi
and
\partial_{u}\phi
are continuous on
\overline{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}
, these imply that
r\partial_{v}\phi|_{\Gamma}=r\partial_{u}\phi|_{\Gamma}=0
.
By the fundamental theorem of calculus and the extreme value theorem, there exists a constant
M(v_{0})
depending on
v_{0}
such that

(5.74)

r|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v)\leq M(v_{0})(v-u)\quad\text{ and }\quad r|\partial_{u}\phi|\leq M(v_{0})(v-u).

Under the bootstrap assumption, similar to the proof in (
5.36
), we now have
v-u\leq 3r(u,v)
. Hence, we get

(5.75)

|\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}|(u,v)=|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v)\leq 3M(v_{0})\quad\text{ and }\quad|\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}|(u,v)=|\partial_{u}\phi|\leq 3M(v_{0}).

In addition, since
\partial_{v}r
and
\partial_{u}r
are also
C^{1}
functions, a similar argument allows us to deduce that there exists a constant
M_{1}(v_{0})
such that

(5.76)

|\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|(u,v)\leq M_{1}(v_{0})r(u,v)\quad\text{ and }\quad|\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}|(u,v)\leq M_{1}(v_{0})r(u,v).

Additionally, as
\phi
,
\partial_{v}r
, and
\partial_{u}r
are
C^{1}
functions on
\overline{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}
, these are bounded functions on
\overline{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})}
.
Furthermore, since
Q/r
is yet another
C^{1}
function, there exists a constant
M_{2}(v_{0})
such that

(5.77)

|Q|(u,v)\leq M_{2}(v_{0})r^{2}(u,v)\quad\text{ and }\quad|A_{u}|(u,v)\leq M_{2}(v_{0})r(u,v).

Using (
1.26
), together with (
5.69
) and
m|_{\Gamma}=0
, we derive

(5.78)

\displaystyle m(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{u}^{v}\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})+\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{4}}\cdot(r^{2}\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{24}{5}(3M(v_{0}))^{2}\pi r^{2}(v-u)+\frac{M_{2}(v_{0})^{2}}{2}r^{2}(u,v)(v-u)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{648}{5}\pi^{2}M^{2}(v_{0})+\frac{3M_{2}(v_{0})^{2}}{2}\right)r^{3}(u,v).

This implies

(5.79)

\mu(u,v)\leq\left(\frac{1296}{5}\pi^{2}M^{2}(v_{0})+3M_{2}(v_{0})^{2}\right)r^{2}(u,v).

We are back to derive estimates for
\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}
. Differentiating both sides of (
5.40
) with respect to
v
, we get

(5.80)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{v}\left(\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r\right)-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\phi_{0}(\partial_{v}A_{u})\partial_{v}r-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\phi_{0}A_{u}\partial_{v}\lambda-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right).

This can be rewritten as

(5.81)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\left(\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}\right)

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\left(\tilde{\phi}\partial_{v}\lambda\right)+G_{1}+G_{2}+\frac{\partial(\lambda,\tilde{\phi})}{\partial(u,v)}

with

(5.82)

\displaystyle G_{1}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\left(\phi_{0}A_{u}+\frac{\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right)\partial_{v}\lambda,

\displaystyle G_{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\left((\partial_{v}A_{u})\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}+\phi_{0}(\partial_{v}A_{u})\partial_{v}r+A_{u}\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}+\partial_{v}r\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right)\right).

For each
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
, we then have

(5.83)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}-\tilde{\phi}\partial_{v}\lambda\right|(u,v)\leq\left|\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}-\tilde{\phi}\partial_{v}\lambda\right|(0,v)+\int^{u}_{0}\left|G_{1}+G_{2}+\frac{\partial(\lambda,\tilde{\phi})}{\partial(u,v)}\right|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Using the fact
\lambda(0,v)=\frac{1}{2}
and integrating (
5.83
) with respect to
v
from
u
to
v_{0}
, we obtain

(5.84)

\displaystyle\int_{u}^{v_{0}}\left|\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}-\tilde{\phi}\partial_{v}\lambda\right|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\int^{v_{0}}_{u}\left|\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}\right|(0,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}+\int_{u}^{v_{0}}\int^{u}_{0}\left(|G_{1}|+|G_{2}|+\left|\frac{\partial(\lambda,\tilde{\phi})}{\partial(u,v)}\right|\right)(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

By taking the supremum over
u\in(0,v_{0})
in (
5.35
) and using (
5.9
), (
5.34
), we arrive at

(5.85)

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}]\leq

\displaystyle D+P\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\lambda]+A[\lambda,\tilde{\phi}]

\displaystyle+\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}\int_{u}^{v_{0}}\int^{u}_{0}|G_{1}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}+\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}\int_{u}^{v_{0}}\int^{u}_{0}|G_{2}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Next, we turn to estimate the integrals containing
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
in (
5.85
). Observe that, for each
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
, the following inequality holds uniformly in
(u,v)

(5.86)

\left|\phi_{0}A_{u}+\frac{\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right|(u,v)\lesssim Dr(u,v)\lesssim D.

This implies that for every
u\in(0,v_{0})
, we have

(5.87)

\displaystyle\int_{u}^{v_{0}}\int^{u}_{0}|G_{1}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim D\int_{0}^{u}\int^{v_{0}}_{u}|\partial_{v}\lambda|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\lesssim D\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\lambda],

where we employ the fact
u\leq v_{0}\leq 1
and apply Fubini’s theorem to interchange the order of integrations.

To estimate the integrand
G_{2}
, we start by writing

(5.88)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial_{v}\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}+\frac{\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{(\partial_{v}Q)\phi}{r}+\frac{\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\partial_{v}\phi}{r}-\frac{\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r^{2}}\partial_{v}r+\frac{\partial_{u}r}{(1-\mu)^{2}}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\partial_{v}\mu

and proceed to estimate each of these terms. For the first term, by (
1.19
), we have

(5.89)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial_{v}\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right|(u,v)

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{(\partial_{v}r)(\partial_{u}r)}{(1-\mu)^{2}}\frac{Q\phi}{r^{2}}\left(\mu-\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)\right|(u,v)\lesssim D,

where we have used (
5.34
), (
5.68
), (
5.71
), (
5.72
), (
5.69
) and the fact that
|\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}|=\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}\leq\mu\leq 1/3
. In the remaining parts of this section, we will not explicitly mention that these are used unless we really need. In a similar fashion, for the second term in (
5.88
), by (
1.18
), we obtain

(5.90)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{(\partial_{v}Q)\phi}{r}\right|(u,v)=\left|\frac{\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{\phi}{r}(-4\pi\mathfrak{e}r^{2}\im(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi))\right|(u,v)\lesssim D.

The third term in (
5.88
) can be estimated directly as

(5.91)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\partial_{v}\phi}{r}\right|(u,v)\lesssim D^{2}\lesssim D.

The fourth term in (
5.88
) obeys

(5.92)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r^{2}}\partial_{v}r\right|(u,v)\lesssim D.

For the fifth term in (
5.88
), we appeal to (
1.28
) and obtain

(5.93)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial_{u}r}{(1-\mu)^{2}}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\partial_{v}\mu\right|(u,v)

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\partial_{u}r}{(1-\mu)^{2}}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\left(\frac{4\pi r(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\left(\mu-\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)\right)\right|(u,v)\lesssim D^{3}+D\lesssim D.

Plugging (
5.89
), (
5.90
), (
5.91
), (
5.92
), (
5.93
) into (
5.88
), we thus deduce that

(5.94)

\left|\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right)\right|(u,v)\lesssim D.

For the other terms of
G_{2}
in (
5.82
), by (
1.31
) and
A\lesssim D
, we get

(5.95)

\left|(\partial_{v}A_{u})\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}\right|(u,v)=\left|\frac{2Q\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}\right|(u,v)\lesssim D^{2}\lesssim D.

Similarly, we deduce that

(5.96)

\left|\phi_{0}(\partial_{v}A_{u})\partial_{v}r\right|(u,v)=\left|\frac{2Q\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}\phi_{0}\partial_{v}r\right|(u,v)\lesssim D.

For the term
A_{u}\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}
, we first observe from (
5.60
) that

(5.97)

|A_{u}|(u,v)\leq 96v_{0}\mathfrak{e}(|\phi_{0}|+12)D.

By modifying our choice of
v_{0}
from (
5.66
) to

(5.98)

v_{0}=\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\sqrt{2}}{C_{1}\left(0,1;\frac{1}{2}\right)},\frac{2}{9\mathfrak{e}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{384\mathfrak{e}^{2}|\phi_{0}|\left(|\phi_{0}|+12\right)}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{1536}\mathfrak{e}\sqrt{|\phi_{0}|+12}},\frac{3}{128\mathfrak{e}(|\phi_{0}|+12)}\right\},

we obtain

(5.99)

|A_{u}|(u,v)\leq\frac{9}{4}D\leq\frac{9}{4},

which in turn implies that

(5.100)

\left|A_{u}\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}\right|(u,v)\leq\frac{9}{4}\left|\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}\right|(u,v).

Consequently, we prove

(5.101)

\displaystyle\int^{v_{0}}_{u}\int^{u}_{0}|A_{u}\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int^{u}_{0}\int^{v_{0}}_{u}|A_{u}\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{2\mathfrak{e}}\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}].

Combining (
5.94
), (
5.95
), (
5.96
), (
5.101
), together with (
5.82
), we thus arrive at

(5.102)

\int_{u}^{v_{0}}\int^{u}_{0}|G_{2}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}]\lesssim\int_{u}^{v_{0}}\int^{u}_{0}D\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\lesssim D.

Since the estimates in (
5.87
) and (
5.102
) are uniform in
u\in(0,v_{0})
, taking supremum over
u\in(0,v_{0})
and substituting them into (
5.85
), it yields

(5.103)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}]\lesssim

\displaystyle D\left(1+\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\lambda]\right)+A[\lambda,\tilde{\phi}].

Note that in (
5.103
), we are allowed to shift the term
\frac{1}{2}\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}]
in the inequality as it is a finite term for a
C^{1}
solution.

Next, we move to derive estimates for
\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}
. In view of the definition of
\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}
in (
5.7
), we define a renormalized quantity

(5.104)

\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}:=\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}r\phi=\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}r+r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}r\phi=\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}r+rD_{u}\phi.

By (
1.24
), taking derivative of it with respect to
v
, it yields

(5.105)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}.

Upon taking an additional derivative with respect to
u
, we arrive at

(5.106)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\left(\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r\right)+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\partial_{u}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right).

This can be rewritten as

(5.107)

\partial_{v}\partial_{u}\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}=\partial_{v}\left(\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}\nu\right)+G_{3}+G_{4}-\frac{\partial(\nu,\tilde{\phi})}{\partial(u,v)}

with

(5.108)

\displaystyle G_{3}=\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\partial_{u}\nu\left(\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right)\quad\text{ and }\quad G_{4}=\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\partial_{u}r\partial_{u}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right).

For each
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
, by integrating along the future-directed outgoing null curve originating from
\Gamma
, we obtain

(5.109)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{u}\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}-\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}\nu\right|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\partial_{u}\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}-\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}\nu\right|_{\Gamma}(u)+\int^{v}_{u}\left|G_{3}+G_{4}-\frac{\partial(\nu,\tilde{\phi})}{\partial(u,v)}\right|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime},

where we use (
2.8
), (
2.9
), (
2.13
).
Applying (
1.19
), together with
0\leq\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}\leq\mu
, (
5.75
), (
5.77
), (
5.79
), we derive

(5.110)

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}\nu|(u,v)=\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\frac{\lambda|\nu|}{r}\lesssim r(u,v).

Employing (
5.105
), we also get

(5.111)

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}|(u,v)\lesssim Dr(u,v)+Dr^{2}(u,v)\lesssim r(u,v).

Henceforth, we deduce that

(5.112)

\lim_{v\rightarrow u^{+}}\partial_{v}\nu(u,v)=\lim_{v\rightarrow u^{+}}\partial_{v}\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}(u,v)=0.

Since by (
2.12
) and (
2.13
), we also have

(5.113)

\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}|_{\Gamma}=\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}|_{\Gamma}=-\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{\Gamma}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\lambda|_{\Gamma}=-\nu|_{\Gamma}.

By the chain rule, we deduce

(5.114)

\lim_{v\rightarrow u^{+}}\partial_{u}\nu(u,v)=\lim_{v\rightarrow u^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu|_{\Gamma}(u)}{\mathrm{d}u}=-\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda|_{\Gamma}(u)}{\mathrm{d}u}

and

(5.115)

\lim_{v\rightarrow u^{+}}\partial_{u}\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}(u,v)=\lim_{v\rightarrow u^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}|_{\Gamma}(u)}{\mathrm{d}u}=-\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{\Gamma}(u)}{\mathrm{d}u}.

In addition, (
5.113
) also implies that

(5.116)

\displaystyle TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}|_{\Gamma}]=TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{\Gamma}],\quad TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\nu|_{\Gamma}]=TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\lambda|_{\Gamma}].

Hence, by integrating (
5.109
) along a future-directed incoming null curve originating from
C_{0}^{+}
and applying (
5.112
) - (
5.122
), we obtain

(5.117)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{0}\left|\partial_{u}\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}-\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}\nu\right|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq

\displaystyle TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}|_{\Gamma}]+P\cdot TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\nu|_{\Gamma}]+A[\nu,\tilde{\phi}]

\displaystyle+\int^{v}_{0}\int^{v}_{u^{\prime}}|G_{3}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\int^{v}_{0}\int^{v}_{u^{\prime}}|G_{4}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

We proceed to estimate the integrals containing
G_{3}
and
G_{4}
in (
5.117
). For the former, for each
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})
, we have

(5.118)

|G_{3}|(u,v)\lesssim Dr(u,v)|\partial_{u}\nu|(u,v)\lesssim D|\partial_{u}\nu|(u,v)

and hence

(5.119)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{0}\int^{v}_{u^{\prime}}|G_{3}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int^{v}_{0}\int^{v^{\prime}}_{0}|G_{3}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\lesssim D\cdot\sup_{v\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{(0,v)\times\{v\}}[\nu].

For the integrand
G_{4}
, similar to equation (
5.88
), we now have

(5.120)

\partial_{u}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right)=\frac{\partial_{v}\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}+\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{(\partial_{u}Q)\phi}{r}+\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\partial_{u}\phi}{r}-\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r^{2}}\partial_{u}r+\frac{\partial_{v}r}{(1-\mu)^{2}}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\partial_{u}\mu.

Notice that each of the terms above is analogous to the corresponding ones in (
5.88
). Their estimates are exactly symmetric (with the use of (
1.17
) and (
1.27
) instead of (
1.18
) and (
1.28
) for the second and the fifth terms). Hence, by utilizing (
5.73
), we then deduce that

(5.121)

\left|\partial_{u}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right)\right|(u,v)\lesssim D,

which implies that

(5.122)

\int^{v}_{0}\int^{v}_{u^{\prime}}|G_{4}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\lesssim D.

Plugging (
5.119
) and (
5.122
) into (
5.117
), we hence derive

(5.123)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{0}\left|\partial_{u}\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}-\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}\nu\right|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\lesssim

\displaystyle TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{\Gamma}]+P\cdot TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\lambda|_{\Gamma}]+A[\nu,\tilde{\phi}]+D\cdot\sup_{v\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{(0,v)\times\{v\}}[\nu]+D.

This implies that

(5.124)

\int^{v}_{0}\left|\partial_{u}\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}\right|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\lesssim TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{\Gamma}]+P\cdot TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\lambda|_{\Gamma}]+A[\nu,\tilde{\phi}]+D\cdot\sup_{v\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{(0,v)\times\{v\}}[\nu]+D.

Taking supremum over
v\in(0,v_{0})
, we then conclude that

(5.125)

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{(0,v)\times\{v\}}[\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}]\lesssim

\displaystyle TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{\Gamma}]+D\left(1+TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\lambda|_{\Gamma}]+\sup_{v\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{(0,v)\times\{v\}}[\nu]\right)+A[\nu,\tilde{\phi}].

By scrutinizing the terms in the upper bound in (
5.125
), it necessitates estimating
TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{\Gamma}]
,
TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\lambda|_{\Gamma}]
and
\sup_{v\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{(0,v)\times\{v\}}[\nu]
. We now estimate each of them. Starting from
TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{\Gamma}]
, for a fixed
u\in(0,v_{0})
, we integrate (
5.81
) along the future-directed incoming null curve
v=u
starting from
C_{0}^{+}
to
(u,u)\in\Gamma
and obtain

(5.126)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{\Gamma}(u)=

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}|_{\Gamma}(u)\partial_{v}\lambda|_{\Gamma}(u)+(\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}})(0,u)-\left(\tilde{\phi}\partial_{v}\lambda\right)(0,u)

\displaystyle+\int^{u}_{0}G_{1}(u^{\prime},u)+G_{2}(u^{\prime},u)+\frac{\partial(\lambda,\tilde{\phi})}{\partial(u,v)}(u^{\prime},u)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Using similar estimates as in (
5.110
) and (
5.111
), it is not difficult to see that we have

(5.127)

\lim_{v\rightarrow u^{+}}\partial_{u}\lambda(u,v)=\lim_{v\rightarrow u^{+}}\partial_{u}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}(u,v)=0.

Together with the fact that
\lambda(0,v)=\frac{1}{2}
, this implies that

(5.128)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{\Gamma}(u)=

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}|_{\Gamma}(u)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}\lambda|_{\Gamma}(u)+(\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}})(0,u)+\int^{u}_{0}G_{1}(u^{\prime},u)+G_{2}(u^{\prime},u)+\frac{\partial(\lambda,\tilde{\phi})}{\partial(u,v)}(u^{\prime},u)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Integrating (
5.128
) with variable
u\in(0,v_{0})
, we deduce

(5.129)

\displaystyle TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{\Gamma}]

\displaystyle\leq P\cdot TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\lambda|_{\Gamma}]+D+A[\lambda,\tilde{\phi}]+\int_{0}^{v_{0}}\int^{v}_{0}|G_{1}|(u^{\prime},v)+|G_{2}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=P\cdot TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\lambda|_{\Gamma}]+D+A[\lambda,\tilde{\phi}]+\int_{0}^{v_{0}}\int^{v_{0}}_{u^{\prime}}|G_{1}|(u^{\prime},v)+|G_{2}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

For the integral with
|G_{1}|
, analogous to (
5.87
), we deduce

(5.130)

\displaystyle\int^{v_{0}}_{0}\int^{v_{0}}_{u^{\prime}}|G_{1}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim D\int^{v_{0}}_{0}\int^{v_{0}}_{u^{\prime}}|\partial_{v}\lambda|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\lesssim D\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\lambda].

To estimate the integral with
|G_{2}|
, in a similar fashion as for (
5.101
), we obtain

(5.131)

\displaystyle\int^{v_{0}}_{0}\int^{v_{0}}_{u^{\prime}}|A_{u}\partial_{v}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{2\mathfrak{e}}\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}].

Consequently, by utilizing identical pointwise estimates (
5.94
), (
5.95
), (
5.96
), we derive the below estimate analogous to (
5.102
)

(5.132)

\int^{v_{0}}_{0}\int^{v_{0}}_{u^{\prime}}|G_{2}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}]\lesssim D.

Applying (
5.130
) and (
5.132
) to (
5.129
), we then conclude that

(5.133)

TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}|_{\Gamma}]\lesssim D\left(1+TV_{(0,v_{0})}[\lambda|_{\Gamma}]+\sup_{u\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\lambda]\right)+A[\lambda,\tilde{\phi}].

Observe that the upper bound in (
5.133
) involves the term
\sup_{v\in(0,v_{0})}TV_{\{u\}\times(u,v_{0})}[\lambda]
. We now move to bound it. Recall that from (
1.19
) we have

(5.134)

\partial_{u}\log\lambda=\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}.

Upon taking an additional derivative with respect to
v
and using (
1.19
), (
1.28
), (
1.29
), we arrive at

(5.135)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\partial_{u}\log\lambda=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{v}\left(\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}\right)}{1-\mu}\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}+(\partial_{v}\mu)\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{(1-\mu)^{2}}\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}+\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\frac{\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r}{r}-\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\frac{(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{v}\mu}{1-\mu}\left(1+\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)-\frac{\partial_{v}\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)}{1-\mu}\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}+\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}-1\right)\frac{(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4\pi r|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{1-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)+\left(\frac{8\pi\mathfrak{e}Q\im(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)+\frac{2Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{r^{3}}}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}-1\right)\frac{(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4\pi r|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\left(\frac{1-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)+\left(\frac{8\pi\mathfrak{e}Q\im(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)+\frac{2Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{r^{3}}}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)

\displaystyle-2\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}}\right).

We can also rewrite (
1.19
) as

(5.136)

\partial_{v}\log(-\nu)=\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}.

By an analogous computation, with the help of (
1.27
) and (
1.30
), we obtain

(5.137)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\log(-\nu)=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4\pi r|D_{u}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{u}r}\right)\left(\frac{1-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\right)+\left(\frac{-8\pi\mathfrak{e}Q\im(\phi^{\dagger}D_{u}\phi)+\frac{2Q^{2}\partial_{u}r}{r^{3}}}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\right)

\displaystyle-2\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}}\right).

Observe that (
5.135
) and (
5.137
) together imply

(5.138)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\partial_{u}\log\lambda-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\log(-\nu)=

\displaystyle\frac{4\pi}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{1-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{(r|\partial_{v}\phi|)^{2}(\partial_{u}r)}{(\partial_{v}r)}-\frac{(r|D_{u}\phi|)^{2}(\partial_{v}r)}{\partial_{u}r}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{8\pi\mathfrak{e}Q}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left((\partial_{u}r)\im(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)+(\partial_{v}r)\im(\phi^{\dagger}D_{u}\phi)\right).

We then exploit the symmetry in (
5.138
). Notice that
\partial_{v}\partial_{u}\log\lambda
and
\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\log(-\nu)
have alternative expressions given below

(5.139)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\partial_{u}\log\lambda=

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\right)+\frac{4\pi}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{1-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{(r|\partial_{v}\phi|)^{2}(\partial_{u}r)}{(\partial_{v}r)}-\frac{(r|D_{u}\phi|)^{2}(\partial_{v}r)}{\partial_{u}r}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{8\pi\mathfrak{e}Q}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left((\partial_{u}r)\im(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)+(\partial_{v}r)\im(\phi^{\dagger}D_{u}\phi)\right)

and

(5.140)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\log(-\nu)=

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)-\frac{4\pi}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{1-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{(r|\partial_{v}\phi|)^{2}(\partial_{u}r)}{(\partial_{v}r)}-\frac{(r|D_{u}\phi|)^{2}(\partial_{v}r)}{\partial_{u}r}\right)

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{8\pi\mathfrak{e}Q}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left((\partial_{u}r)\im(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)+(\partial_{v}r)\im(\phi^{\dagger}D_{u}\phi)\right).

Next, we define

(5.141)

\tilde{f}(u,v):=4\pi\re\left(\frac{r(\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi})^{\dagger}}{r}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}}{\lambda}-\frac{\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}}{\nu}\right)\right)(u,v)

and

(5.142)

\tilde{f}^{*}(u,v):=4\pi\re\left(\frac{r(\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi})^{\dagger}}{r}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}}{\lambda}-\frac{\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}}{\nu}\right)\right)(u,v).

To compute the partial derivative of
f
with respect to
u
, we first observe from (
1.23
) that

(5.143)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}(r\partial_{v}\phi)=-(\partial_{v}r)(\partial_{u}\phi)+E_{1}\quad\text{ and }\quad\partial_{v}(r\partial_{u}\phi)=-(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}\phi)+E_{1}

with

(5.144)

E_{1}:=-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}(r\partial_{v}\phi+\phi\partial_{v}r)-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi}{1-\mu}\frac{(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r}

and
|E_{1}|(u,v)\lesssim Dr(u,v)
. In addition, we observe that

(5.145)

\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}}{\lambda}-\frac{\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}}{\nu}=\frac{r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{u}r}+E_{2}\quad\text{ with }\quad E_{2}:=-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{A_{u}r\phi}{\partial_{u}r}\right)

and
|E_{2}|(u,v)\lesssim Dr^{2}(u,v)
. We then proceed to take the partial derivative with respect to
u
on both sides of (
5.141
). Together with (
5.143
) and (
5.145
), we obtain

(5.146)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\tilde{f}}{\partial u}=

\displaystyle 4\pi\re\left(-\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}}{r}+\frac{\partial_{v}r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}}{r}-\frac{E_{1}}{r}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{u}r}+E_{2}\right)+\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}}{r}\right)^{\dagger}\partial_{u}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}}{\lambda}-\frac{\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}}{\nu}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4\pi}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}r}{\partial_{u}r}(r|\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}|)^{2}-\frac{\partial_{u}r}{\partial_{v}r}(r|\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}|)^{2}\right)+4\pi\re\left(\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}}{r}\right)^{\dagger}\partial_{u}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}}{\lambda}-\frac{\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}}{\nu}\right)\right)-4\pi\re\left((\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi})^{\dagger}(\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi})-(\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi})(\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi})^{\dagger}\right)

\displaystyle+4\pi\re\left(\left(\frac{E_{1}^{\dagger}}{r}\right)\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{u}r}\right)\right)-4\pi\re\left(\left(\frac{E_{2}}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\partial_{u}r(r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi})+\partial_{v}r(r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi})\right)\right)+4\pi\re\left(\frac{E_{1}^{\dagger}E_{2}}{r}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4\pi}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{(r|\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}|)^{2}(\partial_{v}r)}{\partial_{u}r}-\frac{(r|\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}|)^{2}(\partial_{u}r)}{\partial_{v}r}\right)+4\pi\re\left(\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}}{r}\right)^{\dagger}\partial_{u}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}}{\lambda}-\frac{\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}}{\nu}\right)\right)+E_{3}

with

(5.147)

E_{3}:=4\pi\re\left(\left(\frac{E_{1}^{\dagger}}{r}\right)\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{u}r}\right)\right)-4\pi\re\left(\left(\frac{E_{2}}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\partial_{u}r(r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi})+\partial_{v}r(r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi})\right)\right)+4\pi\re\left(\frac{E_{1}^{\dagger}E_{2}}{r}\right).

Note that we use the fact that
(\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi})^{\dagger}(\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi})-(\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi})(\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi})^{\dagger}
is purely imaginary. And we have
|E_{3}|(u,v)\lesssim D^{2}
. Similarly, we compute the partial derivative of
\tilde{f}^{*}
with respect to
v
and get

(5.148)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\tilde{f}^{*}}{\partial v}=

\displaystyle 4\pi\re\left(-\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}}{r}+\frac{\partial_{v}r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}}{r}-\frac{E_{1}}{r}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{u}r}+E_{2}\right)+\left(\frac{r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}}{r}\right)^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}}{\lambda}-\frac{\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}}{\nu}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4\pi}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{(r|\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}|)^{2}(\partial_{v}r)}{\partial_{u}r}-\frac{(r|\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}|)^{2}(\partial_{u}r)}{\partial_{v}r}\right)+4\pi\re\left(\left(\frac{r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}}{r}\right)^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}}{\lambda}-\frac{\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}}{\nu}\right)\right)+E_{3}.

The equations for
\frac{\partial\tilde{f}}{\partial u}
and
\frac{\partial\tilde{f}^{*}}{\partial v}
will be used in the estimates for
\partial_{u}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}}{\lambda}-\frac{\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}}{\nu}\right)
and
\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}}{\lambda}-\frac{\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}}{\nu}\right)
. To evaluate them, we start by considering (
5.143
) and rewrite it as

(5.149)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\partial_{v}(r\tilde{\phi})=\partial_{v}\partial_{u}(r\tilde{\phi})=\tilde{\phi}\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r+E_{1}.

By the definition of
\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}
from (
5.5
) and employing (
1.19
), the above equation implies that

(5.150)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}}{\lambda}\right)=\frac{1}{\partial_{v}r}\left(E_{1}-\frac{r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{v}r}\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r\right)=-\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)+\frac{E_{1}}{\partial_{v}r}.

Similarly, we also obtain

(5.151)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}}{\nu}\right)=-\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\left(\frac{r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{u}r}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\right)+\frac{E_{1}}{\partial_{u}r}.

By using the definition of
\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}
in (
5.104
), the above further simplifies to

(5.152)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}}{\nu}\right)=-\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\left(\frac{r\partial_{u}\tilde{\phi}}{\partial_{u}r}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\right)+\frac{E_{4}}{\partial_{u}r}

with

(5.153)

E_{4}:=E_{1}+(\partial_{u}r)\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}r\phi}{\partial_{u}r}\right).

Observe that by applying (
1.19
) and (
1.31
), we get

(5.154)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{A_{u}r\phi}{\partial_{u}r}\right)

\displaystyle=-A_{u}r\phi\left(\frac{\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r}{(\partial_{u}r)^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{\partial_{u}r}\left((\partial_{v}A_{u})(r\phi)+A_{u}\phi(\partial_{v}r)+A_{u}r(\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi})\right)

\displaystyle=-A_{u}\phi\left(\frac{(\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2})(\partial_{v}r)}{(1-\mu)(\partial_{u}r)}\right)+\frac{1}{\partial_{u}r}\left(\left(\frac{2Q(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}\right)(r\phi)+A_{u}\phi(\partial_{v}r)+A_{u}r(\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi})\right).

Using the fact that
|E_{1}|(u,v)\lesssim Dr(u,v)
, we derive the below estimate

(5.155)

\displaystyle|E_{4}|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\left|E_{1}\right|(u,v)+\left|(\partial_{u}r)\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}r\phi}{\partial_{u}r}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim Dr(u,v)+Dr(u,v)+Dr(u,v)+Dr(u,v)+Dr^{2}(u,v)\lesssim Dr(u,v).

At the same time, by the expressions in (
5.150
) and (
5.152
), we compute the following terms

(5.156)

\displaystyle(\partial_{v}\tilde{\phi})^{\dagger}\partial_{u}\left(\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}}{\lambda}-\frac{\tilde{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.85pt]{$\tilde{\mathfrak{b}}$}}}{\nu}\right)=
