\displaystyle=-\frac{\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r}{\partial_{v}r}\alpha^{\prime}+\frac{1}{\partial_{v}r}\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}\right).

Observe that by (
1.19
) we have

(6.15)

-\frac{\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r}{\partial_{v}r}\alpha^{\prime}=-\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\alpha^{\prime}.

Employing (
1.15
) and (
1.19
), we deduce that

(6.16)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}\right)=\partial_{u}\left(\phi+\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\phi+\frac{\partial_{v}r\partial_{u}(r\partial_{v}\phi)-(r\partial_{v}\phi)\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial_{u}\phi\partial_{v}r+\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi+r\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r}{\partial_{v}r}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\Psi(A)}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{\partial_{v}\phi\partial_{u}r}{\partial_{v}r}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)

and this implies

(6.17)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\partial_{v}r}\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\partial_{v}r}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\Psi(A)}{\partial_{v}r}\right)-\frac{1}{\partial_{v}r}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}\phi\partial_{u}r}{\partial_{v}r}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\right).

Plugging (
6.15
) and (
6.17
) into (
6.14
), we thus obtain

(6.18)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\alpha^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\alpha^{\prime}-\frac{1}{\partial_{v}r}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\Psi(A)}{\partial_{v}r}\right)-\frac{1}{\partial_{v}r}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}\phi\partial_{u}r}{\partial_{v}r}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=:J_{1}(u,v)+J_{2}(u,v)+J_{3}(u,v).

For
J_{3}
, we compute the derivative and get

(6.19)

\displaystyle J_{3}(u,v)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\partial_{v}r}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}\phi\partial_{u}r}{\partial_{v}r}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\partial_{v}\phi\partial_{u}r}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)-\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{(1-\mu)(\partial_{v}r)}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right).

For the term
-\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)
, we have the expression

(6.20)

\displaystyle-\partial_{v}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)=\;\partial_{v}\left(1+\frac{Q^{2}/r^{2}-1}{1-\mu}\right)=\;\frac{(1-\mu)\partial_{v}(Q^{2}/r^{2})+(Q^{2}/r^{2}-1)\partial_{v}\mu}{(1-\mu)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{8\pi\mathfrak{e}Q\im(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)}{1-\mu}-\frac{2\partial_{v}rQ^{2}}{r^{3}(1-\mu)}+\frac{4\pi r|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}(Q^{2}/r^{2}-1)}{\partial_{v}r(1-\mu)}-\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\frac{\left(\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}\right)(Q^{2}/r^{2}-1)}{(1-\mu)^{2}}.

In the last equality above, we have applied (
1.28
) and (
1.29
).

For the term
\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)
, with the help of (
6.1
) and (
1.19
), we obtain

(6.21)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}-\phi\right)+\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r}\alpha^{\prime}-\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r}+\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\left(\frac{r\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r-\partial_{v}r\partial_{u}r}{r^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r}\alpha^{\prime}-\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r}+\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}-1\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r}\alpha^{\prime}-\frac{2\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r}+\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right).

Substituting both (
6.20
) and (
6.21
) into (
6.19
),
J_{3}
can be expanded as

(6.22)

\displaystyle J_{3}(u,v)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial_{v}\phi\partial_{u}r}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)-\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{(1-\mu)(\partial_{v}r)}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{8\pi\mathfrak{e}Q(\im(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi))(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}r)}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}(1-\mu)}-\frac{2Q^{2}(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}r)}{r^{3}(\partial_{v}r)(1-\mu)}+\frac{4\pi r(Q^{2}/r^{2}-1)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}r)}{(\partial_{v}r)^{3}(1-\mu)}

\displaystyle-\frac{(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}r)}{r(\partial_{v}r)}\frac{\left(\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}\right)(Q^{2}/r^{2}-1)}{(1-\mu)^{2}}-\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\alpha^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r(\partial_{v}r)}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)-\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r\partial_{v}\phi}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{7}J_{3,i}(u.v).

Observing that
J_{3,5}\equiv J_{1}
and
J_{3,2}+J_{3,4}+J_{3,6}+J_{3,7}
forms a simpler expression, we can rewrite
J_{1}+J_{3}
as

(6.23)

\displaystyle J_{1}+J_{3}=

\displaystyle J_{3,1}+J_{3,3}+2J_{3,5}+(J_{3,2}+J_{3,4}+J_{3,6}+J_{3,7})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{8\pi\mathfrak{e}Q(\im(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi))(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}r)}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}(1-\mu)}+\frac{4\pi r(Q^{2}/r^{2}-1)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}r)}{(\partial_{v}r)^{3}(1-\mu)}-\frac{2\partial_{u}r}{r}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\alpha^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r\partial_{v}r}\left(\frac{5Q^{2}/r^{2}-3\mu}{1-\mu}\right).

With the help of (
6.1
), (
1.10
), (
1.16
), we express the terms in
J_{2}
as follows:

(6.24)

\displaystyle J_{2}(u,v)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}}{\partial_{v}r}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{A_{u}\partial_{v}(r\phi)-\frac{\Omega^{2}Q\phi}{4r}}{\partial_{v}r}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}}{\partial_{v}r}\left(\partial_{v}A_{u}\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}+A_{u}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}\right)-\partial_{v}\left(\frac{Q\phi(-\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\left(A_{u}\alpha^{\prime}+\partial_{v}A_{u}\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}-\frac{1}{\partial_{v}r}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right)\right).

Together with (
1.18
), (
1.21
), (
1.31
), we arrive at

(6.25)

\displaystyle\frac{J_{2}(u,v)}{-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}}=

\displaystyle A_{u}\alpha^{\prime}+\left(\frac{2Q\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}\right)\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi+\phi\partial_{v}r}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}-\frac{1}{\partial_{v}r}\left(\frac{Q\phi}{r}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\right)+\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{Q\phi}{r}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{u}\alpha^{\prime}+\frac{2Q\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r(1-\mu)(\partial_{v}r)}+\frac{2Q\phi\partial_{u}r}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}+\frac{1}{\partial_{v}r}\frac{Q\phi}{r}\left(\frac{1-\mu}{-\partial_{u}r}\right)^{-2}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{1-\mu}{-\partial_{u}r}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\partial_{u}r}{\partial_{v}r(1-\mu)}\left(\frac{\phi\partial_{v}Q}{r}+\frac{Q\partial_{v}\phi}{r}-\frac{Q\phi}{r^{2}}\partial_{v}r\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{u}\alpha^{\prime}+\frac{2Q\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r(1-\mu)(\partial_{v}r)}+\frac{2Q\phi\partial_{u}r}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}+\frac{4\pi|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}Q\phi\partial_{u}r}{(1-\mu)(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{4\pi\mathfrak{e}\phi\im(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)r\partial_{u}r}{\partial_{v}r(1-\mu)}+\frac{Q\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r(1-\mu)(\partial_{v}r)}-\frac{Q\phi\partial_{u}r}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{u}\alpha^{\prime}+\frac{3Q\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r(1-\mu)(\partial_{v}r)}+\frac{Q\phi\partial_{u}r}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}+\frac{4\pi|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}Q\phi\partial_{u}r}{(1-\mu)(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}-\frac{4\pi\mathfrak{e}\phi\im(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)r\partial_{u}r}{\partial_{v}r(1-\mu)}.

Combining (
6.23
) and (
6.25
), we hence obtain

(6.26)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\alpha^{\prime}=

\displaystyle J_{1}+J_{3}+J_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{8\pi\mathfrak{e}Q(\im(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi))(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}r)}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}(1-\mu)}+\frac{4\pi r(Q^{2}/r^{2}-1)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}r)}{(\partial_{v}r)^{3}(1-\mu)}

\displaystyle-\frac{2\partial_{u}r}{r}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\alpha^{\prime}+\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r\partial_{v}r}\left(\frac{5Q^{2}/r^{2}-3\mu}{1-\mu}\right)

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\left(A_{u}\alpha^{\prime}+\frac{3Q\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi}{r(1-\mu)(\partial_{v}r)}+\frac{Q\phi\partial_{u}r}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}+\frac{4\pi|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}Q\phi\partial_{u}r}{(1-\mu)(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}-\frac{4\pi\mathfrak{e}\phi\im(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)r\partial_{u}r}{\partial_{v}r(1-\mu)}\right).

Treating
\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}
as a purely imaginary integrating factor, we thus arrive at (
6.4
).

We now proceed to obtain estimates for
|Q|,|Q\phi|,
and
|\phi|^{2}
. We first prove (
6.13
) under the assumption
\mu\leq\varepsilon<1
for all
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})\setminus\mathcal{D}(u_{1},v_{*})
.

For
|Q|
, from (
3.7
) of Proposition
3.3
, by setting
\overline{u}=0
and recalling the definitions of
\bar{B},\overline{\mu_{*}},\overline{P}
in (
3.2
) and (
3.5
), we have that the
C_{1}(0,v;\chi)
in (
3.20
) obeys the following upper bound

(6.27)

\displaystyle C_{1}(0,v;\chi)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\pi\mathfrak{e}\left(\sup_{v^{\prime}\in[0,v*]}\partial_{v}r(0,v^{\prime})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(1-\sup_{v^{\prime}\in[0,v_{*}]}\mu(0,v^{\prime}))^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\frac{\overline{P}(0,v_{*})}{\sqrt{4\pi}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{16\pi^{2}\chi\mathfrak{e}^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\pi\mathfrak{e}}{1-\mu^{*}}\left(\frac{\overline{P}(0,v_{*})}{\sqrt{4\pi}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{16\pi^{2}\chi\mathfrak{e}^{2}}}\right)<+\infty.

Here we use (
2.6
) and (
2.15
) to bound
\sup_{v^{\prime}\in[0,v_{*}]}\partial_{v}r(0,v^{\prime})
and
\sup_{v^{\prime}\in[0,v_{*}]}\mu(0,v^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}
respectively. We also appeal to the fact that
\overline{P}(0,v_{*})=\sup_{v^{\prime}\in[0,v_{*}]}|\phi|<+\infty
since
\phi\in C^{1}
along
C_{0}^{+}
and
\phi|_{\Gamma}<+\infty
from (
2.10
). Plugging these back into (
3.7
), we thus obtain

(6.28)

\displaystyle|Q|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\pi\mathfrak{e}}{1-\mu^{*}}\left(\frac{\overline{P}(0,v_{*})}{\sqrt{4\pi}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{16\pi^{2}\chi\mathfrak{e}^{2}}}\right)r^{\frac{\chi}{2}}(0,v_{*})v_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}(u,v)=C_{6}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}(u,v)

with
C_{6}
defined in (
6.10
). Note that the constant
C_{6}
defined above depends only on
\chi
and
v_{*}
. In addition, for brevity, since
\chi
and
v_{*}
were fixed from the beginning, we can then drop the dependency of
C_{6}
and subsequent constants on
v_{*}
and
\chi
, if there is no danger of confusion.

For
|Q\phi|
, following a similar strategy as in the proof of Proposition
3.3
but with the assumption that
\mu
is small, we can obtain a better estimate as follows. Since
Q|_{\Gamma}=0
and
|\phi||_{\Gamma}<+\infty
, by (
1.18
), we have

(6.29)

\displaystyle Q\phi(u,v)

\displaystyle=\int^{v}_{u}(\partial_{v}Q)\phi+Q\partial_{v}\phi(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime},

and

|Q\phi|(u,v)\leq\int^{v}_{u}(4\pi\mathfrak{e}r^{2}|\phi|^{2}+|Q|)|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

With the help of (
1.26
), this implies that

(6.30)

\displaystyle|Q\phi|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int^{v}_{u}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)}(4\pi\mathfrak{e}r^{2}|\phi|^{2}+|Q|)^{2}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int^{v}_{u}\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int^{v}_{u}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)}(16\pi^{2}\mathfrak{e}^{2}r^{4}|\phi|^{4}+|Q|^{2})(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mu(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}r(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(16\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}\int^{v}_{u}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{(1-\mu)}r^{2}|\phi|^{4}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}+\frac{1}{\pi}\int^{v}_{u}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}|Q|^{2}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}r(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Our strategy to estimate the first integral remains unchanged as in Proposition
3.3
. Hence, by setting
\overline{u}=0
,
\xi_{1}=1
and
\xi_{2}=\chi
(this is valid since we did not impose
\xi_{2}\in(0,1-\chi)
for this estimate) in (
3.32
), we derive the below bound

(6.31)

16\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}\int^{v}_{u}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{(1-\mu)}r^{2}|\phi|^{4}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq 16\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}C_{3}(0,v_{*};\chi,1,\chi)r^{2-\chi}(u,v).

On the other hand, the second integral can be estimated with the help of (
6.28
) as follows

(6.32)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{u}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}|Q|^{2}r^{-2}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\int^{v}_{u}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\varepsilon}C_{6}^{2}\varepsilon r^{1-\chi}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=\frac{C_{6}^{2}\varepsilon}{(2-\chi)(1-\varepsilon)}r^{2-\chi}(u,v).

Summarizing (
6.31
) and (
6.32
), we obtain

(6.33)

\displaystyle|Q\phi|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq C_{7}(v_{*})\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(1-\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}}r^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}(u,v)

with
C_{7}
defined in (
6.11
).

For
|\phi|^{2}
, analogous to the proof of Proposition
3.3
and (
6.29
), we instead consider
\partial_{v}(r\phi)
in an attempt to obtain pointwise estimates for
|\phi|^{2}
. With
r\phi|_{\Gamma}=0
, we have

(6.34)

|r\phi|(u,v)\leq\int^{v}_{u}r|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}+\int^{v}_{u}|\phi|\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

The first term in (
6.34
) can be estimated via

(6.35)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{u}r|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\int^{v}_{u}\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int^{v}_{u}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\pi}}\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(1-\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}}r(u,v).

For the second term in (
6.34
), by Proposition
3.3
, Lemma
3.2
and inequality (
3.6
), we can exploit its structure and obtain

(6.36)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{u}|\phi|\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{u}(\overline{P}(0,v_{*}))\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}+\int^{v}_{u}\frac{1}{(4\pi)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\log\left(\frac{\frac{1-\mu}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})}{\frac{1-\mu}{\partial_{v}r}(0,v^{\prime})}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\log\left(\frac{r(0,v^{\prime})}{r(u,v^{\prime})}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\overline{P}(0,v_{*})r(u,v)+\frac{1}{(4\pi e)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\frac{\bar{B}(0,v_{*})}{(1-\overline{\mu_{*}})(0,v_{*})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\int^{v}_{u}(\partial_{v}r)^{\frac{1}{2}}(u,v^{\prime})(1-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\left(\log\left(\frac{r(0,v^{\prime})}{r(u,v^{\prime})}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\overline{P}(0,v_{*})r(u,v)+\frac{v_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(4\pi e)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\frac{\bar{B}(0,v_{*})}{(1-\overline{\mu_{*}})(0,v_{*})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int^{v}_{u}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\log\left(\frac{r(0,v^{\prime})}{r(u,v^{\prime})}\right)\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\overline{P}(0,v_{*})r(u,v)+\frac{v_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}}r(0,v_{*})^{\frac{\iota}{2}}}{(4\pi e\iota(1-\iota))^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\frac{\bar{B}(0,v_{*})}{(1-\overline{\mu_{*}})(0,v_{*})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}r(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\iota}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{8}(\iota)r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\iota}{2}}.

In (
6.36
) above, the constant
C_{8}
is defined in (
6.12
) and depends on a parameter
\iota\in(0,1)
.
Combining the two estimates above and using the fact that
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2(a^{2}+b^{2})
for all
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, we derive

r^{2}|\phi|^{2}(u,v)\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}r^{2}(u,v)+2C_{8}^{2}(\iota)r^{1-\iota}(u,v),

and hence

(6.37)

\displaystyle|\phi|^{2}(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}+2C_{8}^{2}(\iota)r^{-1-\iota}(u,v).

We continue to estimate these terms under the assumption that
\gamma(u,v)\leq G
and
\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}\leq H
uniformly for
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})\setminus\mathcal{D}(u_{1},v_{*})
.

For
|Q|
, using a similar argument as above with a minor exception of not estimating
\mu(u,v)
by
\varepsilon
, analogous to (
6.28
), we obtain the estimate for
|Q|
in (
6.6
) with
C_{6}^{\prime}(v_{*};\chi)
defined in (
6.7
).

For
|Q\phi|
, by our assumption
\frac{1}{1-\mu}\leq H
, we have the estimate for
|Q\phi|
in (
6.6
) with
C_{7}^{\prime}(v_{*};\chi)
and
C_{7}^{\prime\prime}(v_{*};\chi)
defined in (
6.8
).

For
|\phi|^{2}
, employing a similar argument, by (
6.37
),
\mu<1
, and (
6.57
), we deduce the estimate for
|\phi|
in (
6.6
) and (
6.9
) with
C_{8}
defined in (
6.12
) and
\iota
chosen to be
\frac{1}{2}
.

∎

With Lemma
6.3
established, we now start to prove Theorem
6.1
.

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.
By assumption, there is a positive constant
G
such that

(6.38)

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})}\gamma(u,v)\leq G.

For each
u\in[0,v_{*})
, we define the quantity

(6.39)

B^{\prime}(u):=\sup_{v\in[u,v_{*}]}|\alpha^{\prime}|(u,v).

We will attempt to show that this quantity remains bounded uniformly in
u
, which in turn implies that the constant

(6.40)

B^{\prime}:=\sup_{u\in[0,v_{*})}\sup_{v\in[u,v_{*}]}|\alpha^{\prime}|(u,v)=\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})}|\alpha^{\prime}|<+\infty

and the solution extends as a
C^{1}
solution up to
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})
.

Estimates for
B^{\prime}(u)
.
To do so, we proceed as follows. By (
6.4
) of Lemma
6.3
, we have

(6.41)

\alpha^{\prime}(u,v)e^{\int_{0}^{u}\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}=\alpha^{\prime}(0,v)+\sum_{i=1}^{11}I_{i}(u,v)

with
K_{i}
defined in (
6.5
) and
I_{i}:=\int_{0}^{u}e^{\int^{u^{\prime}}_{0}\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}(u^{\prime\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}}K_{i}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}
.
Hence, we obtain

(6.42)

|\alpha^{\prime}|(u,v)\leq E^{\prime}(v_{*})+\sum_{i=1}^{11}|I_{i}|(u^{\prime},v)

with

(6.43)

E^{\prime}(v_{*}):=\sup_{\{0\}\times[0,v_{*}]}|\alpha^{\prime}|.

and
|I_{i}|
satisfying the following estimates:

(6.44)

|I_{1}|(u,v)\leq 8\pi\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q||\phi|}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.45)

|I_{2}|(u,v)\leq 4\pi\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q|^{2}}{r^{4}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.46)

|I_{3}|(u,v)\leq 4\pi\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.47)

|I_{4}|(u,v)\leq 2\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)|\alpha^{\prime}|\left(\frac{1}{r}\right)\left(\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.48)

|I_{5}|(u,v)\leq 2\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)|\alpha^{\prime}|\left(\frac{|Q|^{2}}{r^{3}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.49)

|I_{6}|(u,v)\leq 5\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q|^{2}}{r^{4}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.50)

|I_{7}|(u,v)\leq 3\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.51)

|I_{8}|(u,v)\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q||\phi|}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.52)

|I_{9}|(u,v)\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(|\phi|^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.53)

|I_{10}|(u,v)\leq 3\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q|}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.54)

|I_{11}|(u,v)\leq\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q||\phi|}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Next, we proceed to estimate the following quantities:

\frac{1}{1-\mu},\,|\alpha^{\prime}|,\,\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r},\,\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2},\,\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3},\,|Q|,\,|Q\phi|,\,|\phi|^{2}\mbox{ for all }(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*}).

For these terms listed above, we carry out the following strategy:

\frac{1}{1-\mu}
:

With the help of (
1.27
), we compute

\partial_{u}\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}\right)=\frac{\partial_{u}\mu}{(1-\mu)^{2}}=\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}\left(-\frac{4\pi r|D_{u}\phi|^{2}}{(-\partial_{u}r)}+\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right).

This gives

(6.55)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\mu(0,v)}\exp\left({\int^{u}_{0}\left(-\frac{4\pi r|D_{u}\phi|^{2}}{(-\partial_{u}r)}+\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\right).

Since
\partial_{u}r<0
, this implies

(6.56)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\mu(0,v)}\exp\left({\int^{u}_{0}\left(\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\right).

Employing the definition of
\gamma
in (
1.33
) and
\mu_{*}
in (
2.15
), we obtain

(6.57)

\frac{1}{1-\mu}(u,v)\leq\frac{1}{1-\mu_{*}}e^{G}.

|\alpha^{\prime}|
:

By the definition of
B^{\prime}(u)
in (
6.39
), for each
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})
, we have

(6.58)

|\alpha^{\prime}|(u,v)\leq B^{\prime}(u).

\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}
:

Utilizing the definition of
\alpha
in (
5.4
) and
r\phi|_{\Gamma}=0
, we can rewrite
\phi
as

(6.59)

\phi(u,v)=\frac{1}{r}\int^{v}_{u}(\alpha\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime},

which in turn implies that

(6.60)

\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v)=\alpha(u,v)-\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}(\alpha\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Using the fact that
r(u,u)=r|_{\Gamma}=0
and inequality (
6.58
), we obtain

(6.61)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\alpha(u,v)-\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}(\alpha\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\left|\alpha(u,v)-\alpha(u,v^{\prime})\right|\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\left(\int^{v}_{v^{\prime}}\left|\partial_{v}\alpha(u,v^{\prime\prime})\right|\mathrm{d}v^{\prime\prime}\right)\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\left(\int^{v}_{v^{\prime}}B^{\prime}(u)(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime\prime}\right)\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=\frac{B^{\prime}(u)r(u,v)}{2}.

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}:

For this term, we proceed with a different strategy. Observe that since

(6.62)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}

\displaystyle=\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}-\phi\right)^{3}=3\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}-\phi\right)

\displaystyle=3\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}\left(\alpha^{\prime}\partial_{v}r-\partial_{v}\phi\right)=3\alpha^{\prime}\frac{r^{2}(\partial_{v}\phi)^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{3\partial_{v}r}{r}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3},

we then derive

(6.63)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}

\displaystyle\left(r^{3}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}\right)=3r^{2}\partial_{v}r\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}+r^{3}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}

\displaystyle=3r^{2}\partial_{v}r\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}+r^{3}\left(3\alpha^{\prime}\frac{r^{2}(\partial_{v}\phi)^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{3\partial_{v}r}{r}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}\right)=3\alpha^{\prime}r^{3}\left(\frac{r^{2}(\partial_{v}\phi)^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}\right).

Since
r^{3}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}|_{\Gamma}=0
, by (
1.26
), (
6.57
), (
6.58
),
\partial_{v}r\geq 0,1-\mu\geq 0,\partial_{v}m\geq 0
, we deduce

(6.64)

\displaystyle r^{3}\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}

\displaystyle(u,v)\leq\int_{u}^{v}\left|\partial_{v}\left(r^{3}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}\right)\right|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq 3B^{\prime}(u)r^{3}(u,v)\int_{u}^{v}\frac{r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3e^{G}}{2\pi(1-\mu_{*})}B^{\prime}(u)r^{3}(u,v)\int^{v}_{u}\partial_{v}m(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\frac{3e^{G}}{\pi(1-\mu_{*})}B^{\prime}(u)r^{4}(u,v)\mu(u,v),

which in turn implies

(6.65)

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}(u,v)\leq C_{3}^{\prime}B^{\prime}(u)r(u,v)\mu(u,v)

with the constant
C_{1}^{\prime}
given by

(6.66)

C_{3}^{\prime}:=\frac{3e^{G}}{\pi(1-\mu_{*})}.

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}
:

Here we apply an interpolation estimate between (
6.61
) and (
6.65
) to obtain

(6.67)

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}(u,v)=\sqrt{\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}}\leq C_{2}^{\prime}B^{\prime}(u)r(u,v)\mu(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

with

(6.68)

C_{2}^{\prime}:=\sqrt{\frac{3e^{G}}{2\pi(1-\mu_{*})}}

In addition, recall that estimates for
|Q|,|Q\phi|,|\phi|^{2}
have been established in Lemma
6.3
, with
H=\frac{e^{G}}{1-\mu_{*}}
.

With these estimates, we now obtain an upper bound for each of the terms
|I_{i}|
as follows. By plugging (
6.57
), (
6.58
), (
6.61
), (
6.64
), (
6.67
), and (
6.6
), using
r\leq r(0,v_{*})
whenever necessary, we have the following estimates:

(6.69)

|I_{1}|(u,v)\leq 8\pi\mathfrak{e}C_{7}^{\prime}C_{2}^{\prime}r(0,v_{*})\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.70)

|I_{2}|(u,v)\leq 4\pi(C_{6}^{\prime})^{2}C_{3}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.71)

|I_{3}|(u,v)\leq 4\pi C_{3}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

The next two terms
|I_{4}|
and
|I_{5}|
involve
|\alpha^{\prime}|
. By employing (
6.58
), we proceed and obtain

(6.72)

|I_{4}|(u,v)\leq 2\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.73)

|I_{5}|(u,v)\leq 2(C_{6}^{\prime})^{2}\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

For the next three terms
|I_{6}|,|I_{7}|,|I_{8}|
, we have

(6.74)

|I_{6}|(u,v)\leq\frac{5(C_{6}^{\prime})^{2}}{2}\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.75)

|I_{7}|(u,v)\leq\frac{3}{2}\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.76)

|I_{8}|(u,v)\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{e}C_{7}^{\prime}C_{2}^{\prime}r(0,v_{*})\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

The integrands in the terms
|I_{9}|
and
|I_{10}|
lack a natural upper bound in
\mu
to construct the integrand of
\gamma
. Instead, we proceed to show that these integrands have additional powers of
r
as follows:

(6.77)

|I_{9}|(u,v)\leq\frac{2\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}C_{8}^{\prime}e^{G}}{1-\mu_{*}}\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.78)

|I_{10}|(u,v)\leq\frac{3\mathfrak{e}C_{6}^{\prime}e^{G}r(0,v_{*})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1-\mu_{*}}\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

For the final term
|I_{11}|
, its integrand lacks a natural upper bound in terms of
B^{\prime}(u)
. We instead treat this term as an additional forcing term in a Grönwall-type argument that we are about to employ. This implies that we have the following estimate for
|I_{11}|
:

(6.79)

|I_{11}|(u,v)\leq\frac{C_{7}^{\prime\prime}\mathfrak{e}e^{G}}{1-\mu_{*}}\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\chi}{2}}}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\frac{2C_{7}^{\prime\prime}\mathfrak{e}e^{G}r(0,v_{*})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}}{(1-\mu_{*})(1-\chi)}.

Plugging these estimates for
|I_{1}|
to
|I_{11}|
back into (
6.42
), we obtain

(6.80)

|\alpha^{\prime}|(u,v)\leq E^{\prime}(v_{*})+C_{11}+\int_{0}^{u}\left(C_{9}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}+C_{10}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

with the constants
C_{9}
,
C_{10},C_{11}
given by

(6.81)

\displaystyle C_{9}

\displaystyle:=12\pi\mathfrak{e}C_{7}^{\prime}C_{2}^{\prime}r(0,v_{*})+4\pi(C_{6}^{\prime})^{2}C_{3}^{\prime}+\frac{9}{2}(C_{6}^{\prime})^{2}+4\pi C_{3}^{\prime}+\frac{7}{2},

\displaystyle\quad C_{10}

\displaystyle:=\frac{(2\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}C_{8}^{\prime}+3\mathfrak{e}C_{6}^{\prime}r(0,v_{*})^{\frac{1}{2}})e^{G}}{1-\mu_{*}},\quad C_{11}:=\frac{2C_{7}^{\prime\prime}\mathfrak{e}e^{G}r(0,v_{*})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}}{(1-\mu_{*})(1-\chi)}.

For each
u
, we pick
\tilde{v}\in[u,v_{*}]
such that the supremum for (
6.39
) is attained. Since (
6.81
) is true for all
v\in[u,v_{*}]
, we thus have

(6.82)

B^{\prime}(u)\leq E^{\prime}(v_{*})+C_{11}+\int_{0}^{u}\left(C_{9}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}+C_{10}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)(u^{\prime},\tilde{v})B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Applying Grönwall’s inequality, we deduce

(6.83)

\displaystyle B^{\prime}(u)

\displaystyle\leq(E^{\prime}(v_{*})+C_{11})\exp\left(\int_{0}^{u}\left(C_{9}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}+C_{10}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)(u^{\prime},\tilde{v})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(E^{\prime}(v_{*})+C_{11})\exp\left(C_{9}\gamma(u,\tilde{v})+2C_{10}r(0,\tilde{v})^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(E^{\prime}(v_{*})+C_{11})\exp\left(C_{9}G+2C_{10}r(0,v_{*})^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Observe that the upper bound obtained in (
6.83
) is uniform in
u
and
\tilde{v}
. Hence, from (
6.40
), we deduce that

(6.84)

B^{\prime}\leq(E^{\prime}(v_{*})+C_{11})\exp\left(C_{9}G+2C_{10}r(0,v_{*})^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)<+\infty,

which in turn implies that the solution extends as a
C^{1}
solution up to
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})
.

Extension Beyound Boundary.
Next, we will show that there exists some
\xi^{\prime}>0
, such that the solution extends as a
C^{1}
solution to
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*}+\xi^{\prime})
. This is done by estimating the change in
\gamma
across a rectangular region
\{(u,v):u\in[0,v_{*}],v\in[v_{*},v_{*}+\xi]\}
for some
\xi>0
.

\mathcal{O}
\Gamma
C_{0}^{+}
v_{*}
(u_{1},u_{1})
\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*})
v_{*}+\xi
U_{\xi}(u_{1})
Legend:
\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*})
U_{\xi}(u_{1})
\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)

Figure 8.
Illustration of
U_{\xi}(u_{1})
and
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{1},v_{*}+\xi)
.

For any given
u_{1}\in[0,v_{*})
, we consider the corresponding rectangular strip given by

(6.85)

U_{\xi}(u_{1})=[0,u_{1})\times[v_{*},v_{*}+\xi].

In the spirit of (
6.39
), we define

(6.86)

\overline{B^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{1}):=\sup_{U_{\xi}(u_{1})}(|\alpha^{\prime}|).

We also note that, under the convention in the previous subsections, we can write

(6.87)

\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)=\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*})\cup U_{\xi}(u_{1}).

Recall from the first part of the proof that the quantity
B^{\prime}
in (
6.39
) is bounded, independent of
u_{1}
, and obeys the following estimate derived in (
6.84
):

(6.88)

B^{\prime}(u)\leq(E^{\prime}(v_{*})+C_{11}(G))\exp\left(C_{9}(G)G+2C_{10}(G)r(0,v_{*})^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)=:C_{12}(G,v_{*}).

Note that in (
6.88
), the constants
C_{9},C_{10},C_{11}
depends on
G
, and we define
C_{12}
to be a constant depending only on
v_{*}
and
G
.

Now, fix some
\hat{\xi}>0
. We then have the following lemma:

Lemma 6.4
.

For any
u_{1}\in(0,v_{*})
and
\xi\in(0,\hat{\xi}]
, if
\gamma
satisfies

(6.89)

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)}\gamma\leq 3G,

then we have

(6.90)

\overline{B^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{1})\leq\max\{C_{12}(G,v_{*}),C_{12}(3G,v_{*}+\hat{\xi})\}=:B_{2}.

The proof of this lemma follows from the fact that in the region
U_{\xi}(u_{1})
estimates derived as above now rely on the initial data along
\{0\}\times[v_{*},v_{*}+\xi]
and rely on a new uniform upper bound for
\gamma
to be
3G
. The remaining details are similar to those outlined in the estimates for
B^{\prime}(u)
as above. Note that the upper bound in (
6.90
) is independent of
u_{1}
and
\xi
.

With the above lemma, we proceed back on track to prove that the
C^{1}
solution extends beyond the domain of dependence
\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*})
. Next, we define

(6.91)

u_{2}=\sup\left\{u_{1}\in[0,v_{*}):\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)}\gamma\leq 3G\right\}.

By the definition of
u_{2}
, we then have either
u_{2}=v_{*}
or

(6.92)

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)}\gamma=3G

is attained at some
u_{2}\in[u_{0},v_{*})
. We now show that for
\xi
sufficiently small, the latter will not happen. Suppose for a contradiction that the latter happens. Then, we set
u_{1}=u_{2}
as in the hypothesis of Lemma
6.4
. Consequently, as described in the proof of Lemma
6.4
, in the region
U_{\xi}(u_{2})
, we have

(6.93)

\frac{|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v)\leq\frac{B_{2}}{2}.

Note that we can obtain a different estimate for
|Q|
with a higher power of
r
albeit without an upper bound in
\mu
. To do so, we leverage on (
1.18
),
Q|_{\Gamma}=0
and (
6.93
) to obtain

(6.94)

\displaystyle|Q|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{u}^{v}|\partial_{v}Q|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq 2\pi\mathfrak{e}B_{2}C_{8}^{\prime}\int_{u}^{v}r^{\frac{5}{4}}\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\frac{8\pi\mathfrak{e}B_{2}\sqrt{C_{8}^{\prime}}}{9}r^{\frac{9}{4}}(u,v)\leq C_{13}r^{2}(u,v),

with

(6.95)

C_{13}:=\frac{8\pi\mathfrak{e}B_{2}\sqrt{C_{8}^{\prime}}r^{\frac{1}{4}}(0,v_{*})}{9}.

Here, the dependence of
C_{8}^{\prime}
on
v_{*}+\hat{\xi}
is dropped for brevity.

We are now able to estimate
m(u,v)
as follows. By (
1.26
) and
m|_{\Gamma}=0
, these imply

(6.96)

m(u,v)\leq\int^{v}_{u}\left(\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}+\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}\right)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Employing (
6.93
), we get

(6.97)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{u}\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi\int^{v}_{u}\frac{r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\pi B_{2}^{2}}{2}\int^{v}_{u}r^{2}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=\frac{\pi B_{2}^{2}}{6}r^{3}(u,v).

Furthermore, by (
6.94
), we obtain

(6.98)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{u}\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{13}^{2}}{2}\int^{v}_{u}r^{2}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=\frac{C_{13}^{2}}{6}r^{3}(u,v).

This in turn implies that

(6.99)

\mu(u,v)\leq\left(\frac{\pi B_{2}^{2}}{3}+\frac{C_{13}^{2}}{3}\right)r^{2}(u,v).

Meanwhile, from (
6.57
) but with
G
replaced by
3G
, we have

(6.100)

\frac{1}{1-\mu}(u,v)\leq\frac{1}{1-\mu_{*}}e^{3G}.

With the above estimates, we can now estimate the change in
\gamma
. Recall the expressions for the derivative of
\gamma
with respect to
v
from (
1.40
) and (
1.41
) as below:

(6.101)

\partial_{v}\gamma(u,v)=\int_{0}^{u}(T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3})(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

By utilizing the estimates on relevant quantities above, together with (
1.34
), we derive

(6.102)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{u}|T_{1}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\pi\mathfrak{e}e^{3G}}{1-\mu_{*}}\int_{0}^{u}\frac{|Q||\phi||\partial_{v}\phi|}{r}(-\partial_{u}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\frac{4\pi\mathfrak{e}e^{3G}\sqrt{C_{8}^{\prime}}C_{13}B_{2}}{1-\mu_{*}}\int_{0}^{u}r^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\partial_{v}r)(-\partial_{u}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\pi\mathfrak{e}e^{4G}\sqrt{C_{8}^{\prime}}C_{13}B_{2}}{1-\mu_{*}}\int_{0}^{u}r^{-\frac{1}{4}}(-\partial_{u}r)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\frac{(8)^{\frac{3}{4}}\pi\mathfrak{e}e^{4G}\sqrt{C_{8}^{\prime}}C_{13}B_{2}}{3(1-\mu_{*})}(v_{*}+\hat{\xi})^{\frac{3}{4}},

(6.103)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{u}|T_{2}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\pi e^{3G}}{1-\mu_{*}}\left(1+\frac{C_{13}^{2}(v_{*}+\hat{\xi})^{2}}{4}\right)\int_{0}^{u}(-\partial_{u}r)\frac{|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\pi e^{3G}B_{2}^{2}}{1-\mu_{*}}\left(1+\frac{C_{13}^{2}(v_{*}+\hat{\xi})^{2}}{4}\right)\int_{0}^{u}(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\pi e^{4G}B_{2}^{2}}{1-\mu_{*}}\left(1+\frac{C_{13}^{2}(v_{*}+\hat{\xi})^{2}}{4}\right)\int_{0}^{u}(-\partial_{u}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\pi e^{4G}B_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\mu_{*})}\left(1+\frac{C_{13}^{2}(v_{*}+\hat{\xi})^{2}}{4}\right)(v_{*}+\hat{\xi}),

(6.104)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{u}|T_{3}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2e^{3G}}{1-\mu_{*}}\int_{0}^{u}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r^{2}}\left(|\mu|+\frac{2Q^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2e^{3G}}{1-\mu_{*}}\left(\frac{\pi B_{2}^{2}}{3}+\frac{7C_{13}^{2}}{3}\right)\int_{0}^{u}(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{4G}}{1-\mu_{*}}\left(\frac{\pi B_{2}^{2}}{3}+\frac{7C_{13}^{2}}{3}\right)\int_{0}^{u}(-\partial_{u}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{4G}}{2(1-\mu_{*})}\left(\frac{\pi B_{2}^{2}}{3}+\frac{7C_{13}^{2}}{3}\right)(v_{*}+\hat{\xi}).

Note that (
6.102
), (
6.103
), (
6.104
) imply

\int^{u}_{0}|T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}<+\infty
, and hence the expression in (
6.101
) is valid by the dominated convergence theorem. Furthermore, for all
(u,v)\in U_{\xi}(u_{2})
, we also obtain

(6.105)

|\partial_{v}\gamma|(u,v)\leq C_{14}

with

(6.106)

\displaystyle C_{14}:=

\displaystyle\frac{(8)^{\frac{3}{4}}\pi\mathfrak{e}e^{4G}\sqrt{C_{8}^{\prime}}C_{13}B_{2}}{3(1-\mu_{*})}(v_{*}+\hat{\xi})^{\frac{3}{4}}+\frac{\pi e^{4G}B_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\mu_{*})}\left(1+\frac{C_{13}^{2}(v_{*}+\hat{\xi})^{2}}{4}\right)(v_{*}+\hat{\xi})

\displaystyle+\frac{e^{4G}}{2(1-\mu_{*})}\left(\frac{\pi B_{2}^{2}}{3}+\frac{7C_{13}^{2}}{3}\right)(v_{*}+\hat{\xi}).

For each
(u,v)\in U_{\xi}(u_{2})
, we thus have

(6.107)

\displaystyle\gamma(u,v)\leq\gamma(u,v_{*})+\int_{v}^{v_{*}}|\partial_{v}\gamma|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq G+C_{14}\xi.

If we pick

(6.108)

\xi:=\min\left\{\hat{\xi},\frac{G}{C_{14}}\right\},

we then deduce that

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)}\gamma\leq 2G
, contradicting (
6.92
).

Henceforth, for each
u_{1}\in(u_{0},v_{*})
, the hypothesis of Lemma
6.4
is verified. This then implies that (
6.90
) holds and hence
\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*}+\Delta v)}|\alpha^{\prime}|
remains bounded as
u_{1}\rightarrow v_{*}
. Therefore, we conclude that the solution extends as a
C^{1}
solution up to the closure of
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},v_{*},v_{*}+\xi)
. In particular, with
C^{1}
initial data prescribed along
\{v_{*}\}\times[v_{*},v_{*}+\xi]
, we show that the solution further extends as a
C^{1}
solution to
\mathcal{D}(v_{*},v_{*}+\xi^{\prime})
for some
\xi^{\prime}\in(0,\xi]
. This concludes the proof of Theorem
6.1
. ∎

We continue to prove Theorem
6.2
.

Proof of Theorem
6.2
.
By the assumption, we can find a
u_{0}<v_{*}
such that

(6.109)

\varepsilon=\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},v_{*})}\mu<\varepsilon^{\prime}.

Given any
u_{1}\in(u_{0},v_{*})
, we further define

(6.110)

\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*}):=\mathcal{D}(u_{0},v_{*})\setminus\overline{\mathcal{D}}(u_{1},v_{*}).

This region is demonstrated in Figure
7
. For any given
\delta\in(0,1)
, we set

(6.111)

A^{\prime}(u_{1}):=\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})}(r^{\delta}|\alpha^{\prime}|).

We will show that this quantity remains bounded as
u_{1}\rightarrow v_{*}
. Based on it, we will further prove that

(6.112)

B^{\prime}(u_{1}):=\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})}(|\alpha^{\prime}|)

remains bounded as
u_{1}\rightarrow v_{*}
. The fact that the solution extends to
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})
as a
C^{1}
solution then follows.

\mathcal{O}
\Gamma
C_{0}^{+}
v_{*}
(u_{0},u_{0})
(u_{1},u_{1})
(v_{*},v_{*})
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})

Figure 9.
Illustration of
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},v_{1},v_{*})
.

Estimates for
A^{\prime}(u_{1})
.

To bound
A^{\prime}(u_{1})
, we begin by applying Lemma
6.3
to obtain (
6.4
). From (
6.4
), for every
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})
, via integration along the
u
direction, we obtain

(6.113)

\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}(u,v)e^{\int^{u}_{u_{0}}\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{\prime}(u_{0},v)+\sum_{i=1}^{11}I_{i}(u,u_{0},v).

We now define

(6.114)

D^{\prime}(u_{0}):=\sup_{\{u_{0}\}\times(u_{0},v_{*})}(r^{\delta}|\alpha^{\prime}|)

and observe that this term is finite as we have
\alpha\in C^{1}(\{u_{0}\}\times[u_{0},v_{*}])
.
This implies

(6.115)

\displaystyle|\alpha^{\prime}(u,v)|

\displaystyle\leq D^{\prime}(u_{0})\left(r(u_{0},v)\right)^{-\delta}+\sum_{i=1}^{11}|I_{i}|(u,u_{0},v)

with

(6.116)

I_{i}(u,u_{0},v)=\int^{u}_{u_{0}}e^{\int^{u^{\prime}}_{u_{0}}\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}(u^{\prime\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}}K_{i}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Since
A_{u}
is real-valued, we also obtain

(6.117)

|I_{i}|(u,u_{0},v)\leq\int^{u}_{u_{0}}|K_{i}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Next, we move to derive an upper bound for each of the
I_{i}
’s. We begin by observing that

(6.118)

|I_{1}|(u,u_{0},v)\leq 8\pi\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q||\phi|}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.119)

|I_{2}|(u,u_{0},v)\leq 4\pi\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q|^{2}}{r^{4}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.120)

|I_{3}|(u,u_{0},v)\leq 4\pi\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.121)

|I_{4}|(u,u_{0},v)\leq 2\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)|\alpha^{\prime}|\left(\frac{1}{r}\right)\left(\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.122)

|I_{5}|(u,u_{0},v)\leq 2\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)|\alpha^{\prime}|\left(\frac{|Q|^{2}}{r^{3}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.123)

|I_{6}|(u,u_{0},v)\leq 5\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q|^{2}}{r^{4}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.124)

|I_{7}|(u,u_{0},v)\leq 3\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.125)

|I_{8}|(u,u_{0},v)\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q||\phi|}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.126)

|I_{9}|(u,u_{0},v)\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(|\phi|^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.127)

|I_{10}|(u,u_{0},v)\leq 3\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q|}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(6.128)

|I_{11}|(u,u_{0},v)\leq\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q||\phi|}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

In order for us to prove desired upper bounds for these
11
terms, we derive useful estimates for

\frac{1}{1-\mu},\,|\alpha^{\prime}|,\,\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r},\,\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2},\,\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3},\,|Q|,\,|Q\phi|,\,|\phi|^{2}\mbox{ for all }(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*}).

For these listed terms, we carry out the following strategy

\frac{1}{1-\mu}
:

Using the definition of
\varepsilon
in (
6.109
), we have

(6.129)

\frac{1}{1-\mu}(u,v)\leq\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}.

|\alpha^{\prime}|
:

Appealing to the definition of
A(u_{1})
in (
6.111
), we get

(6.130)

|\alpha^{\prime}|(u,v)\leq A^{\prime}(u_{1})r^{-\delta}(u,v).

\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}
:

Recall from the proof of Theorem
6.1
that we have

(6.131)

\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v)=\alpha(u,v)-\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}(\alpha\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Using the fact that
r(u,u)=r|_{\Gamma}=0
, inequality (
6.130
) and the definition of
A^{\prime}(u_{1})
in (
6.111
), we obtain

(6.132)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\alpha(u,v)-\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}(\alpha\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\left|\alpha(u,v)-\alpha(u,v^{\prime})\right|\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\left(\int^{v}_{v^{\prime}}\left|\partial_{v}\alpha(u,v^{\prime\prime})\right|\mathrm{d}v^{\prime\prime}\right)\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\left(\int^{v}_{v^{\prime}}A^{\prime}(u_{1})r^{-\delta}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime\prime}\right)\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r(u,v)}\left(\frac{r^{2-\delta}}{(1-\delta)}-\frac{r^{2-\delta}}{(1-\delta)(2-\delta)}\right)(u,v)=\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})r^{1-\delta}(u,v)}{2-\delta}.

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}
:

Here we will obtain an upper bound, which is linear in
A^{\prime}(u_{1})
. We recall a lemma established by Christodoulou in
[
19
]
as follows:

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
f
be a
C^{1}
function on
[0,r_{*}]
with
f(0)=0
and let

(6.133)

a:=\sup_{r\in(0,r_{*})}\left(\frac{g}{r}\right),\quad b:=\sup_{r\in(0,r_{*})}\left(r^{\delta}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}f}{\mathrm{d}r}\right|\right)

with

(6.134)

g(r)=\int^{r}_{0}f^{2}\mathrm{d}r.

Then, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, we have

(6.135)

|f(r)|^{3}\leq\frac{3ab}{1-\delta}r^{1-\delta}.

Proof.

Observe that

(6.136)

f(r)^{3}=\int^{r}_{0}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}f(r)^{3}\mathrm{d}r=\int^{r}_{0}3f^{2}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}f}{\mathrm{d}r}\right)\mathrm{d}r.

This implies

(6.137)

\displaystyle|f(r)|^{3}

\displaystyle\leq\int^{r}_{0}3f^{2}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}f}{\mathrm{d}r}\right|\mathrm{d}r\leq 3b\int^{r}_{0}r^{-\delta}f^{2}\mathrm{d}r=3b\left(\int^{r}_{0}r^{-\delta}\frac{dg}{dr}\mathrm{d}r\right)

\displaystyle=3b\left(r^{-\delta}g(r)-r^{-\delta}g(r)|_{r=0}+\delta\int^{r}_{0}gr^{-1-\delta}\mathrm{d}r\right)

\displaystyle\leq 3b\left(ar^{1-\delta}+a\delta\int^{r}_{0}r^{-\delta}\mathrm{d}r\right)=\frac{3ab}{1-\delta}r^{1-\delta}.

Here we use the fact that

(6.138)

r^{-\delta}g(r)|_{r=0}=\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{g(r)}{r^{\delta}}=\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{f(r)^{2}}{\delta r^{\delta-1}}=\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{f(r)^{2}r^{1-\delta}}{\delta}=0.

∎

We then set
f=\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}
and apply the above lemma. To obtain a bound for
\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}
, we need to compute
a
and
b
as in (
6.133
). For each fixed
u\in(u_{0},u_{1})
with
r_{*}=r(u,v_{*})
, we have

(6.139)

\displaystyle a(u)

\displaystyle=\sup_{r\in(0,r_{*})}\left(\frac{1}{r}\int^{r}_{0}f(r^{\prime})^{2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)=\sup_{v\in(u,v_{*})}\left(\frac{1}{r}\int^{v}_{u}\left(\frac{(r|\partial_{v}\phi|)^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right).

Noting that (
1.26
) implies
\frac{(r|\partial_{v}\phi|)^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}\leq\frac{\partial_{v}m}{2\pi(1-\mu)}
, we therefore obtain

(6.140)

\displaystyle a\leq\sup_{v\in(u,v_{*})}\left(\frac{1}{2\pi r}\int^{v}_{u}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}m}{1-\mu}\right)\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4\pi(1-\varepsilon)}\sup_{v\in(u,v_{*})}\left(\mu(u,v)-\frac{2m(u,u)}{r(u,v)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4\pi(1-\varepsilon)}.

For
b
, by applying (
6.130
) and (
6.132
), we have

(6.141)

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=\sup_{r\in(0,r_{*})}\left(r^{\delta}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}f}{\mathrm{d}r}\right|\right)=\sup_{v\in(u,v_{*})}\left(r^{\delta}\left|\frac{\partial_{v}f}{\partial_{v}r}\right|\right)=\sup_{v\in(u,v_{*})}\left(\frac{r^{\delta}}{\partial_{v}r}\left|\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}-\phi\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})}\left(\frac{r^{\delta}}{\partial_{v}r}\left|\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}-\phi\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})}\left(r^{\delta}|\alpha^{\prime}|\right)+\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})}\left(r^{\delta-1}\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq A^{\prime}(u_{1})+\frac{1}{2-\delta}A^{\prime}(u_{1})=\frac{3-\delta}{2-\delta}A^{\prime}(u_{1}).

Henceforth, combining the lemma together with (
6.140
) and (
6.141
), we obtain

(6.142)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}(u,v)\leq\frac{3ab}{1-\delta}r^{1-\delta}\leq\frac{3(3-\delta)}{4\pi(1-\delta)(2-\delta)}\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}A^{\prime}(u_{1})r^{1-\delta}.

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}:

A direct interpolation between the upper bounds in (
6.132
) and (
6.142
) implies that

(6.143)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}(u,v)=\sqrt{\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{3(3-\delta)}{4\pi(1-\delta)(2-\delta)^{2}}\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}}A^{\prime}(u_{1})r^{1-\delta}.

Note that the estimates for
|Q|,|Q\phi|,
and
|\phi|^{2}
have been established in Lemma
6.3
.

With these estimates, we now obtain an estimate for each of the
|I_{i}|
’s as follows. For
I_{1}
, we have

(6.144)

\displaystyle|I_{1}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq 8\pi\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q||\phi|}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 8\pi\mathfrak{e}C_{7}A^{\prime}(u_{1})\sqrt{\frac{3(3-\delta)}{4\pi(1-\delta)(2-\delta)^{2}}}\frac{\varepsilon}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}-\delta}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 8\pi\mathfrak{e}C_{7}A^{\prime}(u_{1})\sqrt{\frac{3(3-\delta)}{4\pi(1-\delta)(2-\delta)^{2}}}\frac{\varepsilon}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}\frac{r^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}(u_{0},v)}{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{r^{\delta}(u,v)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\pi\mathfrak{e}C_{7}r^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}(0,v_{*})}{3-\chi}\sqrt{\frac{3(3-\delta)}{4\pi(1-\delta)(2-\delta)^{2}}}\frac{\varepsilon}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r^{\delta}(u,v)}.

Here we use the fact
\chi\in(0,1)
,
\delta\in(0,1)
and
\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}-\delta>-1
.

Following a similar idea, the next term can be bounded as follows:

(6.145)

\displaystyle|I_{2}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq 4\pi\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q|^{2}}{r^{4}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C_{6}^{2}A^{\prime}(u_{1})\frac{3(3-\delta)}{(1-\delta)(2-\delta)}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)r^{-\delta-\chi}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{6}^{2}r(0,v_{*})^{1-\chi}}{1-\chi}\frac{3(3-\delta)}{(1-\delta)(2-\delta)}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r^{\delta}(u,v)}.

We proceed to derive

(6.146)

\displaystyle|I_{3}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq 4\pi\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq A^{\prime}(u_{1})\frac{3(3-\delta)}{(1-\delta)(2-\delta)}\frac{\varepsilon}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)r^{-1-\delta}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3(3-\delta)}{\delta(1-\delta)(2-\delta)}\frac{\varepsilon}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r^{\delta}(u,v)}

and

(6.147)

\displaystyle|I_{4}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq 2\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)|\alpha^{\prime}|\left(\frac{1}{r}\right)\left(\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 2A^{\prime}(u_{1})\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)r^{-1-\delta}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\frac{2}{\delta}\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r^{\delta}(u,v)}.

The subsequent three terms obey the following bounds

(6.148)

\displaystyle|I_{5}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq 2\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)|\alpha^{\prime}|\left(\frac{|Q|^{2}}{r^{3}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 2C^{2}_{6}A^{\prime}(u_{1})\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)r^{-\delta-\chi}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\frac{2C^{2}_{6}r^{1-\chi}(0,v_{*})}{1-\chi}\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r^{\delta}(u,v)},

(6.149)

\displaystyle|I_{6}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq 5\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q|^{2}}{r^{4}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 5C_{6}^{2}A^{\prime}(u_{1})\frac{1}{2-\delta}\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)r^{-\delta-\chi}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5C_{6}^{2}r^{1-\chi}(0,v_{*})}{(1-\chi)(2-\delta)}\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r^{\delta}(u,v)},

(6.150)

\displaystyle|I_{7}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq 3\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{2-\delta}\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)r^{-1-\delta}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\frac{3}{\delta(2-\delta)}\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r^{\delta}(u,v)}.

For the next term
I_{8}
, we note that its upper bound is exactly half of that of
I_{1}
. This implies

(6.151)

\displaystyle|I_{8}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q||\phi|}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\pi\mathfrak{e}C_{7}r^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}(0,v_{*})}{3-\chi}\sqrt{\frac{3(3-\delta)}{4\pi(1-\delta)(2-\delta)^{2}}}\frac{\varepsilon}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r^{\delta}(u,v)}.

To estimate
|I_{9}|
, we employ (
6.37
) and get

(6.152)

\displaystyle|I_{9}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(|\phi|^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{2-\delta}\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)r^{1-\delta}|\phi|^{2}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{2-\delta}\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)r^{1-\delta}\left(\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}+2C_{8}^{2}(\iota)r^{-1-\iota}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}}{2-\delta}\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r^{\delta}(u,v)}\left(\frac{r^{2}(0,v_{*})}{4\pi}\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}+\frac{2C_{8}^{2}(\iota)r^{1-\iota}(u_{0},v_{*})}{1-\iota}\right).

The upper bounds for the last two terms
I_{10}
and
I_{11}
can be derived as

(6.153)

\displaystyle|I_{10}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq 3\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q|}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 3\mathfrak{e}C_{6}A^{\prime}(u_{1})\frac{1}{2-\delta}\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1-\varepsilon}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}-\delta}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\mathfrak{e}C_{6}r^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}(u_{0},v_{*})}{(3-\chi)(2-\delta)}\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r^{\delta}(u,v)}

and

(6.154)

\displaystyle|I_{11}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{e}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{|Q||\phi|}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{e}C_{7}\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(1-\varepsilon)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)r^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{e}C_{7}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta-\frac{\chi}{2}}(0,v_{*})\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(1-\varepsilon)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int^{u}_{u_{0}}(-\partial_{u}r)r^{-1-\delta}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\mathfrak{e}C_{7}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta-\frac{\chi}{2}}(0,v_{*})}{\delta}\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(1-\varepsilon)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\frac{1}{r^{\delta}(u,v)}.

Note that in (
6.154
), we have restricted our choice for
\chi
to
\chi<2\delta
.

Back to (
6.115
), multiplying
r^{\delta}(u,v)
on both sides and taking the supremum over
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})
, we therefore obtain

(6.155)

A^{\prime}(u_{1})\leq D^{\prime}(u_{0})+\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{11}|I_{i}|r^{\delta}(u,v)\right)

with
D^{\prime}(u_{0})
defined in (
6.114
). For convenience, we impose the requirement

(6.156)

\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2}.

With this restriction, we employ obvious bounds (such as
0<\iota,\delta,\chi<1
and
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2}
) to simplify the constants appearing in the upper bounds for
|I_{1}|
to
|I_{11}|
. We will also apply
1\leq\frac{1}{(1-\delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\delta}\leq\frac{1}{\delta(1-\delta)}
,
\frac{1}{\delta}\leq\frac{1}{\delta(1-\delta)}
,
\frac{1}{(1-\varepsilon)^{b}}\leq 4
for any
b\in[1,2]
. Furthermore, for the ease of impending computation, we will lower all powers of
\varepsilon
that are greater than
\frac{1}{2}
in the numerator to
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
since
\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2}
. For brevity, we will also define the unified coupling constant
E
as

(6.157)

E:=\max\{1,\mathfrak{e},\mathfrak{e}^{2}\}.

With the above, we can simplify the constants in our estimates for
|I_{i}|
. We start by analyzing
|I_{1}|
and get

(6.158)

\displaystyle|I_{1}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\pi\mathfrak{e}C_{7}r^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}(0,v_{*})}{3-\chi}\sqrt{\frac{3(3-\delta)}{4\pi(1-\delta)(2-\delta)^{2}}}\frac{\varepsilon}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r^{\delta}(u,v)}\leq\frac{C_{9,1}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\delta(1-\delta)}\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r(u,v)^{\delta}}

with constant
C_{9,1}>0
depending on
v_{*}
,
\chi
,
E
. Explicitly for
|I_{1}|
, this is given by

(6.159)

C_{9,1}=16\sqrt{6\pi}EC_{7}r^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}(0,v_{*}).

One can repeat a similar argument for
|I_{i}|
with
i=2,3,4,5,6,7,8,10
and get constants
C_{9,i}>0
depending on
v_{*}
,
\chi
, and
E
.
5
5

5

Here, observe that
C_{7}
also depends on
v_{*},\chi,E
by its explicit expression in (
6.11
).
Note that each of these constants does not depend on
u_{0}
. For
i=11
, we employ a similar bound without
A^{\prime}(u_{1})
and obtain

(6.160)

\displaystyle|I_{11}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\mathfrak{e}C_{7}r^{\delta-\frac{\chi}{2}}(0,v_{*})}{\delta}\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{1}{r^{\delta}(u,v)}\leq\frac{C_{10,11}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\delta(1-\delta)}\frac{1}{r(u,v)^{\delta}}

with constant
C_{10,11}>0
depending on
v_{*}
,
\chi
,
E
. For
i=9
, there is a term without
\varepsilon
. Thus, we use the following upper bound

(6.161)

\displaystyle|I_{9}|(u,u_{0},v)

\displaystyle\leq\left(C_{9,9}\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\delta(1-\delta)}+C_{11,9}(u_{0};\iota)\right)\frac{A^{\prime}(u_{1})}{r(u,v)^{\delta}}

with
C_{9,9}>0
and
C_{11,9}(u_{0})>0
. Note that
C_{9,9}
depends only on
v_{*},\chi,E
, while
C_{11,9}
depends additionally on
\iota
,
u_{0}
and can be expressed as

(6.162)

C_{11,9}(u_{0};\iota)=\frac{32\pi EC_{8}^{2}(\iota)}{1-\iota}r^{1-\iota}(u_{0},v_{*}).

Combining these estimates together with (
6.155
), we derive

(6.163)

A^{\prime}(u_{1})\leq D^{\prime}(u_{0})+\left(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{10}C_{9,i}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\delta(1-\delta)}+C_{11,9}(u_{0};\iota)\right)A^{\prime}(u_{1})+\frac{C_{10,11}}{\delta(1-\delta)}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Next, we demand the following constraints on the constants

(6.164)

\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{10}C_{9,i}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\delta(1-\delta)}<\frac{1}{4},

and

(6.165)

C_{11,9}(u_{0};\iota)<\frac{1}{8}.

Note that (
6.164
) can be rewritten as

(6.166)

\varepsilon<\left(\frac{\delta(1-\delta)}{4\sum_{i=1}^{10}C_{9,i}}\right)^{2}.

For (
6.165
), this can be attained by picking
u_{0}
sufficiently close to
v_{*}
with using the explicit expression of
C_{11,9}(u_{0})
in (
6.162
), in which for a fixed
\iota\in(0,1)
, the term
r^{1-\iota}(u_{0},v_{*})
serves as a small factor which goes to
0
as
u_{0}\rightarrow v_{*}
. Henceforth, (
6.164
) implies

(6.167)

\frac{C_{10,11}}{\delta(1-\delta)}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}<\frac{C_{10,11}}{4\sum_{i=1}^{10}C_{9,i}}=:C_{12}<+\infty.

From (
6.156
) and (
6.166
), if we pick

(6.168)

\varepsilon^{\prime}=\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\left(\frac{\delta(1-\delta)}{4\sum_{i=1}^{10}C_{9,i}}\right)^{2}\right\},

then for all
\varepsilon<\varepsilon^{\prime}
, we have that (
6.156
) and (
6.166
) hold. Plugging (
6.164
), (
6.165
), and (
6.167
) back into (
6.163
), we deduce that

(6.169)

A^{\prime}(u_{1})\leq 2(D^{\prime}(u_{0})+C_{12})=:C_{13}.

In other words,
A^{\prime}(u_{1})
is bounded by a constant depending only on
\delta
,
\iota
,
\chi
. Note that this bound is valid as long as we have
\chi
,
\delta
,
\iota
satisfying
0<\chi<\delta<1
and
0<\iota<1
.

Estimates for
B^{\prime}(u_{1})
.

Next, we will further show that
B^{\prime}(u_{1})
as defined in (
6.112
) remains bounded. This is important for concluding the extension of the
C^{1}
solution up to the boundary as this in turn implies that the solution in
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})
is indeed
C^{1}
. We start off by observing from (
6.132
) and (
6.169
) that

(6.170)

\displaystyle\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq C_{14}(\delta)r^{1-\delta}(u,v)\mbox{ with }C_{14}(\delta):=\frac{C_{13}}{1-\delta}.

By (
1.26
), from
m|_{\Gamma}=0
, this implies

(6.171)

m(u,v)\leq\int^{v}_{u}\left(\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}+\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}\right)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

The first term in (
6.171
) can be estimated with the help of (
6.170
) as

(6.172)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{u}\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi\int^{v}_{u}\frac{r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi\int^{v}_{u}C_{14}^{2}r^{2-2\delta}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=\frac{2\pi C_{14}^{2}}{3-2\delta}r^{3-2\delta}(u,v).

For the second term, from (
6.28
), we have

(6.173)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{u}\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\frac{C_{6}^{2}}{2}\int^{v}_{u}r^{1-\chi}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=\frac{C_{6}^{2}}{2(2-\chi)}r^{2-\chi}(u,v).

If we restrict our choice of
\delta
to
\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right]
, from (
6.171
), (
6.172
), (
6.173
), we observe that

(6.174)

m(u,v)\leq\frac{C_{15}}{2}r^{2-\chi}(u,v)

with

(6.175)

C_{15}(\delta,\chi):=\frac{4\pi C_{14}^{2}}{3-2\delta}r^{1-2\delta+\chi}(0,v_{*})+\frac{C_{6}^{2}}{(2-\chi)}.

Consequently, we obtain

(6.176)

\mu(u,v)\leq C_{15}r^{1-\chi}(u,v).

To see how (
6.176
) can help us to obtain a bound for
B^{\prime}(u_{1})
, we first revisit (
6.113
) and observe that if we define

(6.177)

D^{\prime\prime}(u_{0}):=\sup_{\{u_{0}\}\times[u_{0},v_{*}]}(|\alpha^{\prime}|)<+\infty,

we then have

(6.178)

|\alpha^{\prime}(u,v)|\leq D^{\prime\prime}(u_{0})+\sum_{i=1}^{11}|I_{i}|(u,u_{0},v)

with
I_{i}
defined in (
6.117
) remains unchanged. Analogous to (
6.155
), taking supremum over
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})
yields

(6.179)

B^{\prime}(u_{1})\leq D^{\prime\prime}(u_{0})+\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{11}|I_{i}|\right).

By comparing (
6.179
) with (
6.155
), we observe that the supremum is taken without an additional factor of
r^{\delta}(u,v)
paired with each
|I_{i}|
. This is where (
6.176
) comes in. By viewing an upper bound for
\mu
as that for
\varepsilon
, we are able to pick appropriate values of
\delta,\chi,\iota
such that the term
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
could be replaced by sufficient powers of
r(u,v)
to counteract the term
r^{-\delta}(u,v)
in each of the estimates for
|I_{i}|
. The details for relevant terms are provided as follows.

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}
:

By (
6.170
), we have

(6.180)

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}\leq C_{14}^{2}r^{2-2\delta}(u,v).

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}
:

By (
6.170
), we obtain

(6.181)

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}\leq C_{14}^{3}r^{3-3\delta}(u,v).

|Q|
:

As (
6.28
) was derived from a more general expression in Proposition
3.3
, if we instead apply (
6.176
), we would obtain

(6.182)

|Q|(u,v)\leq C_{6}C_{15}^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{2-\chi}(u,v).

|Q\phi|:

Similar to
|Q|
, if we work through the corresponding arguments and apply (
6.176
) instead, we get

(6.183)

|Q\phi|\leq\frac{C_{7}C_{15}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(1-\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}}r^{2-\chi}(u,v).

|\phi|^{2}:

In the same fashion, we deduce

(6.184)

\displaystyle|\phi|^{2}(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{15}}{2\pi}\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}r^{1-\chi}(u,v)+2C_{8}^{2}(\iota)r^{-1-\iota}(u,v)\leq\frac{C_{16}}{1-\varepsilon}r^{-1-\iota}(u,v)

with
C_{16}(\chi,\iota,\delta):=\frac{C_{15}(\delta,\chi)}{2\pi}r^{2-\chi+\iota}(0,v_{*})+2C_{8}^{2}(\iota).

For the remaining terms with
A^{\prime}(u_{1})
, a direct application of (
6.169
) suffices.

With the above preparation, we are now ready to derive improved estimates for
|I_{i}|
with
i=1
to
11
. We summarize the bounds obtained for each
|K_{i}|(u,v)
defined in (
6.117
) and thus list the bounds for each
|I_{i}|(u,u_{0},v)
in Table
1
. Here we use
\lesssim
to refer to inequalities with constants depending only on
v_{*}
and parameters
\delta,\chi,\iota
. Meanwhile, due to (
6.169
), the dependence on
A^{\prime}(u_{1})
will be encoded under
\lesssim
as
C_{13}
depends on
\delta,\chi,\iota,v_{*}
, while being independent of
u_{1}
. Furthermore, we recall that we require the parameters to satisfy
0<\chi<\delta\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
0<\iota<1
.
