.

From Table
1
, we observe the relevant improvement in the estimates for
|I_{3}|
,
|I_{4}|
,
|I_{7}|
,
|I_{11}|
. In addition, we see that

(6.185)

\sum_{i=1}^{11}|I_{i}|(u,u_{0},v)\lesssim r^{1-\chi-\delta}(u,v)=r^{\frac{1}{4}}(u,v)\leq r^{\frac{1}{4}}(0,v_{*})<+\infty

if we pick
\delta=\frac{1}{2}
and
\iota=\chi=\frac{1}{4}
. Henceforth, from (
6.179
), we deduce that
B^{\prime}(u_{1})
uniformly bounded in
u_{1}
. We then conclude that the solution extends up to
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})
as a
C^{1}
solution.

Extension Beyond Boundary.
Last but not least, we will show that there exists some
\xi^{\prime}>0
such that the solution extends as a
C^{1}
solution to
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*}+\xi^{\prime})
. This is done by estimating the change in
\mu
across a rectangular region
\{(u,v):u\in[0,v_{*}],v\in[v_{*},v_{*}+\xi]\}
for some
\xi>0
and through a contradiction argument. Furthermore, to maintain the generality of the arguments and to draw parallel conclusions made in the previous subsections, we will keep
\delta,\chi,\iota
as parameters to be chosen, and it is implicitly assumed that all constants depend on
\delta,\chi,\iota
unless stated otherwise. One can observe that the argument below holds if we retain our choice of
\delta=\frac{1}{2}
and
\chi=\iota=\frac{1}{4}
as in the previous subsection.

\mathcal{O}
\Gamma
C_{0}^{+}
v_{*}
(u_{0},u_{0})
(u_{1},u_{1})
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})
v_{*}+\xi
U_{\xi}(u_{1})
Legend:
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})
U_{\xi}(u_{1})
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)

Figure 10.
Illustration of
U_{\xi}(u_{1})
and
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},v_{1},v_{*}+\xi)
.

First, we consider a
u_{0}\in[0,v_{*})
sufficiently close to
v_{*}
so that (
6.109
) holds. Next, we use the continuity of
\partial_{v}r
to deduce that
\partial_{v}r\geq 0
on
\{u_{0}\}\times[v_{*},v_{*}+\Delta v]
for some fixed
\Delta v>0
. Then, for any
0<\xi<\Delta v
, we consider the corresponding rectangular strip given by

(6.186)

U_{\xi}(u_{1})=[u_{0},u_{1})\times[v_{*},v_{*}+\xi].

In the spirit of (
6.111
), we define

(6.187)

\overline{A^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{1}):=\sup_{U_{\xi}(u_{1})}(r^{\delta}|\alpha^{\prime}|).

We also note that, under the convention in the previous subsections, we can write

(6.188)

\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)=\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})\cup U_{\xi}(u_{1}).

In below we will show that
\overline{A^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{1})
remains bounded for some
u_{1}\in(u_{0},v_{*})
sufficiently close to
u_{0}
. This can be proved by utilizing the continuity of
\mu
on
\mathcal{Q}
. With
\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}
as in the previous subsections, if we pick
v_{*}-u_{0}
and
\xi
to be sufficiently small, then we have

(6.189)

\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}(u_{1}):=\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)}\mu\leq\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime})<\varepsilon^{\prime}.

Later, we will show that
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}(u_{1})
defined above obeys the same upper bound as in (
6.189
) for all
u_{1}\in(u_{0},v_{*})
.

Before we begin detailing the arguments, we will first state and prove two lemmas that would be useful. We first define

(6.190)

A^{\prime}_{*}:=\sup_{u_{1}\in(u_{0},v_{*})}A^{\prime}(u_{1})<+\infty.

Here
A^{\prime}_{*}
is independent of
u_{1}
as we have showed in (
6.169
) that
A^{\prime}(u_{1})
is uniformly bounded in
u_{1}
by
C_{13}
.

Lemma 6.6
.

For any
u_{1}\in(u_{0},v_{*})
, if
\mu
satisfies

(6.191)

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)}\mu\leq\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime}),

then we have

(6.192)

\overline{A^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{1})\leq\max\left\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{17}(u_{0},v_{*}+\Delta v)\right\}

with
C_{17}
defined in (
6.202
).

Proof.

Similar to (
6.114
), we start by defining

(6.193)

\overline{D^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{0}):=\sup_{\{u_{0}\}\times[v_{*},v_{*}+\xi]}(r^{\delta}|\alpha^{\prime}|).

Then, repeating the same derivations for (
6.4
) and (
6.115
), but instead with
u_{1}\in(u_{0},v_{*})
and
v\in[v_{*},v_{*}+\xi]
, we get

(6.194)

|\alpha^{\prime}|(u,v)\leq\overline{D^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{0})r(u_{0},v)^{-\delta}+\sum_{i=1}^{11}|I_{i}|(u,u_{0},v).

In (
6.194
), the expressions for
I_{i}
remains unchanged as in (
6.118
) to (
6.128
). Now, we will obtain an upper bound for each of the terms
|I_{1}|
to
|I_{11}|
as in the previous subsections. A few key differences are listed below.

For
\mu
, we apply (
6.189
) and replace each
\varepsilon
in the estimates obtained above by

(6.195)

\tilde{\varepsilon}:=\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime}).

This is justified since instead of
\mu\leq\varepsilon
, we now have
\mu\leq\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime})
. Hence, in the region
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)
as the supremum of the quantity
r^{\delta}|\alpha^{\prime}|
is bounded above by the maximum of its supremum over
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})
and its supremum over
U_{\xi}(u_{1})
(see Figure
10
), we obtain

(6.196)

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)}(r^{\delta}|\alpha^{\prime}|)\leq\max\{A^{\prime}(u_{1}),\overline{A^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{1})\}\leq\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},\overline{A^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{1})\}.

For the remaining terms, an analogous logic to the previous sections applies with a slight modification demonstrated as follows. For
(u,v)\in U_{\xi}(u_{1})
, we estimate
\alpha^{\prime}
over the entire region
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)
to derive relevant estimates for
\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)
,
|Q|
,
|Q\phi|
, and consequently
\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}
,
\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}
,
|\phi|^{2}
. Thus, by (
6.196
), we can replace
A^{\prime}(u_{1})
from (
6.131
) to (
6.37
) by
\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},\overline{A^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{1})\}
.

With above pointers, we then multiply
r^{\delta}
on both sides of (
6.194
) and take supremum over
U_{\xi}(u_{1})
to obtain

(6.197)

\displaystyle\overline{A^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\overline{D^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{0})+\left(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{10}C_{9,i}(v_{*}+\xi)}{\delta(1-\delta)}\tilde{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}}+C_{11,9}(u_{0};v_{*}+\xi)\right)\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},\overline{A^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{1})\}+\frac{C_{10,11}(v_{*}+\xi)}{\delta(1-\delta)}\tilde{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{D^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{0})+\left(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{10}C_{9,i}(v_{*}+\Delta v)}{\delta(1-\delta)}\tilde{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}}+C_{11,9}(u_{0};v_{*}+\Delta v)\right)\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},\overline{A^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{1})\}+\frac{C_{10,11}(v_{*}+\Delta v)}{\delta(1-\delta)}\tilde{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}},

where we use relevant estimates in the previous subsections, subject to the remarks above. Moreover, as explained in the previous subsections, the constants derived are non-decreasing with increasing
v_{*}
, which thus allows us to obtain the
\xi-
independent estimates for the constants
C_{9,i},C_{10,11},C_{11,9}
. By further refining
\Delta v>0
from the start, we could also assume that

(6.198)

C_{11,9}(u_{0},v_{*}+\Delta v)<\frac{1}{4},

which is analogous to (
6.165
). Note that inequality (
6.197
) bears resemblance to (
6.163
).

With a similar choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
as in (
6.168
) but with constants evaluated at
(u_{0},v_{*}+\Delta v)
, we then deduce that

(6.199)

\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\left(\frac{\delta(1-\delta)}{\sum_{i=1}^{10}C_{9,i}(u_{0},v_{*}+\Delta v)}\right)^{2}.

This implies that the counterpart of (
6.164
) is still true, if we replace
\varepsilon
by
\tilde{\varepsilon}
and it satisfies

(6.200)

\tilde{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime})<\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\left(\frac{\delta(1-\delta)}{\sum_{i=1}^{10}C_{9,i}(u_{0},v_{*}+\xi)}\right)^{2}.

This implies

(6.201)

\frac{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{10}C_{9,i}(u_{0},v_{*}+\xi)\right)^{2}}{\delta^{2}(1-\delta)^{2}}<\frac{1}{4}.

Parallel to (
6.169
), we also define

(6.202)

C_{17}(u_{0},v_{*}+\Delta v):=2\left(\overline{D^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{0})+C_{12}(v_{*}+\Delta v)\right).

By considering two separate cases
A_{*}^{\prime}>\overline{A^{\prime}_{\xi}(u_{1})}
and
A_{*}^{\prime}\leq\overline{A^{\prime}_{\xi}(u_{1})}
, following a similar argument as in the previous subsections, analogous to (
6.169
), we then deduce that (
6.192
) holds. This concludes the proof of this lemma. ∎

We will also equip ourselves with an elementary lemma as follows:

Lemma 6.7
.

For any
0<y\leq x
and
0\leq p\leq 1
, we have

(6.203)

x^{p}-y^{p}\leq(x-y)^{p}.

Proof.

Dividing
y^{p}
throughout (
6.203
), it is equivalent to show that, for any
r\geq 1
, we have

(6.204)

r^{p}-1\leq(r-1)^{p}.

Let
f(r)=r^{p}-1-(r-1)^{p}
. For
r\geq 1
, we see that
f(1)=0
and
f^{\prime}(r)=pr^{p-1}\left(1-\left(1-\frac{1}{r}\right)^{p-1}\right)\leq 0
. This implies that
f
is a non-increasing function of
r
since
1\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{r}\right)^{p-1}
for
p\in[0,1]
. Thus, we prove
f(r)\leq f(1)=0
for all
r\geq 1
and conclude.
∎

With the above two lemmas, we proceed back on track to prove that the
C^{1}
solution extends beyond the domain of dependence
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*})
. Similar to
[
19
]
, we first define

(6.205)

u_{2}=\sup\left\{u_{1}\in(u_{0},v_{*}):\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)}\mu\leq\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime})\right\}.

By the definition of
u_{2}
, we then have either
u_{2}=v_{*}
or

(6.206)

\sup_{U_{\xi}(u_{2})}\mu=\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime})

is attained at some
u_{2}\in[u_{0},v_{*})
. We then show that for
\xi
sufficiently small, the latter will not happen.

Suppose for a contradiction that the latter happens. Then, we set
u_{1}=u_{2}
as in the hypothesis of Lemma
6.6
. Consequently, by (
6.206
),
\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{2},v_{*}+\xi)}\mu
satisfies (
6.191
) and hence by Lemma
6.6
, we have that (
6.192
) holds. We then estimate
\mu
in the region
U_{\xi}(u_{2})
to obtain a contradiction with sufficiently small
\xi
. As described in the proof of Lemma
6.6
, in the region
U_{\xi}(u_{2})
, we have

(6.207)

\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v)\leq\frac{r^{1-\delta}(u,v)}{2-\delta}\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{12}\}

with the argument
(u_{0},v_{*}+\Delta v)
suppressed when the context is clear. We can then estimate
\mu
in
U_{\xi}(u_{2})
as follows. From (
6.28
) we first observe that

(6.208)

\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}(u,v)\leq\mu(u,v)C_{6}^{2}(v_{*}+\Delta v)r^{1-\chi}(u,v).

By further requiring
u_{0}
to be sufficiently close to
v_{*}
, we can then demand that

(6.209)

C_{6}^{2}(v_{*})r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\chi}{2}}(u_{0},v_{*})\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Hence, from the expression of
C_{6}
in (
6.10
), we see that the mapping
v\mapsto C_{6}^{2}(v)r^{1-\chi}(u_{0},v)
is continuous. This allows us to refine
\Delta v
to be sufficiently small (depending on the given
u_{0}
) such that

(6.210)

C_{6}(v_{*}+\Delta)r^{1-\chi}(u_{0},v_{*}+\Delta)\leq\frac{7}{8},

and consequently,

(6.211)

\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}(u,v)\leq\frac{7}{8}\mu(u,v).

This in turn implies that

(6.212)

\left(\mu-\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)(u,v)\geq\frac{\mu}{8}(u,v)\geq 0

for all
(u,v)\in U_{\xi}(u_{2})
.
With this and the help of (
1.28
), we move on to estimate
\mu
directly via calculating

(6.213)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\mu

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi r(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\left(\mu-\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)\leq\frac{4\pi r(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}.

In (
6.213
), the inequality follows from the fact that
\partial_{v}r>0
while
\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}\geq 0
.
For
(u,v)\in U_{\xi}(u_{2})
, we further perform integration and apply (
6.207
) and (
6.213
) to obtain

(6.214)

\displaystyle\mu(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\mu(u,v_{*})+4\pi\int^{v}_{v_{*}}\frac{(1-\mu)}{r}\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\mu(u,v_{*})+\frac{4\pi(\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{17}\})^{2}}{(2-\delta)^{2}}\int^{v}_{v_{*}}r^{1-2\delta}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mu(u,v_{*})+\frac{4\pi(\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{17}\})^{2}}{2(2-\delta)^{2}(1-\delta)}\left(r^{2-2\delta}(u,v)-r^{2-2\delta}(u,v_{*})\right).

On the other hand, since
\partial_{v}r
is
C^{1}
for
(u,v)\in U_{\xi}(u_{2})
, we have
\partial_{v}r(u_{0},v)
is
C^{1}
on
[v_{*},v_{*}+\Delta v]
. Thus, there exists
C_{18}(u_{0})>0
, such that
\partial_{v}r(u_{0},v)\leq C_{18}(u_{0})
for all
v\in[v_{*},v_{*}+\Delta v]
. Hence, for
(u,v)\in U_{\xi}(u_{2})
, using the fact that
\partial_{v}\partial_{u}r\leq 0
as described above, we obtain

(6.215)

\displaystyle r(u,v)-r(u,v_{*})

\displaystyle=r(u_{0},v)-r(u_{0},v_{*})+\int^{u}_{u_{0}}\int^{v}_{v_{*}}\partial_{v}\partial_{u}r(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq r(u_{0},v)-r(u_{0},v_{*})=\int_{v_{*}}^{v}\partial_{v}r(u_{0},v^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C_{18}(u_{0})(v-v_{*})\leq C_{18}\xi.

Now, for
0<\delta<\frac{1}{2}
and hence
2-2\delta>1
, we employ (
6.214
) and (
6.215
) to obtain

(6.216)

\displaystyle\mu(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\mu(u,v_{*})+\frac{4\pi(\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{17}\})^{2}}{2(2-\delta)^{2}(1-\delta)}(r^{2-2\delta}(u,v)-r^{2-2\delta}(u,v_{*}))

\displaystyle\leq\mu(u,v_{*})+\frac{4\pi(\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{17}\})^{2}}{2(2-\delta)^{2}(1-\delta)}r^{2-2\delta-1}(u,v_{*}+\Delta v)(r(u,v)-r(u,v_{*}))

\displaystyle\leq\mu(u,v_{*})+\frac{4\pi(\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{17}\})^{2}C_{18}}{2(2-\delta)^{2}(1-\delta)}r^{2-2\delta-1}(u_{0},v_{*}+\Delta v)\xi.

Since
\mu(u,v_{*})\leq\varepsilon
, to obtain
\mu(u,v)<\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime})
, it suffices to pick
\xi>0
sufficiently small, such that

(6.217)

\frac{4\pi(\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{17}\})^{2}C_{18}}{2(2-\delta)^{2}(1-\delta)}r^{2-2\delta-1}(u_{0},v_{*}+\Delta v)\xi<\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon).

In contrast, for the case
\delta\geq\frac{1}{2}
and hence
2-2\delta\leq 1
, we can apply Lemma
6.7
and (
6.214
) to obtain

(6.218)

\displaystyle\mu(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\mu(u,v_{*})+\frac{4\pi(\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{17}\})^{2}}{2(2-\delta)^{2}(1-\delta)}\left(r^{2-2\delta}(u,v)-r^{2-2\delta}(u,v_{*})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mu(u,v_{*})+\frac{4\pi(\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{17}\})^{2}}{2(2-\delta)^{2}(1-\delta)}\left(r(u,v)-r(u,v_{*})\right)^{2-2\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\mu(u,v_{*})+\frac{4\pi(\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{17}\})^{2}C_{18}^{2-2\delta}}{2(2-\delta)^{2}(1-\delta)}\xi^{2-2\delta}.

Similarly to the former case, it suffices to pick
\xi>0
sufficiently small such that

(6.219)

\frac{4\pi(\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{17}\})^{2}C_{18}^{2-2\delta}}{2(2-\delta)^{2}(1-\delta)}\xi^{2-2\delta}<\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon).

Combining (
6.217
) and (
6.219
), while requiring
\xi
to be less than or equals to
\Delta v
, a valid choice of
\xi
would be

(6.220)

\xi=\min\left\{\Delta v,\frac{(2-\delta)^{2}(1-\delta)(\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon)}{8\pi(\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{17}\})^{2}C_{18}r^{2-2\delta-1}(u_{0},v_{*}+\xi_{1})},\left(\frac{(2-\delta)^{2}(1-\delta)(\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon)}{8\pi(\max\{A^{\prime}_{*},C_{17}\})^{2}C_{18}^{2-2\delta}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2-2\delta}}\right\}.

Therefore, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, such a choice
\xi
would imply that

(6.221)

\sup_{U_{\xi}(u_{2})}\mu<\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime}),

contradicting (
6.206
).

In summary, the above arguments show that
u_{2}
in (
6.205
) is achieved at
u_{2}=v_{*}
, and hence the bound in (
6.205
) continues to hold. This implies that

(6.222)

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},v_{*},v_{*}+\xi)}\mu\leq\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime}).

Thus, for each
u_{1}\in(u_{0},v_{*})
, the hypothesis of Lemma
6.6
is verified. This then implies that (
6.192
) holds. Observe that (
6.192
) further implies that
\overline{A^{\prime}}(u_{1}):=\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*}+\Delta v)}(r^{\delta}|\alpha^{\prime}|)
remains bounded as
u_{1}\rightarrow v_{*}
. By conducting a similar argument as in previous subsections, we can also obtain improved estimates for
|I_{1}|
to
|I_{11}|
. Henceforth, we can deduce that the solution extends as a
C^{1}
solution to the closure of
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},v_{*},v_{*}+\xi)
. In particular, with
C^{1}
initial data along
\{v_{*}\}\times[v_{*},v_{*}+\xi]
, we show that the solution further extends as a
C^{1}
solution to
\mathcal{D}(v_{*},v_{*}+\xi^{\prime})
for some
\xi^{\prime}\in(0,\xi]
. This concludes the proof of Theorem
6.2
. ∎

7.
First Instability Theorem

As previously described, we consider the following picture. We mark
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}=(v_{0},v_{0})
along
\Gamma
as the start of the singular boundary
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{J}^{-}(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})\subset\mathcal{R}
. Denote
C_{0}^{-}
to be the past-directed incoming null curve emitting from
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
. For the remaining parts of this section, we shall focus on
\mathcal{Q}\setminus\mathcal{J}^{-}(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
lying in the future of both
C_{0}^{+}
and
C_{0}^{-}
. Figure
11
illustrates the above set-up.

\mathcal{O}
\Gamma
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
\mathcal{J}^{-}(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
C_{0}^{+}
\mathcal{B}_{0}
\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}
v_{0}
\mathcal{Q}\setminus\mathcal{J}^{-}(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})

Figure 11.
Diagram for this section. The shaded region corresponds to the region
\mathcal{Q}\setminus\mathcal{J}^{-}(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
, while the unshaded region corresponds to the region
\mathcal{J}^{-}(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
.

Recall the quantity
\gamma
from (
1.33
) below:

(7.1)

\gamma(u,v):=\int^{u}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

For notational simplicity, we denote

(7.2)

\gamma_{0}(u):=\gamma(u,v_{0}).

In lieu of estimates that we will establish, we also define the truncated gamma
\tilde{\gamma}
, with
\overline{u}\in[0,v_{0})
as a parameter, as below

(7.3)

\tilde{\gamma}(u,v):=\gamma(u,v;\overline{u})=\int^{u}_{\overline{u}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

For brevity, the dependence on
\overline{u}
will be dropped if the given context is clear. In addition, similar to Section
4
, we use the subscript
()_{1}
to indicate that a given quantity is evaluated along
C_{0}^{-}
.

We also use
g(u,v)
and
f(u,v)
to denote the following functions

(7.4)

g(u,v):=D_{u}\phi(u,v)+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}(u,v),

(7.5)

f(u,v):=\int^{u}_{0}-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}(u^{\prime},v)+\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

With them, we define

(7.6)

I(u):=\int^{u}_{0}g_{0}(u^{\prime})e^{-f_{0}(u^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

We are now ready to state the main theorem as follows.

Theorem 7.1
.

(First Instability Theorem.)
Let
\gamma_{0}(u)
defined in (
7.2
) be unbounded as
u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
. Suppose that
I
defined in (
7.6
) does not converge to a finite limit as
u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
or otherwise

(7.7)

\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)\neq\lim_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}I(u).

Then, we can conclude that

(1)

\mathcal{A}
is non-empty,

and

(2)

Either one of the following two scenarios holds:

(a)

\mathcal{O}^{\prime}=\mathcal{B}_{0}
(and thus both
\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}
and
\mathcal{A}
issue from
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
), or

(b)

\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\subsetneq\mathcal{B}_{0}
and for any sufficiently small open neighborhood
U
of
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
with
U\subset\mathcal{Q}\setminus\mathcal{J}^{-}(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
, we have
U\cap\mathcal{A}\neq\emptyset
.

Before we begin with the proof of the theorem, we first establish several useful lemmas.

Lemma 7.2
.

For the spherically symmetric Einstein-Maxwell-charged scalar field system, if
\mathcal{A}=\emptyset
, then we have
\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}=\emptyset
and
\mathcal{B}_{0}
is an outgoing null segment originating from
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
and extends all the way to
v\rightarrow\infty
.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.1
, under the assumption of this lemma, we have that
\mathcal{A}\cup\mathcal{T}=\emptyset
.
For any potential point
p\in\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}
, we have
r(p)=0
. From
\mathcal{A}\cup\mathcal{T}=\emptyset
, we also get
\partial_{v}r>0
everywhere. Henceforth, any outgoing null curve originating from
\Gamma
will never hit
\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}
.
∎

Lemma 7.3
.

Along any incoming null curve with
v\geq v_{0}
, for all
0\leq u<v_{0}
, if
(u^{\prime},v)\in\mathcal{R}
for all
u^{\prime}\in[0,u]
, then we have

(7.8)

(\partial_{v}r)(u,v)=(\partial_{v}r)(0,v)e^{-\gamma(u,v)}.

In particular, along
v=v_{0}
, we deduce

(7.9)

(\partial_{v}r)_{0}(u)=(\partial_{v}r)_{0}(0)e^{-\gamma_{0}(u)}

Furthermore, for any
\overline{u}\in[0,v_{0})
, with
\tilde{\gamma}
defined in (
7.3
), we also get

(7.10)

(\partial_{v}r)(u,v)=(\partial_{v}r)(\overline{u},v)e^{-\tilde{\gamma}(u.v)}.

Proof.

Using (
1.19
), we can rearrange its expression to obtain

(7.11)

\partial_{u}\log\left(\partial_{v}r\right)=\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right).

Integrating (
7.11
), we retrieve (
7.8
), (
7.9
) and (
7.10
).
∎

Lemma 7.4
.

Along any incoming null curve with
v\geq v_{0}
, for all
0\leq\overline{u}\leq u<v_{0}
, if
(u^{\prime},v)\in\mathcal{R}
for all
u^{\prime}\in[\overline{u},u]
, we have

(7.12)

\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}\leq\frac{1}{1-\mu(\overline{u},v)}e^{\tilde{\gamma}(u,v)}.

In particular, we also get

(7.13)

\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}\leq\frac{1}{1-\mu(0,v)}e^{\gamma(u,v)}.

Proof.

With the help of (
1.27
), we compute

\partial_{u}\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}\right)=\frac{\partial_{u}\mu}{(1-\mu)^{2}}=\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}\left(-\frac{4\pi r|D_{u}\phi|^{2}}{(-\partial_{u}r)}+\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right).

This gives

(7.14)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\mu(\overline{u},v)}\exp\left({\int^{u}_{\overline{u}}\left(-\frac{4\pi r|D_{u}\phi|^{2}}{(-\partial_{u}r)}+\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\right).

Since
\partial_{u}r<0
, this implies

(7.15)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\mu(\overline{u},v)}\exp\left({\int^{u}_{\overline{u}}\left(\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\right).

Employing the definition of
\gamma
in (
7.1
) yields (
7.12
) and (
7.13
).
∎

Observe that these two lemmas were established when we were proving Theorem
6.1
, albeit only for
\overline{u}=0
.

Next, we remark that the definition of
\gamma
in (
7.1
) naturally arises from the evolution equation for
\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}
along the
u
-direction. To see this, by (
1.19
) and (
1.24
), we get

(7.16)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial_{u}(r\partial_{v}\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}+\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi(-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r)}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{-\partial_{v}rD_{u}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r(1-\mu)}}{\partial_{v}r}-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}+\frac{\partial_{v}\phi(-\partial_{u}r)}{(\partial_{v}r)}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}

\displaystyle=-D_{u}\phi-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}+\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\left(-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}+\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right).

Using the definition of
f
and
g
in (
7.4
) and (
7.5
), we obtain

(7.17)

\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u,v)=\left(\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(0,v)-\int^{u}_{0}g(u^{\prime},v)e^{-f(u^{\prime},v)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\right)e^{f(u,v)}

Taking absolute values, we arrive at

(7.18)

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{|\partial_{v}r|}\right)(u,v)=\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(0,v)-\int^{u}_{0}g(u^{\prime},v)e^{-f(u^{\prime},v)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\right|e^{\gamma(u,v)}.

Employing
(\partial_{v}r)_{0}>0
in lieu of Lemma
7.3
, we then derive

(7.19)

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u)=\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-I(u)\right|e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}

with
I(u)
defined in (
7.6
).

Last but not least, we have the following lemma

Lemma 7.5
.

Along
v=v_{0}
, as
u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
, we have that

(7.20)

\gamma_{0}(u)<+\infty\quad\mbox{ implies }\quad\mu_{0}(u)\rightarrow 0.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.3
, with
\overline{u}=0
and
\chi=\frac{1}{2}
, inequality (
3.8
) implies that

(7.21)

\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)_{0}(u)\leq C_{1}^{2}\left(0,v_{0};\frac{1}{2}\right)r_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(0)v_{0}r_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(u)\mu_{0}(u).

We then pick
u_{1}\in[0,v_{0})
with
u_{1}
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
, such that
C_{1}^{2}\left(0,v_{0};\frac{1}{2}\right)r_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(0)v_{0}r_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(u)\leq\frac{1}{2}
for all
u\in[u_{1},v_{0})
. This simplifies (
7.21
) to

(7.22)

0\leq\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)_{0}(u)\leq\frac{\mu_{0}(u)}{2}.

Recalling the definition of
\gamma_{0}
in (
7.2
), with a fixed
u_{2}\in[u_{1},v_{0})
, for all
u\in[u_{2},v_{0})
, we have

(7.23)

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}(u)-\gamma_{0}(u_{2})

\displaystyle=\int^{u}_{u_{2}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)_{0}}{r_{0}}\frac{(\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2})_{0}}{1-\mu_{0}}(u^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\geq\frac{1}{2}\int^{u}_{u_{2}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)_{0}}{r_{0}}\frac{\mu_{0}}{1-\mu_{0}}(u^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\int^{u}_{u_{2}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)_{0}(u^{\prime})}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\frac{\frac{2m_{0}(u)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}}{1-\frac{2m_{0}(u)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}}\;\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})-2m_{0}(u)}\right)|^{u^{\prime}=u}_{u^{\prime}=u_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{1-\mu_{0}(u)\frac{r_{0}(u)}{r_{0}(u_{2})}}{1-\mu_{0}(u)}\right).

This in turn implies that

(7.24)

\displaystyle e^{2(\gamma_{0}(u)-\gamma_{0}(u_{2}))}-1

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mu_{0}(u)}{1-\mu_{0}(u)}\left(1-\frac{r_{0}(u)}{r_{0}(u_{2})}\right)

and hence

(7.25)

0\leq\mu_{0}(u)\leq\frac{e^{2\left(\gamma_{0}(u)-\gamma_{0}(u_{2})\right)}-1}{1-\frac{r_{0}(u)}{r_{0}(u_{2})}}.

Since
r_{0}(u)\rightarrow 0
as
u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
, for each
u_{2}
, we can select
u(u_{2})>u_{2}
such that
r_{0}(u)/r_{0}(u_{2})=1/2
. Furthermore, by the continuity of
r_{0}
, we have that
u_{2}\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
implies
u(u_{2})\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
. In addition, as
\gamma_{0}
is non-decreasing with respect to
u
and is bounded by hypothesis, this implies that

\lim_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}\gamma_{0}(u)
exists and thus

(7.26)

\lim_{u_{2}\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}\left(\gamma_{0}(u(u_{2}))-\gamma_{0}(u_{2})\right)=0.

By (
7.25
), it in turn yields that

(7.27)

\lim_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}\mu_{0}(u)=0.

∎

In below we will see that the blueshift effect in the regular region would trigger our established trapped surface formation criterion. We now start to prove Theorem
7.1
.

Proof.

Suppose that the conclusion in the statement of Theorem
7.1
is not true. Then either
\mathcal{A}
is empty or there exists a sufficiently small open neighborhood
U
of
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
(with
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\subsetneq\mathcal{B}_{0}
) such that
U\cap\mathcal{A}=\emptyset
.

If the former holds, by Lemma
7.2
, we deduce that
\mathcal{B}_{0}
is an outgoing null segment originating from
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
and extends all the way to
v\rightarrow\infty
. In particular, there exists an
\varepsilon>0
and a corresponding outgoing null segment
\{v_{0}\}\times[v_{0},v_{0}+\varepsilon]
in
\mathcal{B}_{0}
. This enables us to find
u_{1}\in(0,v_{0})
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
, such that the coordinate patch
([u_{1},v_{0}]\times[v_{0},v_{0}+\varepsilon])
satisfies
([u_{1},v_{0}]\times[v_{0},v_{0}+\varepsilon])\cap\mathcal{A}=\emptyset
.

If the latter holds, by an analogous argument, we can also find an
\varepsilon>0
and a
u_{1}\in(0,v_{0})
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
such that
([u_{1},v_{0})\times[v_{0},v_{0}+\varepsilon])\cap\mathcal{A}=\emptyset
. Hence, for both cases, we define this coordinate patch to be
\mathcal{P}=[u_{1},v_{0})\times[v_{0},v_{0}+\varepsilon]
and
\mathcal{P}\cap\mathcal{A}=\emptyset
.
Figure
12
below illustrates the existence of
\mathcal{P}
in both cases. In view of Proposition
2.1
, we deduce that
\mathcal{P}\subset\mathcal{R}
. This allows us to carry out the estimates and lemmas established as above.

\mathcal{O}
\Gamma
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
C_{0}^{+}
\mathcal{B}_{0}
v_{0}
\mathcal{P}
u=u_{1}
v_{0}+\varepsilon
\mathcal{O}
\Gamma
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
C_{0}^{+}
\mathcal{B}_{0}
\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}
\mathcal{A}
v_{0}
\mathcal{P}
u=u_{1}
v_{0}+\varepsilon

Figure 12.
The existence of the coordinate patch
\mathcal{P}
in both cases.

Similar to (
4.2
), for
(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}
, we first define
\delta(u,v)
and
\eta^{*}(u,v)
as

(7.28)

\delta(u,v):=\frac{r(u,v)-r(u,v_{0})}{r(u,v_{0})},

(7.29)

\eta^{*}(u,v):=\frac{2\left(m(u,v)-m(u,v_{0})\right)}{r(u,v)}-\frac{2}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{Q^{2}(\partial_{v}r)}{2r^{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

By Theorem
4.1
, with
u_{1}
taking the role of
\overline{u}
, considering
v_{3}
as
v_{0}+\varepsilon
, regarding
v_{1}
as
v_{0}
, and treating
(u_{0},v_{2})
as
(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}
, with
\delta(u,v)<1
we then have

(7.30)

\eta^{*}(u,v)\leq C_{24}(\chi_{1})\frac{\delta(u,v)}{1+\delta(u,v)}\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta(u,v)}\right)+C_{23}(\chi_{1})\frac{\delta(u,v)r(u,v_{0})}{1-\sup_{{v\in[v_{0},v_{0}+\varepsilon]}}\mu(u,v)}

for all
(u,v)\in\widehat{\mathcal{P}}
with
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}
being a subset of
\mathcal{P}
. And via (
4.7
),
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}\subset\mathcal{P}
is defined as

(7.31)

\widehat{\mathcal{P}}:=\left\{(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}:v-u\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}^{2}(u_{1},v_{0}+\varepsilon;\chi_{1})r(u_{1},v_{0}+\varepsilon)}\right\}.

Here we choose the parameter
\chi_{1}\in\left(0,\frac{1}{3}\right]
and note that the constants
C_{24}
and
C_{23}
are the only terms depending on
\chi_{1}
. For simplicity, we can pick
\chi_{1}=\frac{1}{3}
and denote

(7.32)

C_{31}:=C_{1}^{2}\left(u_{1},v_{0}+\varepsilon;\frac{1}{3}\right)\cdot r(u_{1},v_{0}+\varepsilon).

As we have seen in previous sections, obtaining the correct sign for the function
\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}
is crucial. This motivates a further restriction on
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}
. Fix a parameter
\xi\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right)
and choose
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
as

(7.33)

\tilde{\mathcal{P}}:=\left\{(u,v)\in\mathcal{P}:v-v_{0}\leq\frac{\xi}{2C_{31}},u-v_{0}\leq\frac{\xi}{2C_{31}}\right\}.

Recall that by Proposition
3.3
via (
3.8
) we have

(7.34)

\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}(u,v)\leq C_{1}^{2}\left(u_{1},v_{0}+\varepsilon;\frac{1}{3}\right)\cdot r(u_{1},v_{0}+\varepsilon)\cdot(v-u)\cdot\mu(u,v).

Consequently, for all
(u,v)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
we obtain

(7.35)

\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}(u,v)\leq\xi\mu(u,v)\leq\xi.

In particular, we have

(7.36)

\left(\mu-\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)(u,v)\geq(1-\xi)\mu(u,v)\geq\frac{\mu(u,v)}{2}\geq 0.

Motivated from (
7.33
), we also define
\tilde{\varepsilon}:=\frac{\xi}{2C_{31}}
representing the maximal width of
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
.

Within
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
we then find a further refinement
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
. And the following lemma holds.

Lemma 7.6
.

Along
v=v_{0}
, we define

(7.37)

\gamma_{s}(u):=\int^{u}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

and

(7.38)

\gamma_{s,0}(u):=\gamma_{s}(u,v_{0}).

For each
\xi\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right)
, there exists
u_{2}\in[u_{1},v_{0})
and in the corresponding refinement

(7.39)

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=[u_{2},v_{0})\times[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]

of
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
, for any
u\in[u_{2},v_{0})
, we have

(7.40)

\gamma_{0}(u)\leq\gamma_{s,0}(u)\leq(1+2\xi)\gamma_{0}(u).

Remark 13
.

The
s
in the subscript of
\gamma_{s,0}(u)
represents the corresponding
\gamma
defined for the uncharged scalar field system. One can compare this to our definition of
\gamma
in (
7.1
), for which the term
Q^{2}/r^{2}
is dropped in (
7.37
).

Proof.

To prove (
7.40
), we first note that for any choice of
u_{2}\in[u_{1},v_{0})
, inequality
\gamma_{0}(u)\leq\gamma_{s,0}(u)
follows immediately from definitions in (
7.1
) and (
7.37
). For the upper bound of
\gamma_{s,0}(u)
, under the assumption of Theorem
7.1
, we have
\gamma_{0}(u)\rightarrow\infty
as
u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
. This signals the existence of
u_{2}^{\prime}\in[u_{1},v_{0})
satisfying
0<\gamma_{0}(u_{2}^{\prime})\leq\gamma_{s,0}(u_{2}^{\prime})<\infty
. Here the finiteness of
\gamma_{s,0}(u_{2}^{\prime})
comes from the fact that the only possible singularity for the integrand of
\gamma_{s,0}
occurs at
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
. On the other hand, by (
7.36
), we note that

(7.41)

\int_{u_{2}^{\prime}}^{u}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\int_{u_{2}^{\prime}}^{u}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{1-\xi}\int_{u_{2}^{\prime}}^{u}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Consequently, as
u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
, since

(7.42)

\int_{u_{2}^{\prime}}^{u}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty,

then we have

(7.43)

\int^{u}_{{u_{2}}^{\prime}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty.

We then decompose
\gamma_{s,0}(u)
as

(7.44)

\gamma_{s,0}(u)=\int^{u}_{u_{2}^{\prime}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\int^{u_{2}^{\prime}}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Observe that, for a fixed
u_{2}^{\prime}
, the second integral is finite for a fixed
u_{2}^{\prime}
. By (
7.41
), we further derive

(7.45)

\gamma_{s,0}(u)\leq\frac{1}{1-\xi}\int^{u}_{u_{2}^{\prime}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\int^{u_{2}^{\prime}}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Since
\xi\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right)
, from (
7.42
), if we pick
u_{2}\in[u_{2}^{\prime},v_{0})
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
such that

(7.46)

\frac{\xi(1-2\xi)}{1-\xi}\int^{u_{2}}_{u_{2}^{\prime}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\geq\int^{u_{2}^{\prime}}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

then from (
7.45
), for all
u\in[u_{2},v_{0})
, we have

(7.47)

\displaystyle\gamma_{s,0}(u)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\xi}\int^{u}_{u_{2}^{\prime}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\int^{u_{2}^{\prime}}_{0}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\xi}\int^{u}_{u_{2}^{\prime}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\frac{\xi(1-2\xi)}{1-\xi}\int^{u_{2}}_{u_{2}^{\prime}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\xi}\int^{u}_{u_{2}^{\prime}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\frac{\xi(1-2\xi)}{1-\xi}\int^{u}_{u_{2}^{\prime}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(1+2\xi)\int^{u}_{u_{2}^{\prime}}\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v_{0})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq(1+2\xi)\gamma_{0}(u),

where the last inequality follows from
\gamma_{0}(u_{2}^{\prime})>0
.
∎

We continue the proof of Theorem
7.1
. Recalling the definition of
I
in (
7.6
), since
I
is complex-valued, we can decompose it into

(7.48)

I(u)=X(u)+\mathrm{i}Y(u)

with
X(u)=\re[I(u)]
and
Y(u)=\im[I(u)]
. Let us further define the following:

(7.49)

\displaystyle X_{-}:=\liminf_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}X(u),

\displaystyle X_{+}:=\limsup_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}X(u),

\displaystyle Y_{-}:=\liminf_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}Y(u),

\displaystyle Y_{+}:=\limsup_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}Y(u).

A priori
, as the asymptotic behavior of
I
is unknown, similar to
[
21
]
for the uncharged case (dealing with only
X
), here we will have to consider all possible behaviors for
X
and
Y
. For each separate
X
or
Y
, denoted by
Z
, we have the following cases

(1)

-\infty<Z_{-}=Z_{+}<\infty
(limit exists and is finite),

(2)

-\infty=Z_{-}=Z_{+}
(limit exists and is equals to
-\infty
),

(3)

Z_{-}=Z_{+}=\infty
(limit exists and is equals to
+\infty
),

(4)

-\infty<Z_{-}<Z_{+}<\infty
(limit does not exist, but the sequence is bounded),

(5)

-\infty<Z_{-}<Z_{+}=\infty
(limit does not exist),

(6)

-\infty=Z_{-}<Z_{+}<\infty
(limit does not exist),

(7)

-\infty=Z_{-}<Z_{+}=\infty
(limit does not exist).

For brevity, we denote case
(a,b)
to refer to the case where
a
corresponds to one of the seven scenarios as listed above for
X
, while
b
corresponds to that for
Y
. For instance, case
(1,6)
corresponds to the scenario, whereby the limit of
X(u)
exists as
u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
, while the limit for
Y(u)
does not exist, with
Y_{-}=-\infty
and
Y_{+}<\infty
.

Analogous to
[
21
]
with 7 cases there, in this paper we characterize our
49
cases into the following dichotomy

(I)

I(u_{n})
is bounded for all possible sequences
\{u_{n}\}_{n}
approaching to
v_{0}^{-}
. These correspond to cases
(1,1),(1,4),(4,1),(4,4)
.

(II)

There exists a sequence
\{u_{n}\}_{n}
such that
|I(u_{n})|\rightarrow\infty
as
u_{n}\rightarrow v_{0}
. These correspond to the remaining
45
cases.

For
Scenario (I)
, we proceed as follows. For case
(1,1)
, since the limit for
X
and
Y
both exist, we deduce that the limit for the complex-valued
I
also exists. By the hypothesis (
7.7
), we can set

(7.50)

h:=\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-l\right|\neq 0

with

(7.51)

l=\lim_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}I(u).

For
u_{*}
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
and for all
u\in[u_{*},v_{0})
, we then have

(7.52)

|I(u)-l|\leq\frac{2h}{3}.

Hence, for each
u\in[u_{*},v_{0})
, by applying (
7.19
), we have

(7.53)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u)=

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-l+l-I(u)\right|e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-l\right|-|l-I(u)|\right|e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\geq\frac{h}{3}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}.

For cases
(1,4),(4,1),(4,4)
, we denote

(7.54)

h_{X}:=X_{+}-X_{-}\quad\text{ and }\quad h_{Y}:=Y_{+}-Y_{-}.

By the definitions of
\limsup
and
\liminf
, we must have the following

(7.55)

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left|\re\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-X_{-}\right|,\left|\re\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-X_{+}\right|\right\}\geq\frac{h_{X}}{2},

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left|\im\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-Y_{-}\right|,\left|\im\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-Y_{+}\right|\right\}\geq\frac{h_{Y}}{2}.

For each case, we denote
X_{*}
and
Y_{*}
to be the quantities (
X_{*}=X_{+}
or
X_{-}
and
Y_{*}=Y_{+}
or
Y_{-}
) that the maximums in (
7.55
) are attained. With those, we can find an increasing sequence
\{u_{n}\}
with
u_{n}\rightarrow v_{0}
, and for
u_{n}
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
we have

(7.56)

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-I(u_{n})\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\re\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-X_{*}+X_{*}-X(u_{n})+\mathrm{i}\left(\im\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-Y_{*}+Y_{*}-Y(u_{n})\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left|\re\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-X_{*}+X_{*}-X(u_{n})\right|^{2}+\left|\im\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-Y_{*}+Y_{*}-Y(u_{n})\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left|\left|\re\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-X_{*}\right|-\left|X_{*}-X(u_{n})\right|\right|^{2}+\left|\left|\im\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-Y_{*}\right|-\left|Y_{*}-Y(u_{n})\right|\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left|\left|\re\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-X_{*}\right|-\left|X_{*}-X(u_{n})\right|\right|,\left|\left|\im\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-Y_{*}\right|-\left|Y_{*}-Y(u_{n})\right|\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\max\left\{\frac{h_{X}}{3},\frac{h_{Y}}{3}\right\}=\frac{h}{3}>0.

Note that for the last step in (
7.56
), we use the fact that
h:=\max\{h_{X},h_{Y}\}>0
. This is true since both
h_{X}
and
h_{Y}
cannot be simultaneously zero (otherwise, we fall back to case
(1,1)
). Thus, in the above scenarios, we can find an increasing sequence
\{u_{n}\}
approaching
v_{0}
such that

(7.57)

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u_{n})\geq\frac{h}{3}e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}.

In addition, since
I
is bounded in these cases, by (
7.19
), we have that
\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{-\gamma_{0}(u)}
is bounded. Henceforth, we can define the following quantity

(7.58)

b:=\sup_{u\in[0,v_{0})}\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{-\gamma_{0}(u)}<+\infty.

Given our sequence
u_{n}
as mentioned above, for each
n
, we will pick a corresponding value of
v
(denoted by
v(u_{n})
) such that
(u_{n},v(u_{n}))
is in the refined region and a few geometric inequalities hold. We summarize these into a couple of lemmas.

First, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 7.7
.

Consider a fixed
u\in[u_{2},v_{0})
and recall region
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
in (
7.39
). For
(u,v_{1}),(u,v_{2})\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
with
v_{1}<v_{2}
, we have

(7.59)

\delta(u,v_{1})\leq\delta(u,v_{2}).

Proof.

This follows from the fact that
\partial_{v}r\geq 0
in
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{R}
.
∎

Next, by leveraging on Lemma
7.4
, we have the following improvement

Lemma 7.8
.

Given any sequence
u_{n}\in[u_{2},v_{0})
, for each
n
, suppose that there exists a corresponding value
v(u_{n})\in[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]
such that

(7.60)

\delta\left(u,v(u_{n})\right)\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta(u,v(u_{n}))}\right)\leq C_{33}e^{-2\gamma_{0}(u)}

with
C_{33}
given in (
7.69
) and
\delta(u,v(u_{n}))\leq 1
for all
u\in[u_{2},u_{n}]
.
Then, for all
v\in[v_{0},v(u_{n})]
, we have

(7.61)

\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}\leq\frac{3}{2}\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}(u)}\leq\frac{3}{2}\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}.

Proof.

We prove this lemma via a bootstrapping argument. Consider the following set

(7.62)

S_{1}(u)=\left\{v^{\prime\prime}\in[v_{0},v(u)]:\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v^{\prime})}\leq\frac{3}{2}\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}(u)}\;\text{ for all }v^{\prime}\in[v_{0},v^{\prime\prime}]\right\}.

It is clear that
S_{1}(u)
is closed. Furthermore, by Lemma
7.4
, we have

(7.63)

\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v_{0})}=\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}(u)}\leq\frac{3}{2}\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}(u)}.

This indicates that
v_{0}\in S_{1}(u)
and thus
S_{1}(u)
is non-empty. It remains to show that
S_{1}(u)
is open. To do so, we rewrite
\eta^{*}
in (
7.29
) as

(7.64)

\eta^{*}(u,v)=\mu(u,v)-\mu_{0}(u)\frac{r(u,v_{0})}{r(u,v)}-\frac{2}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{Q^{2}\partial_{v}r}{2r^{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Let
v\in[v_{0},v(u)]
. To obtain an estimate for
\mu(u,v)
, by rearranging the terms in (
7.64
) and by (
7.30
), (
7.35
), we derive

(7.65)

\displaystyle\mu(u,v)\leq

\displaystyle C_{24}\frac{\delta(u,v)}{1+\delta(u,v)}\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta(u,v)}\right)+C_{23}\frac{\delta(u,v)r_{0}(u)}{1-\sup_{{v\in[v_{0},v_{0}+\varepsilon]}}\mu(u,v)}

\displaystyle+\mu_{0}(u)\frac{r(u,v_{0})}{r(u,v)}+\frac{2\xi}{r(u,v_{0})}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{24}\delta\left(u,v(u)\right)\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta\left(u,v(u)\right)}\right)+\frac{3C_{23}}{2}\frac{r_{0}(0)}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta\left(u,v(u)\right)+\mu_{0}(u)+2\xi\delta\left(u,v(u)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{32}\delta\left(u,v(u)\right)\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta\left(u,v(u)\right)}\right)e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}+\mu_{0}(u)

with

(7.66)

\displaystyle C_{32}:=C_{24}+\frac{3C_{23}}{2}\frac{r_{0}(0)}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}+2\xi.

Here we use
\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta(u,v)}\right)\geq 1
,
v\leq v(u)
, inequality (
7.35
) and Lemma
7.7
. We can rewrite (
7.65
) as

(7.67)

\displaystyle 1-\mu(u,v)

\displaystyle\geq 1-C_{32}\delta\left(u,v(u_{n})\right)\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta\left(u,v(u_{n})\right)}\right)e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}-\mu_{0}(u).

If we further demand that

(7.68)

\displaystyle\delta(u,v(u_{n}))\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta(u,v(u_{n}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{33}e^{-2\gamma_{0}(u)},

with

(7.69)

C_{33}:=\frac{1-\mu_{0}(0)}{8C_{32}},

we then get
1-\mu(u,v)\geq\frac{7}{8}(1-\mu_{0}(u))
, which implies

(7.70)

\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}\leq\frac{8}{7}\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}(u)}.

With (
7.70
) we improve the estimate in (
7.62
) and hence show that
S_{1}(u)
is open. Thus, the estimate holds for all
v^{\prime}\in[v_{0},v(u_{n})]
. Inequality (
7.61
) then follows from (
7.13
).
∎

Lemma
7.7
describes the evolution of
\delta(u,v)
along the
u
-direction. In contrast, the following lemma describes the evolution of
\delta(u,v)
along the
v
-direction.

Lemma 7.9
.

Given any sequence
u_{n}\in[u_{2},v_{0})
, for each
n
, suppose that there exists a corresponding
v(u_{n})\in[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]
, such that, for all
u\in[u_{2},u_{n}]
, both

(7.71)

\delta(u,v(u_{n}))\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))e^{-\gamma_{0}(u)}}{3},\frac{e^{-\frac{3}{2}(1+2\xi)\gamma_{0}(u)}}{2C_{34}}\right\}

and (
7.60
) hold, with
C_{34}
defined in (
7.97
). Then, for any
v\in(v_{0},v(u_{n})]
and
u_{3},u_{4}\in[u_{2},u_{n}]
with
u_{3}<u_{4}
, we have

(7.72)

\delta(u_{3},v^{\prime})\leq 4e^{-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(u_{3})}{r_{0}(u_{4})}\right)+\frac{3}{2}(1+2\xi)\gamma_{0}(u_{4})}\delta(u_{4},v^{\prime}).

\mathcal{O}
\Gamma
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
C_{0}^{+}
v_{0}
v(u_{n})
v
u=u_{n}
u=u_{2}
u_{4}
u_{3}

Figure 13.
The relative positions as described in Lemma
7.9
.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.7
and (
7.71
), we have that
\delta(u,v)\leq\frac{1}{2}
for all
u\in[u_{3},u_{4}]\subset[u_{2},u_{n}]
and
v\in(v_{0},v(u_{n})]
. Consequently, by the inequality
\frac{1}{2}x\leq\log(1+x)\leq x
for
0\leq x\leq\frac{1}{2}
, for all
u\in[u_{3},u_{4}]
and
v\in(v_{0},v(u_{n})]
, we get

(7.73)

\frac{1}{2}\delta(u,v)\leq\log\left(\frac{r(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)}\right)\leq\delta(u,v).

Furthermore, by (
1.19
), we also have

(7.74)

\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)=\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}-1\right).

Consider any
(u,v)\in[u_{2},u_{n}]\times(v_{0},v(u_{n})]
. By (
7.74
) and (
7.61
) of Lemma
7.8
, we obtain

(7.75)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\log\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\left(\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}-1\right)\leq\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\left(\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}-1\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}-2\right)\leq\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}}-2\right).

Next, we perform an integration from
v=v_{0}
to
v=v
and derive

(7.76)

\log\left(\frac{-\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}(u,v)}{\left(-\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)}\right)\leq\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}(u)}-2\right)\log\left(\frac{r(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)}\right),

which implies

(7.77)

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\left(-\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}(u)}-2\right)\log\left(\frac{r(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)}\right)}.

By our assumption in (
7.71
), for
u\in[u_{2},u_{n}]
, we have

(7.78)

\delta(u,v(u_{n}))\leq\frac{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))}{3}e^{-\gamma_{0}(u)}.

Hence, by (
7.59
), (
7.63
), (
7.73
), (
7.78
), the exponent in the right hand side of (
7.77
) can be bounded from above by

(7.79)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}(u)}-2\right)\log\left(\frac{r(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u))}\delta(u,v)\leq\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v(u_{n}))\leq 1.

Combining (
7.77
) and (
7.79
) implies

(7.80)

\left(-\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)(u,v)\leq 3\left(-\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u).

On the other hand, we can rewrite (
7.77
) as

(7.81)

\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)(u,v)-\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)\leq\left(e^{\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}(u)}-2\right)\log\left(\frac{r(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)}\right)}-1\right)\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}.

This form is of great use if we calculate

(7.82)

\displaystyle-\partial_{u}\log\left(\frac{r(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r_{0}}(u,v)-\frac{r(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)^{2}}(\partial_{u}r)_{0}(u)}{\frac{r(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)}}=\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}(u,v)-\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)

\displaystyle\leq\left(e^{\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}(u)}-2\right)\log\left(\frac{r(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)}\right)}-1\right)\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}.

For each
(u,v)\in[u_{2},u_{n}]\times(v_{0},v(u_{n})]
, we define

(7.83)

\tilde{\delta}(u,v):=\log\left(\frac{r(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)}\right).

By employing the inequality
e^{x}-1\leq x+(e-2)x^{2}
for
x\leq 1
, inequality (
7.82
) yields

(7.84)

-\partial_{u}\tilde{\delta}\leq\left(\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u))}-2\right)\tilde{\delta}+(e-2)\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u))}-2\right)^{2}\tilde{\delta}^{2}\right)\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}\\

and

(7.85)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u))}-2\right)\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\delta}}\right)+(e-2)\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u))}-2\right)^{2}\right)\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}.

By multiplying a suitable integrating factor on both sides of (
7.85
), we arrive at

(7.86)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\delta}(u,v)}e^{-\int^{u}_{0}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-2\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(e-2)\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u))}-2\right)^{2}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{-\int^{u}_{0}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-2\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}.

By performing integration along
v\in(u_{0},v(u_{2})]
from
u_{3}
to
u_{4}
, for
u_{2}\leq u_{3}<u_{4}\leq u_{n}
, we obtain

(7.87)

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-\int^{u_{4}}_{0}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-2\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}}{\tilde{\delta}(u_{4},v)}-\frac{e^{-\int^{u_{3}}_{0}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-2\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}}{\tilde{\delta}(u_{3},v)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(e-2)\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime\prime}))}-2\right)^{2}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})e^{-\int^{u^{\prime\prime}}_{0}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-2\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}.

As the exponent in (
7.87
) has the following upper bound

(7.88)

\displaystyle-\int^{u^{\prime\prime}}_{0}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-2\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}=-\int^{u^{\prime\prime}}_{0}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}{1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq-\int^{u^{\prime\prime}}_{0}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{(\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2})_{0}(u^{\prime})}{1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})}\right),

we can shorten (
7.87
) to

(7.89)

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-\int^{u_{4}}_{0}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-2\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}}{\tilde{\delta}(u_{4},v)}-\frac{e^{-\int^{u_{3}}_{0}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-2\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}}{\tilde{\delta}(u_{3},v)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(e-2)\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-2\right)^{2}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

To further simplify the upper bound in (
7.89
), we first observe that, for all

, we have

\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{1}{1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}-2\right)^{2}\leq\frac{9}{4}\left(\frac{\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}{1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}.

This implies that we can analyze the contribution of the integral from two separate terms as follows. First, we have

(7.91)

\displaystyle\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{3})}\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{3})}e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u_{4})}\right)},

since inequality (
7.36
) with
\xi\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right)
implies that
\gamma_{0}(u)
is non-decreasing in

for

. Next, appealing to (
7.13
) and (

) along

, we obtain an estimate for the following integral

(7.92)

\displaystyle\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}{1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)^{2}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{(\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2})_{0}(u^{\prime})}{1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)\left(\frac{\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}{1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{(\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2})_{0}(u^{\prime})}{1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}\partial_{u}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}\partial_{u}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Integrating by parts, the last line of (
7.92
) can be rewritten as

(7.93)

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}\partial_{u}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}\left(\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}|^{u^{\prime}=u_{4}}_{u^{\prime}=u_{3}}-\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})}\partial_{u}\left(e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{3})+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u_{3})}\right)}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{4})+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u_{4})}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})}\partial_{u}\left(e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Since
\partial_{u}\left(e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\right)\geq 0
and
\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})
is non-decreasing in

, the second term in the last line of (

(7.94)

\displaystyle\frac{4}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})}\partial_{u}\left(e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{3})}}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}\partial_{u}\left(e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{3})+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u_{4})}\right)}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{3})+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u_{3})}\right)}\right).

Combining (

) and (

), we observe precise cancellations and this gives

(7.95)

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}\partial_{u}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u_{4})}\right)}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{3})}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{4})}\right).

Back to the upper bound in (

), by combining (

), (

), we hence deduce that

(7.96)

\displaystyle(e-2)\int^{u_{4}}_{u_{3}}\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-2\right)^{2}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(e-2)\left(\frac{1}{2}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{3})}e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u_{4})}\right)}+\frac{9}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u_{4})}\right)}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{3})}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{4})}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{19(e-2)}{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))}e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u_{4})}\right)}=C_{34}e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u_{4})}\right)}

with

In addition, under the assumption (

), together with (

. For each

Next, we return to (

) and proceed to simplify the terms on the left as follows

(7.100)

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-\int^{u_{4}}_{0}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-2\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}}{\tilde{\delta}(u_{4},v)}-\frac{e^{-\int^{u_{3}}_{0}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-2\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}}{\tilde{\delta}(u_{3},v)}

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-\int^{u_{4}}_{0}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}{(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}}{\tilde{\delta}(u_{4},v)}-\frac{e^{-\int^{u_{3}}_{0}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{\mu_{0}(u^{\prime})}{(1-\mu_{0}(u^{\prime}))}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}}{\tilde{\delta}(u_{3},v)}

and

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\delta}{(u_{4},v)}2e^{-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(u_{3})}{r_{0}(u_{4})}\right)-\frac{3}{2}(\gamma_{s,0}(u_{3})-\gamma_{s,0}(u_{4}))}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\delta}{(u_{4},v)}2e^{-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(u_{3})}{r_{0}(u_{4})}\right)+\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{s,0}(u_{4})}.

Together with (

), the definition of

in (

Lemma 7.10
.

For each

with

and

(7.103)

|Q\phi|(u,v)\leq C_{5}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon};\chi,2,\chi)r^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{3\chi}{4}}(u,v^{\prime}).

Proof.

) of Proposition
3.3
if we set

The second inequality can be deduced from (

and

with

.

With the above preparations, we return to the proof of Theorem

for the cases under

We begin with defining the following quantities

From (

Let

\displaystyle\geq\left|\left|\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}(u_{n},v^{\prime})\left(\frac{r(u_{n},v^{\prime})}{r_{0}(u_{n})}\right)-\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u_{n})\right|-\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u_{n})\right|\right|

\displaystyle\geq\left|\frac{h}{3}-\frac{h}{6}\right|e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}=\frac{h}{6}e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}.

Observe that due to (

Lemma 7.11
.

Let
u_{n}\in[u_{2},v_{0})
and
v(u_{n})\in[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]
be chosen such that (
7.60
) and (
7.71
) hold. If we further demand that for all
u\in[u_{2},u_{n}]
, we have

(7.118)

\delta(u,v(u_{n}))\leq\min\left\{\left(\frac{\log(3)}{8C_{48}}\right)^{2}e^{-(9+6\xi)\gamma_{0}(u)},\left(\frac{h}{1296C_{45}}\right)^{2}e^{-\left(\frac{15}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)},\left(\frac{h}{1296bC_{49}}\right)^{2}e^{-\frac{19}{2}\gamma_{0}(u)}\right\}

and

(7.119)

\sup_{v\in[v_{0},v(u_{n})]}|\psi|(u_{2},v)\leq\frac{h}{54},

then inequality (
7.108
) holds, that is,

\sup_{v^{\prime}\in[v_{0},v(u_{n})]}|\psi|(u_{n},v^{\prime})\leq\frac{h}{6}.

Proof.

From (
7.106
) and (
7.105
), we first compute

(7.120)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\psi(u,v)

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\left(e^{-\gamma_{0}(u)}\Upxi(u,v)\right)=e^{-\gamma_{0}(u)}\left(-\partial_{u}\left(\gamma_{0}(u)\right)\Upxi(u,v)+\partial_{u}\left(\Upxi(u,v)\right)\right).

In computations that follow, we suppress the dependence of the quantities on
(u,v)
and abbreviate terms without a subscript
()_{0}
as being evaluated at
(u,v)
, while terms with that are evaluated at
(u,v_{0})
. We can then calculate

(7.121)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}(\gamma_{0})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)_{0}

and

(7.122)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\Upxi

\displaystyle=\frac{r}{r_{0}}\partial_{u}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)+\partial_{u}\left(\frac{r}{r_{0}}\right)\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}-\partial_{u}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}.

By (
7.16
), we can simplify (
7.122
) to

(7.123)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\Upxi=

\displaystyle\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(\left(-D_{u}\phi-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)+\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\left(-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}+\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\right)+\frac{(\partial_{u}r)r_{0}-(\partial_{u}r)_{0}r}{r_{0}^{2}}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)

\displaystyle-\left(\left(-D_{u}\phi-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)_{0}+\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}\left(-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}+\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)_{0}\right).

Prior to substituting this back to (
7.120
), we first compute

(7.124)

\displaystyle(-\partial_{u}\gamma_{0})\Upxi+\partial_{u}\Upxi=

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)_{0}\left(\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)-\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}\right)+\frac{(\partial_{u}r)r_{0}-(\partial_{u}r)_{0}r}{r_{0}^{2}}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(\left(-D_{u}\phi-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)+\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\left(-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}+\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\left(\left(-D_{u}\phi-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)_{0}+\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}\left(-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}+\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)_{0}\left(\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\right)+\frac{(\partial_{u}r)r_{0}-(\partial_{u}r)_{0}r}{r_{0}^{2}}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(\left(-D_{u}\phi-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)+\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\left(-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}+\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\left(\left(-D_{u}\phi-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)_{0}+\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}\left(-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}\right)_{0}\right).

Rearranging the expression above, we get

(7.125)

\displaystyle(-\partial_{u}\gamma_{0})\Upxi+\partial_{u}\Upxi=

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(-D_{u}\phi-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)+\left(D_{u}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left\{\partial_{u}\gamma-\partial_{u}\gamma_{0}+\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}-\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\left(A_{u}\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)-(A_{u})_{0}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}\right).

Next, observe from (
7.105
) and (
7.106
) that

(7.126)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)\frac{r}{r_{0}}

\displaystyle=\psi e^{\gamma_{0}}+\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}.

Thus, we can rewrite (
7.125
) as

(7.127)

\displaystyle(-\partial_{u}\gamma_{0})\Upxi+\partial_{u}\Upxi=

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(-D_{u}\phi-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)+\left(D_{u}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle+\psi e^{\gamma_{0}}\left\{\partial_{u}\gamma-\partial_{u}\gamma_{0}+\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}-\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}\left\{\partial_{u}\gamma-\partial_{u}\gamma_{0}+\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}-\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}\psi e^{\gamma_{0}}-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}\left(A_{u}-(A_{u})_{0}\right).

We now apply (
7.127
) to (
7.120
) and obtain

(7.128)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\psi=

\displaystyle e^{-\gamma_{0}}\left\{\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(-D_{u}\phi-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)+\left(D_{u}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle+\psi\left\{\partial_{u}\gamma-\partial_{u}\gamma_{0}+\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}-\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}\right\}

\displaystyle+e^{-\gamma_{0}}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}\left\{\partial_{u}\gamma-\partial_{u}\gamma_{0}+\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}-\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}(A_{u})_{0}\right\}.

By defining the following terms:

(7.129)

\displaystyle J_{4}

\displaystyle:=\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(-D_{u}\phi-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)+\left(D_{u}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)_{0},

\displaystyle J_{5}

\displaystyle:=\partial_{u}\gamma-\partial_{u}\gamma_{0}+\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}-\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0},

\displaystyle J_{6}

\displaystyle:=J_{5}-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}(A_{u}-(A_{u})_{0}),

the expression in (
7.128
) can be rewritten as

(7.130)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\psi

\displaystyle=e^{-\gamma_{0}}J_{4}+\psi(J_{5}-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u})+e^{-\gamma_{0}}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}J_{6}.

Through the use of a suitable integrating factor, we deduce that

(7.131)

\partial_{u}(e^{-\int^{u}_{u_{2}}J_{5}(u^{\prime},v)+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\psi)=e^{-\gamma_{0}-\int^{u}_{u_{2}}J_{5}(u^{\prime},v)+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\left(J_{4}+J_{6}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}\right).

Upon integrating with respect to
u
from
u_{2}
, we obtain

(7.132)

\displaystyle e^{-\int^{u}_{u_{2}}J_{5}(u^{\prime},v)-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\psi(u,v)-\psi(u_{2},v)

\displaystyle=\int^{u}_{u_{2}}e^{-\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})-\int^{u^{\prime\prime}}_{u_{2}}J_{5}(u^{\prime},v)-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\left(J_{4}(u^{\prime\prime},v)+J_{6}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}.

This implies that

(7.133)

\displaystyle|\psi|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq e^{\int^{u}_{u_{2}}|J_{5}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\left(|\psi|(u_{2},v)+\int^{u}_{u_{2}}e^{\int^{u^{\prime\prime}}_{u_{2}}|J_{5}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\left(|J_{4}|(u^{\prime\prime},v)+|J_{6}|\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime\prime},v)\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}\right).

Here we use the fact that the term
\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}
is purely imaginary, and thus
e^{\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}}
constitutes an additional phase factor. We then proceed to estimate each of the terms
|J_{4}|,|J_{5}|,|J_{6}|
in (
7.129
).
Starting from
|J_{4}|
, we can rewrite it as

(7.134)

\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(-D_{u}\phi-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)+\left(D_{u}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)_{0}=-\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(D_{u}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)\right)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

This inspires us to compute the integrand in (
7.134
), which yields

(7.135)

-\partial_{v}\left(\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(D_{u}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)\right)=-\frac{1}{r_{0}}\left(\partial_{v}\left(rD_{u}\phi\right)+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{1-\mu}\right)\right).

Observe that by using (
1.18
), (
1.19
), (
1.28
), we have

(7.136)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{1-\mu}\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{\phi(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}Q)}{1-\mu}+\frac{Q(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}r)}{1-\mu}+\frac{Q\phi\partial_{v}\partial_{u}r}{1-\mu}+\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}\mu)}{(1-\mu)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-4\pi\mathfrak{e})\frac{\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{1-\mu}r^{2}\im{(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)}+\frac{Q(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}r)}{1-\mu}+\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)(\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2})}{r(1-\mu)^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{(1-\mu)^{2}}\left(\frac{4\pi r(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\left(\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(4\pi\mathfrak{e})\frac{\phi(-\partial_{u}r)r^{2}\im{(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)}}{1-\mu}+\frac{Q(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}r)}{1-\mu}+4\pi\frac{Qr|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}(\partial_{u}r)\phi}{\partial_{v}r(1-\mu)}.

Together with (
1.24
), equation (
7.136
) implies that

(7.137)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{r}{r_{0}}\left(D_{u}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q\phi(\partial_{u}r)}{r(1-\mu)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r_{0}}\left((-\partial_{u}r)\partial_{v}\phi+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)\phi}{r(1-\mu)}+(4\pi\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}^{2})\frac{\phi(-\partial_{u}r)r^{2}\im{(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)}}{1-\mu}+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}r)}{1-\mu}+4\pi\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Qr|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}(\partial_{u}r)\phi}{\partial_{v}r(1-\mu)}\right).

By (
7.134
) and the fact that
\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r\geq 0
, we have the following estimate for
J_{4}

(7.138)

|J_{4}|(u,v)\leq\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)}\left(|H_{1}|+|H_{2}|+|H_{3}|+|H_{4}|+|H_{5}|\right)(u,v),

with

(7.139)

\begin{aligned} |H_{1}|(u,v)&=\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime},\\
|H_{3}|(u,v)&=4\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{r^{2}|\phi|^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime},\\
|H_{5}|(u,v)&=4\pi\mathfrak{e}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{r|Q||\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}|\phi|}{\partial_{v}r(1-\mu)}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.\\
\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} |H_{2}|(u,v)&=\mathfrak{e}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{|Q||\phi|(\partial_{v}r)}{r(1-\mu)}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime},\\
|H_{4}|(u,v)&=\mathfrak{e}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{|Q||\partial_{v}\phi|}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime},\\
\end{aligned}

As (
7.60
) and (
7.71
) were verified, we thus have proved (
7.72
). Consequently, we have
\delta(u,v(u_{n}))\leq\frac{1}{2}
for each
u\in[u_{2},u_{n}]
. This implies that (
7.30
) is true and hence (
7.113
) holds, not just for
u_{n}
, but also for each
u\in[u_{2},u_{n}]
. This then yields that for each
v\in(v_{0},v(u_{n})]
, we have

(7.140)

\displaystyle\eta^{*}(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq C_{36}\delta(u,v)\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta(u,v)}\right)+e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}r_{0}(u)\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{38}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}

with

(7.141)

C_{38}:=C_{36}\left(\frac{2}{e}+\frac{r_{0}(0)}{2}\right).

Here we have used Lemma
3.2
and the fact that
\gamma_{0}(u_{2})\geq 0
.

We start to bound
|H_{1}|
to
|H_{5}|
as follows. For
|H_{1}|
, using (
7.61
), (
7.110
), (
7.140
), we obtain

\displaystyle|H_{1}|(u,v)=

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(7.142)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{8\pi}\left(1-\mu_{0}(0)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}r(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta^{*}(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u)}\left(\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{8\pi}\left(1-\mu_{0}(0)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\eta^{*}(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u)}\left(\frac{r(u,v)}{r(u,v_{0})}-\frac{r(u,v)}{r(u,v)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{3}C_{38}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\sqrt{8\pi}\left(1-\mu_{0}(0)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}=C_{39}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

with

(7.143)

C_{39}:=\sqrt{\frac{3C_{38}}{8\sqrt{2}\pi(1-\mu_{0}(0))}}.

Employing (
7.103
) with
\chi\in\left(0,\frac{1}{3}\right]
, we can see that
|H_{2}|
obeys

(7.144)

\displaystyle|H_{2}|(u,v)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{e}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{|Q||\phi|(\partial_{v}r)}{r(1-\mu)}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\frac{3C_{5}\mathfrak{e}}{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}r^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{3\chi}{4}}\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3C_{5}\mathfrak{e}r_{0}(0)r(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{3\chi}{4}}}{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)=C_{40}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

with

(7.145)

C_{40}:=\frac{3C_{5}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon};\chi,2,\chi)\mathfrak{e}r_{0}(0)r(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{3\chi}{4}}}{2\sqrt{2}(1-\mu_{0}(0))}.

To bound
|H_{3}|
, we first appeal to (
7.102
) and use inequality
(a+b)^{4}\leq 8(a^{4}+b^{4})
to derive

(7.146)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{v_{0}}r^{2}|\phi|^{4}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq

\displaystyle 8C_{35}^{4}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}r^{2}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}r^{2}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\left(\log\left(\frac{\frac{1-\mu}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v)}{\frac{1-\mu}{\partial_{v}r}(u_{2},v)}\right)\right)^{2}\left(\log\left(\frac{r(u_{2},v)}{r(u,v)}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

The first integrand on the right satisfies

(7.147)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{v_{0}}r^{2}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3r^{2}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})}{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\frac{3r^{2}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})r_{0}(0)}{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v).

By Lemma
3.2
and (
7.61
), the second integrated obeys

(7.148)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{v_{0}}r^{2}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\left(\log\left(\frac{\frac{1-\mu}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v)}{\frac{1-\mu}{\partial_{v}r}(u_{2},v)}\right)\right)^{2}\left(\log\left(\frac{r(u_{2},v)}{r(u,v)}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\bar{B}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})}{(1-\overline{\mu}_{*})(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{16r(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})^{2}}{e^{4}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{3\bar{B}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})\tilde{\varepsilon}}{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))(1-\overline{\mu}_{*})(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{16r(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})^{2}}{e^{4}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u)}\left(\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{3\bar{B}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})\tilde{\varepsilon}r_{0}(0)}{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))(1-\overline{\mu}_{*})(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{16r(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})^{2}}{e^{4}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Combining (
7.146
), (
7.147
), (
7.148
), we then obtain

(7.149)

\int^{v}_{v_{0}}r^{2}|\phi|^{4}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq C_{41}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

with

(7.150)

C_{41}:=\frac{12C_{35}^{4}r^{2}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})r_{0}(0)}{\sqrt{2}(1-\mu_{0}(0))}+\left(\frac{3\bar{B}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})\tilde{\varepsilon}r_{0}(0)}{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))(1-\overline{\mu}_{*})(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{8r(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\pi^{2}e^{4}}.

With these preparations, for
H_{3}
, we have

(7.151)

\displaystyle|H_{3}|(u,v)

\displaystyle=4\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{r^{2}|\phi|^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\frac{6\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}}{(1-\mu_{0}(0))}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}r^{2}|\phi|^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}
