\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\pi\mathfrak{e}^{2}e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}}{\sqrt{2\pi}(1-\mu_{0}(0))}\left(\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{r^{2}|\phi|^{4}\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\sqrt{\pi}\mathfrak{e}^{2}C_{41}^{\frac{1}{2}}C_{38}^{\frac{1}{2}}r(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(1-\mu_{0}(0))}e^{\frac{7}{4}\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}=C_{42}e^{\frac{7}{4}\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

with

(7.152)

C_{42}:=\frac{3\sqrt{\pi}\mathfrak{e}^{2}C_{41}^{\frac{1}{2}}C_{38}^{\frac{1}{2}}r(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(1-\mu_{0}(0))}.

Continuing on to
|H_{4}|
, by employing the estimate for
\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}
that is already done in (
7.142
) and applying (
7.35
) with
\mu<1
, we derive

(7.153)

\displaystyle|H_{4}|(u,v)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{e}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{|Q||\partial_{v}\phi|}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\frac{\mathfrak{e}\sqrt{\xi}r(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathfrak{e}\sqrt{\xi}r(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})C_{39}}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}e^{2\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}=C_{43}e^{2\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

with

(7.154)

C_{43}:=\frac{\mathfrak{e}\sqrt{\xi}r(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})C_{39}}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}.

For
|H_{5}|
, using (
7.103
), we deduce

(7.155)

\displaystyle|H_{5}|(u,v)

\displaystyle=4\pi\mathfrak{e}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{r|Q||\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}|\phi|}{\partial_{v}r(1-\mu)}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{9\mathfrak{e}C_{5}r^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{3\chi}{4}}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})}{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))^{2}}e^{2\gamma_{0}(u)}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{9\mathfrak{e}C_{5}C_{38}r^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{3\chi}{4}}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})}{4(1-\mu_{0}(0))^{2}}e^{3\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}=C_{44}e^{3\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

with

(7.156)

C_{44}:=\frac{9\mathfrak{e}C_{5}C_{38}r^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{3\chi}{4}}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})}{4(1-\mu_{0}(0))^{2}}.

Summarizing the above estimates for
|H_{1}|
to
|H_{5}|
and substituting them back to (
7.138
), we obtain

(7.157)

\displaystyle|J_{4}|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq(C_{39}+C_{40}+C_{42}+C_{43}+C_{44})\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)}e^{3\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=(C_{39}+C_{40}+C_{42}+C_{43}+C_{44})\left(-\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)(u,v)\frac{r(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)}e^{3\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{45}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{3\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

with

(7.158)

C_{45}:=\frac{9}{2}\left(C_{39}+C_{40}+C_{42}+C_{43}+C_{44}\right).

Here we use the fact that
\delta(u,v)\leq 1/2
and the inequality (
7.80
) in the proof of Lemma
7.9
holds.

We now proceed to estimate
|J_{5}|
. From the expression of
J_{5}
in (
7.129
), we can rewrite it as

(7.159)

J_{5}(u,v)=\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\left(\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)+\partial_{v}\partial_{u}\gamma\right)(u,v^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

By (
1.19
), the first term in the integrand yields

(7.160)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)=\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{2\mu-1-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right).

For the second term in the integrand, observe that

(7.161)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\partial_{u}\gamma=

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{8}+J_{9}+J_{10}

with

(7.162)

J_{8}:=T_{1},\quad J_{9}:=T_{2},\quad\text{ and }\quad J_{10}:=T_{3},

and
T_{1},T_{2},
and
T_{3}
defined in (
1.40
).
Henceforth, we have

(7.163)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)+\partial_{v}\partial_{u}\gamma

\displaystyle=J_{8}+J_{9}+J_{10}+\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{2\mu-1-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)

\displaystyle=J_{8}+J_{9}+J_{11}

with

(7.164)

J_{11}:=\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}}\left(-4+\frac{3-5Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right).

Returning back to (
7.159
), we deduce that

(7.165)

\displaystyle|J_{5}|(u,v)\leq\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|J_{8}|+|J_{9}|+|J_{11}|(u,v^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

To estimate
|J_{8}|,|J_{9}|,|J_{11}|
, we utilize similar strategies as above. Together with (
7.80
), for
|J_{8}|
we derive

(7.166)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|J_{8}|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 24\pi\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{|Q||\phi||\partial_{v}\phi|}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{24\pi\mathfrak{e}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)\left(\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}}\frac{|Q|^{2}|\phi|^{2}}{(1-\mu)^{3}}(u,v^{\prime})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{18\sqrt{3\pi}\mathfrak{e}C_{5}r^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{\chi}{4}}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})}{\sqrt{2}(1-\mu_{0}(0))^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{0}(u)}\eta^{*}(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{46}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{2\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

with

(7.167)

C_{46}:=\frac{18\sqrt{3\pi}\mathfrak{e}C_{5}r^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{\chi}{4}}(u_{2},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})r_{0}(0)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{\frac{3}{4}}(1-\mu_{0}(0))^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

For
|J_{9}|
, using
Q^{2}/r^{2}<1
, we get

(7.168)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|J_{9}|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 6\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{2\pi r|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r(1-\mu)}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{27}{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))^{2}}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{2\gamma_{0}(u)}\frac{r(u,v)}{r_{0}(u)}\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{81}{8(1-\mu_{0}(0))^{2}}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{2\gamma_{0}(u)}\eta^{*}(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq C_{47}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{3\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}},

with

(7.169)

C_{47}:=\frac{81C_{38}}{8(1-\mu_{0}(0))^{2}}.

Similarly, we also obtain

(7.170)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|J_{11}|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 3\left(-\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\left(4+\frac{3+5Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{36}{1-\mu_{0}(0)}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}(u,v^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{36}{\sqrt{2}(1-\mu_{0}(0))}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For
J_{6}
, we observe that it is the addition of
J_{5}
and a term that can be estimated by applying

\partial_{v}A_{u}=\frac{2Q\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}<1

as below

(7.171)

\displaystyle|-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}(A_{u}(u,v)-(A_{u})_{0}(u))|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathfrak{e}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\partial_{v}A_{u}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right|\leq\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{2\mathfrak{e}|Q|(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\mathfrak{e}}{(1-\mu_{0}(0))}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\mathfrak{e}r_{0}(0)}{\sqrt{2}(1-\mu_{0}(0))}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Combining the above estimates, we then obtain

(7.172)

\displaystyle|J_{5}|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq C_{48}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{3\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle|J_{6}|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq C_{49}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u)e^{3\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where

(7.173)

\displaystyle C_{48}

\displaystyle:=C_{46}+C_{47}+\frac{36}{\sqrt{2}\left(1-\mu_{0}(0)\right)}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad C_{49}

\displaystyle:=C_{48}+\frac{6\mathfrak{e}r_{0}(0)}{\sqrt{2}\left(1-\mu_{0}(0)\right)}.

With the desired estimates for
|J_{4}|,|J_{5}|,|J_{6}|
as obtained above, we are now in shape to conclude. Applying the crucial inequality (
7.72
), for each
u\in[u_{2},u_{n}]
and
v\in(v_{0},v(u_{n})]
, we deduce that

(7.174)

\displaystyle\int^{u}_{u_{2}}|J_{5}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C_{48}\int^{u}_{u_{2}}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})e^{3\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})}\delta(u^{\prime},v)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{48}e^{\left(\frac{15}{4}+\frac{3}{2}\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}\int^{u}_{u_{2}}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})e^{-\frac{1}{4}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}{r_{0}(u)}\right)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 8C_{48}e^{\left(\frac{15}{4}+\frac{3}{2}\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

In a similar fashion, we also derive

(7.175)

\int^{u}_{u_{2}}|J_{4}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq 8C_{45}e^{\left(\frac{15}{4}+\frac{3}{2}\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(7.176)

\int^{u}_{u_{2}}|J_{6}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq 8C_{49}e^{\left(\frac{15}{4}+\frac{3}{2}\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

In particular, for each given
u
and a correspondingly given
u^{\prime\prime}\in[u_{2},u]
, by applying (
7.72
) and our assumption (
7.118
), we obtain

(7.177)

\displaystyle\int_{u_{2}}^{u^{\prime\prime}}|J_{5}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 8C_{48}e^{\left(\frac{15}{4}+\frac{3}{2}\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u^{\prime\prime},v)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq 8C_{48}e^{\left(\frac{9}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v(u_{n}))^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\log(3).

Consequently, this yields

(7.178)

\int^{u}_{u_{2}}e^{\int^{u^{\prime\prime}}_{u_{2}}|J_{5}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}|J_{4}|(u^{\prime\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}\leq 24C_{45}e^{\left(\frac{15}{4}+\frac{3}{2}\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}},

and by (
7.58
) we have

(7.179)

\displaystyle\int^{u}_{u_{2}}e^{\int^{u^{\prime\prime}}_{u_{2}}|J_{5}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}|J_{6}|(u^{\prime\prime},v)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 3be^{\gamma_{0}(u)}\int^{u}_{u_{2}}|J_{6}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 24bC_{49}e^{(\frac{19}{4}+\frac{3}{2}\xi)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Collecting (
7.177
), (
7.178
), (
7.179
) and going back to (
7.133
), we arrive at

(7.180)

|\psi|(u,v)\leq 3\left(|\psi|(u_{2},v)+24C_{45}e^{\left(\frac{15}{4}+\frac{3}{2}\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}+24bC_{49}e^{(\frac{19}{4}+\frac{3}{2}\xi)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Together with (
7.119
), for each
v\in[v_{0},v(u_{n})]
, we then conclude that (
7.180
) implies

(7.181)

|\psi|(u_{n},v)\leq 3\left(\frac{h}{54}\times 3\right)=\frac{h}{6}.

Taking the supremum over
[v_{0},v(u_{n})]
, we now verify (
7.108
).
∎

Back to the proof of the main theorem, by assuming (
7.118
) and (
7.119
), Lemma
7.11
implies that (
7.108
) holds. Hence, it remains to check that such a choice of
v(u_{n})
in (
7.117
) satisfies the assumptions (
7.60
), (
7.71
), (
7.118
), (
7.119
).
For (
7.60
), (
7.71
), (
7.118
), at
u=u_{n}
with
n
sufficiently large, since
\gamma_{0}(u_{n})\rightarrow\infty
, we have

(7.182)

\displaystyle\delta(u_{n},v(u_{n}))\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta(u_{n},v(u_{n}))}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-\frac{C_{37}}{4C_{36}}e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}}\log\left(e^{\frac{C_{37}}{4C_{36}}e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}}\right)\leq C_{33}e^{-2\gamma_{0}(u_{n})},

(7.183)

\displaystyle\delta(u_{n},v(u_{n}))

\displaystyle=e^{-\frac{C_{37}}{4C_{36}}e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}}\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))e^{-\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}}{3},\frac{e^{-\frac{3}{2}(1+2\xi)\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}}{2C_{34}}\right\},

(7.184)

\displaystyle\delta(u_{n},v(u_{n}))

\displaystyle=e^{-\frac{C_{37}}{4C_{36}}e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}}

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\left(\frac{\log(3)}{8C_{48}}\right)^{2}e^{-(9+6\xi)\gamma_{0}(u_{n})},\left(\frac{h}{1296C_{45}}\right)^{2}e^{-\left(\frac{15}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u_{n})},\left(\frac{h}{1296bC_{49}}\right)^{2}e^{-\frac{19}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}\right\}.

To verify that the above assumptions all hold for all
u\in[u_{2},u_{n}]
, let
u_{*}
be the smallest
u\in[u_{2},u_{n}]
with (
7.60
), (
7.71
), (
7.118
) satisfied for all
u\in[u_{*},u_{n}]
. We can see that, if
u=u_{*}>u_{2}
, then one of the following three scenarios would happen

(7.185)

\delta(u_{*},v(u_{n}))\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta(u_{*},v(u_{n}))}\right)=C_{33}e^{-2\gamma_{0}(u_{*})},

or

(7.186)

\delta(u_{*},v(u_{n}))=\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))e^{-\gamma_{0}(u_{*})}}{3},\frac{e^{-\frac{3}{2}(1+2\xi)\gamma_{0}(u_{*})}}{2C_{34}}\right\},

or

(7.187)

\delta(u_{*},v(u_{n}))=\min\left\{\left(\frac{\log(3)}{8C_{48}}\right)^{2}e^{-(9+6\xi)\gamma_{0}(u_{*})},\left(\frac{h}{1296C_{45}}\right)^{2}e^{-\left(\frac{15}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u_{*})},\left(\frac{h}{1296bC_{49}}\right)^{2}e^{-\frac{19}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{*})}\right\}.

By referring to the proof of Lemma
7.9
, we have that (
7.72
) is true, as long as the assumption (
7.71
) holds for all
u\in[u_{3},u_{4}]
. By setting
u_{3}=u_{*}
and
u_{4}=u_{n}
, we can see that (
7.72
) implies

(7.188)

\displaystyle\delta(u_{*},v(u_{n}))

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta(u_{*},v(u_{n}))}\right)\leq\frac{2}{e}\delta(u_{*},v(u_{n}))^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{e}\delta(u_{n},v(u_{n}))^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(u_{*})}{r_{0}(u_{n})}\right)+\frac{3}{4}\left(1+2\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}=4e^{-\frac{C_{37}}{8C_{36}}e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}+\frac{3}{4}\left(1+2\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u_{n})-1}

and

(7.189)

\displaystyle\delta(u_{*},v(u_{n}))

\displaystyle\leq\delta(u_{n},v(u_{n}))4e^{-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(u_{*})}{r_{0}(u_{n})}\right)+\frac{3}{2}\left(1+2\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}

\displaystyle\leq 4e^{-\frac{C_{37}}{4C_{36}}e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}+\frac{3}{2}\left(1+2\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}.

Hence, we observe that (
7.189
) contradicts both (
7.185
) and (
7.187
), while (
7.188
) contradicts (
7.186
) for sufficiently large
n
. We then deduce that
u_{*}=u_{2}
necessarily, and hence the assumptions (
7.60
), (
7.71
), and (
7.118
) hold for all
u\in[u_{2},u_{n}]
.

It remains to verify that (
7.119
) is true for a sufficiently large
n
. To do so, by the continuity of
|\psi|(u_{2},\cdot)
, it suffices to prove that as
n\rightarrow\infty
, we have
v(u_{n})\rightarrow v_{0}
. By (
7.72
), as
n\rightarrow\infty
, we indeed have

(7.190)

\delta(u_{2},v(u_{n}))\leq 4\delta(u_{n},v(u_{n}))e^{\frac{3}{2}\left(1+2\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}=4e^{-\frac{C_{37}}{4C_{36}}e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}+\frac{3}{2}(1+2\xi)\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}\rightarrow 0.

Henceforth, employing the continuity of
\delta(u_{2},\cdot)
, applying Lemma
7.7
and the fact that
\delta(u_{2},v_{0})=0
, we conclude that
v(u_{n})\rightarrow v_{0}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, which thus verifies (
7.119
). This completes our discussion for cases under
Scenario (I)
.

For
Scenario (II)
, in each of the cases, we have

(7.191)

\limsup_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}|I(u)|=+\infty.

This implies that there exists an increasing sequence
u_{n}\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
such that
|I(u_{n})|\rightarrow+\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Following (
7.58
), we now define
b:[0,v_{0})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as

(7.192)

b(u):=\sup_{u^{\prime}\in[0,u]}\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})e^{-\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})}.

Note that
b(u)
is a non-decreasing function and
b(u_{n})\rightarrow+\infty
as
u_{n}\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
. The latter follows from (
7.19
) and
|I(u_{n})|\rightarrow+\infty
as
u_{n}\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
. Hence, there exists an increasing sequence
u_{n}\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
such that

(7.193)

b(u_{n})=\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u_{n})e^{-\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}.

Parallel to the arguments presented in
Scenario (I)
, we observe that all the corresponding estimates still hold as long as the inequalities in (
7.60
) and (
7.71
) are satisfied. By modifying (
7.118
) via replacing
h
with
1
and replacing
b
with
b(u)
such that for each
u\in[u_{2},v_{0})
we have

(7.194)

\delta\left(u,v(u_{n})\right)\leq\min\left\{\left(\frac{\log(3)}{8C_{48}}\right)^{2}e^{-(9+6\xi)\gamma_{0}(u)},\left(\frac{1}{1296C_{45}}\right)^{2}e^{-\left(\frac{15}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)},\left(\frac{1}{1296b(u)C_{49}}\right)^{2}e^{-\frac{19}{2}\gamma_{0}(u)}\right\},

then we can derive

(7.195)

\sup_{v\in[v_{0},v(u_{n})]}|\psi|(u_{2},v)\leq\frac{1}{54}.

This further implies

(7.196)

\sup_{v\in[v_{0},v(u_{n})]}|\psi|(u_{n},v)\leq\frac{1}{6}.

Henceforth, by imposing (
7.60
), (
7.71
), (
7.194
), it suffices to only check that (
7.119
) holds. To do so, we return to (
7.109
) and instead we deduce that

(7.197)

\displaystyle\frac{r(u_{n},v^{\prime})}{r_{0}(u_{n})}\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}(u_{n},v^{\prime})\geq\left(b(u_{n})-\frac{1}{6}\right)e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}\geq\frac{1}{2}b(u_{n})e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}

for sufficiently large
n
such that
b(u_{n})>\frac{1}{3}
. On the other hand, for
\eta^{*}(u_{n},v(u_{n}))
, instead of (
7.111
), we now have

(7.198)

\displaystyle\eta^{*}(u_{n},v(u_{n}))

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{r\left(u_{n},v(u_{n})\right)}\left(\int^{v(u_{n})}_{v_{0}}\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u_{n},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{8\pi\left(1-\mu_{0}(0)\right)}{3r\left(u_{n},v(u_{n})\right)}e^{-\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}r^{2}_{0}(u_{n})\left(\int^{v(u_{n})}_{v_{0}}(\partial_{v}r)\frac{1}{r^{2}(u_{n},v^{\prime})}\frac{r^{2}(u_{n},v^{\prime})}{r^{2}_{0}(u_{n})}\frac{r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}(u_{n},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2\pi\left(1-\mu_{0}(0)\right)b(u_{n})^{2}}{3r\left(u_{n},v(u_{n})\right)}e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}r^{2}_{0}(u_{n})\left(\int^{v(u_{n})}_{v_{0}}\frac{(\partial_{v}r)(u_{n},v^{\prime})}{r^{2}(u_{n},v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\left(1-\mu_{0}(0)\right)b(u_{n})^{2}}{3}e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}\left(\frac{r_{0}(u_{n})}{r\left(u_{n},v(u_{n})\right)}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{r(u_{n},v(u_{n}))-r_{0}(u_{n})}{r_{0}(u_{n})}\right)

\displaystyle\geq C_{50}b(u_{n})^{2}\delta\left(u_{n},v(u_{n})\right)e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}

with

(7.199)

C_{50}=\frac{8\pi(1-\mu_{0}(0))}{27}.

Comparing with (
7.113
), we observe that a contradiction arises if the following inequality holds

(7.200)

\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta(u_{n},v(u_{n}))}\right)e^{-\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}+r_{0}(u_{n})<\frac{C_{50}}{C_{36}}b(u_{n})^{2}.

For this to happen, we pick

•

n
sufficiently large such that

(7.201)

r_{0}(u_{n})\leq\frac{C_{50}}{4C_{36}}b(u_{n})^{2}

as
r_{0}(u_{n})\rightarrow 0
while
b(u_{n})\rightarrow+\infty
,

•

v(u_{n})
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
verifying

(7.202)

\delta(u_{n},v(u_{n}))=\frac{1}{2}e^{-b(u_{n})^{2}\frac{C_{50}}{4C_{36}}e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}}.

It remains to show that (
7.202
) implies (
7.60
), (
7.71
), (
7.194
), (
7.195
). By employing a similar argument as in
Scenario (I)
presented above, it suffices to conduct the following modifications to (
7.188
) and (
7.189
) respectively, i.e.,

(7.203)

\delta(u_{*},v(u_{n}))\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta(u_{*},v(u_{n}))}\right)\leq 4e^{-\frac{C_{50}}{8C_{36}}b(u_{n})e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}+\frac{3}{4}(1+2\xi)\gamma_{0}(u_{n})-1}

and

(7.204)

\delta(u_{*},v(u_{n}))\leq 4e^{-\frac{C_{50}}{4C_{36}}b(u_{n})e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}+\frac{3}{2}(1+2\xi)\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}.

With (
7.203
) and (
7.204
), in an analogous manner as above, we can conclude that (
7.60
), (
7.71
), (
7.194
) hold. To verify (
7.195
), we use the fact that

\delta(u_{2},v(u_{n}))\leq 4e^{-\frac{C_{50}}{4C_{36}}b(u_{n})e^{\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}+\frac{3}{2}(1+2\xi)\gamma_{0}(u_{n})}\rightarrow 0.

This enables us that we can argue exactly as in
Scenario (I)
to deduce that (
7.195
) is satisfied. This then concludes the proof for the cases in
Scenario (II)
. And we finish the proof of Theorem
7.1
.
∎

8.
Second Instability Theorem

We proceed to prove the second instability theorem for the charged system that we are working with. To do so, we consider the case not covered in Theorem
7.1
, i.e., with
I
as defined in (
7.6
) satisfying

(8.1)

\lim_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}I(u)<+\infty

and

(8.2)

\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)=\lim_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}I(u).

In
[
21
]
, Christodoulou proved the corresponding second instability theorem for the spherically symmetric Einstein-scalar field system by asserting that he has a positive constant
p<1
such that

(8.3)

\limsup_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}\left\{\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-I(u)\right|e^{\frac{1}{2}p\gamma_{0}(u)}\right\}\neq 0.

However, for the charged case, it is not clear
a priori
whether the above assertion still holds. Here we give an argument for the charged case without asserting this assumption. The theorem to be proven in this section is recorded below

Theorem 8.1
.

(Second Instability Theorem.) Along
v=v_{0}
, assume that
\gamma_{0}(u)
is unbounded as
u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
. Furthermore, we require that the limit for
I(u)
exists and is given by (
8.2
). Under these assumptions, for
v\in[v_{0},\infty]
, we define

(8.4)

h^{2}(v):=\frac{1}{v-v_{0}}\int_{v_{0}}^{v}|\psi|^{2}(0,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime},

with
\psi
given in (
7.106
). Let
v(u)
be the function given by

(8.5)

v(u)=v_{0}+e^{-14\gamma_{0}(u)-\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u)}\right)}

for
u\in[u_{7},v_{0})
with some
u_{7}<v_{0}
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
. Also denote
g(v(u))
to be

(8.6)

g(v(u)):=V(u)+e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}(u)}+e^{-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(u)}\right)}

with

(8.7)

V(u):=\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-I(u)\right|.

Based on these requirements, if the following assumption holds

(8.8)

\limsup_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}\frac{h(v(u))}{g(v(u))}=\infty,

then the same conclusion as for Theorem
7.1
still holds.

Before we begin with the proof, we first state a useful calculus lemma.

Lemma 8.2
.

Set
X
to be an arbitrary measurable subset in
\mathbb{R}
and let functions
f:X\rightarrow[0,\infty)
,
g:X\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
h:X\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be arbitrary measurable functions defined on
X
. If we have

(8.9)

\int_{X}f|g|^{2}\geq 4\int_{X}f|h|^{2},

then the following is true:

(8.10)

\int_{X}f|g+h|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{X}f|g|^{2}\geq\int_{X}f|h|^{2}.

The proof of this lemma follows from a simple inequality

(8.11)

|g|^{2}-2|h|^{2}=|g+h-h|^{2}-2|h|^{2}\leq(|g+h|+|h|)^{2}-2|h|^{2}\leq 2|g+h|^{2}

and its integrated form

(8.12)

\displaystyle\int_{X}f|g+h|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\int_{X}f|g|^{2}-2\int_{X}f|h|^{2}\right)\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{X}f|g|^{2}\geq\int_{X}f|h|^{2}.

We now start with the proof of Theorem
8.1
.

Proof.

Following the proof of Theorem
7.1
and arguing by means of contradiction, by constructing
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
via Lemma
7.6
such that (
7.35
) and (
7.40
) hold, for each
u\in[u_{2},v_{0})
, we have

(8.13)

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u)=\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(0)-I(u)\right|e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}=V(u)e^{\gamma_{0}(u)}

with

(8.14)

\lim_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}V(u)=0.

In contrast to the scenario in the proof of Theorem
7.1
in which the limit only holds along a sequence approaching
v_{0}
, here (
8.13
) holds for every
u\in[u_{2},v_{0})
. Hence, we can make a choice of
v(u)\in[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]
for each fixed
u\in[u_{2},v_{0})
as compared to along a sequence. Furthermore, for a given
\tilde{u}\in[u_{2},v_{0})
,
\tilde{u}
plays the role of
u_{n}
in the proof of Theorem
7.1
. Hence, the relevant assumptions (
7.60
) and (
7.71
) with
u_{n}
replaced by
\tilde{u}
imply that the corresponding estimates in Lemma
7.8
and
7.9
hold with
u_{n}
replaced by
\tilde{u}
.

Next, we prove a lemma that will be used in later estimates.

Lemma 8.3
.

Given
\tilde{u}\in[u_{2},v_{0})
, suppose that there exists a corresponding value of
v(\tilde{u})\in[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]
such that, for
\delta(u,v(\tilde{u}))
, conditions (
7.60
) and (
7.71
) hold, and a new requirement

(8.15)

\delta(u,v(\tilde{u}))\leq\frac{e^{-\left(\frac{15}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}}{64C_{53}^{2}}

is satisfied for all
u\in[u_{2},\tilde{u}]
with
C_{53}
defined in (
8.22
).
Then for each
u\in[u_{2},\tilde{u}]
and
v^{\prime}\in[v_{0},v(\tilde{u})]
, we have

(8.16)

|\tilde{\gamma}(u,v^{\prime})-\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(u)|\leq 1.

Here,
\tilde{\gamma}(u,v^{\prime})=\gamma(u,v^{\prime};u_{2})
is defined in (
7.3
).

Proof.

To obtain (
8.16
), we consider
\partial_{v}\gamma
. Hence, we compute
\partial_{v}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)
. By (
7.161
), we have

(8.17)

\partial_{v}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)=J_{8}+J_{9}+J_{10}

with the expressions for
J_{8},J_{9},J_{10}
available in (
7.162
). Hence, by Fubini’s theorem and dominated convergence theorem
8
8

8

The use of Fubini’s theorem and dominated convergence theorem are guaranteed by the estimates for
J_{9},J_{10},J_{11}
, which we will derive below.
, for each
u\in[u_{2},\tilde{u}]
and
v\in[v_{0},v(\tilde{u})]
, we obtain

(8.18)

|\tilde{\gamma}(u,v)-\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(u)|\leq\int^{u}_{u_{2}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|J_{8}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})+|J_{9}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})+|J_{10}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

For the inner integral along
u=u^{\prime}
with
u^{\prime}\in[u_{2},u]
, estimates for
|J_{8}|
and
|J_{9}|
have been derived in (
7.166
) and (
7.168
). In a similar fashion, we can also obtain an estimate for
|J_{10}|
. By (
7.35
) for
\xi\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right)
, we have
0\leq\mu-\frac{2Q^{2}}{r^{2}}\leq 1
, and this further implies

(8.19)

\displaystyle\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|J_{10}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 9\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\frac{1}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{27}{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})e^{\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})}\tilde{\delta}(u^{\prime},v)

\displaystyle\leq C_{52}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})e^{3\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})}\delta(u^{\prime},v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

with

(8.20)

C_{52}:=\frac{27}{2\sqrt{2}(1-\mu_{0}(0))}.

Together with (
7.166
) and (
7.168
), we henceforth obtain

(8.21)

\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|J_{8}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})+|J_{9}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})+|J_{10}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq C_{53}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})e^{3\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})}\delta(u^{\prime},v)^{\frac{1}{2}}

with

(8.22)

C_{53}:=C_{46}+C_{47}+C_{52}.

Conducting another integration along a given incoming null curve, we appeal to (
7.72
) of Lemma
7.9
and (
8.18
) to obtain

(8.23)

\displaystyle|\tilde{\gamma}(u,v)-\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(u)|

\displaystyle\leq\int^{u}_{u_{2}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|J_{8}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})+|J_{9}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})+|J_{10}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C_{53}e^{3\gamma_{0}(u)}\int^{u}_{u_{2}}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\delta(u^{\prime},v)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{53}e^{\left(\frac{15}{4}+\frac{3\xi}{2}\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v)^{\frac{1}{2}}\int^{u}_{u_{2}}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})e^{-\frac{1}{4}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(u^{\prime})}{r_{0}(u)}\right)}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 8C_{53}e^{\left(\frac{15}{4}+\frac{3\xi}{2}\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(u,v(\tilde{u}))^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq 1,

in which the last inequality follows from our assumption (
8.15
).
∎

We are ready to demonstrate the relevant contradiction for proving Theorem
8.1
. Assuming the absence of a trapped surface, at
(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))\in\mathcal{R}
, employing (
7.110
) and (
7.113
), we get

(8.24)

\displaystyle\eta^{*}(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi}{r(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}(1-\mu)\left(\frac{r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C_{36}\delta(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))}\right)+e^{\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}r_{0}(\tilde{u})\right).

Instead of using the usual upper bounds for
\delta(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))
as in (
7.60
), (
7.71
), (
8.15
), we derive an alternative upper bound for
\delta(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))
as follows:

(8.25)

\displaystyle\delta(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}(\partial_{v}r)(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=\frac{1}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}(\partial_{v}r)(0,v^{\prime})e^{-\tilde{\gamma}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})-\gamma(u_{2},v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{eC_{56}}{2r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\frac{eC_{56}e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}}{2r_{0}(\tilde{u})}(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}).

Here

(8.26)

C_{56}(u_{2}):=\max_{v^{\prime}\in[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]}e^{|\gamma|(u_{2},v^{\prime})}<+\infty

and we have utilized (
7.8
) for the evolution of
\partial_{v}r
along
v=v^{\prime}
with
v^{\prime}\in[v_{0},v(\tilde{u})]\subset[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]
. Note that
C_{56}(u_{2})
is finite due to the fact that
\gamma(u_{2},\cdot)
is continuous on
[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]
. By the non-decreasing property of the mapping
x\mapsto x\log\left(\frac{1}{x}\right)
for
x\in\left(0,\frac{1}{e}\right]
, if we further require

(8.27)

v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}\leq\frac{2r_{0}(\tilde{u})}{e^{2}C_{56}}e^{\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})},

then by applying (
8.25
) to (
8.24
), we derive

(8.28)

\displaystyle\eta^{*}(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{36}\left(\frac{eC_{56}e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}}{2r_{0}(\tilde{u})}(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})\log\left(\frac{2r_{0}(\tilde{u})}{eC_{56}e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})}\right)+\frac{eC_{56}}{2}(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{eC_{36}C_{56}}{2r_{0}(\tilde{u})}(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})\left(r_{0}(\tilde{u})+e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}}\right)+\log\left(\frac{2r_{0}(0)}{eC_{56}}\right)+\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}{r_{0}(0)}\right)+\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})\right)\right).

To obtain the contradiction, it is natural to obtain a lower bound for
\eta^{*}(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))
. To do so, observe by the definition of
\Upxi
in (
7.105
) that

(8.29)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{0}}{r}\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}+\Upxi\right|.

This implies that, along
u=\tilde{u}
, we have

(8.30)

\displaystyle\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}(1-\mu)\left(\frac{r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}(1-\mu)\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{2}\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}+\Upxi\right|^{2}(\partial_{v}r)(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{8}{27}(1-\mu_{0}(0))e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}+\Upxi\right|^{2}(\partial_{v}r)(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{8}{27}(1-\mu_{0}(0))e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}+\Upxi\right|^{2}(\partial_{v}r)(0,v^{\prime})e^{-\tilde{\gamma}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})-\gamma(u_{2},v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime},

where we have employed (
7.8
) and a similar argument as in (
7.111
) to obtain a lower bound for
1-\mu
and
r/r_{0}
. Additionally, by applying (
2.6
) and (
8.16
) of Lemma
8.3
, we obtain

(8.31)

\displaystyle\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}(1-\mu)\left(\frac{r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\geq\frac{4C_{54}}{27e}(1-\mu_{0}(0))e^{-2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}+\Upxi\right|^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

with

(8.32)

C_{54}(u_{2})=\min_{v^{\prime}\in[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]}e^{-\gamma(u_{2},v^{\prime})}>0.

For the inequality in (
8.31
), we have used the fact that as
\gamma
is a continuous function on the rectangular region
[0,u_{2}]\times[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]\subset\mathcal{R}
.

Contrary to the proof of Theorem
7.1
, we instead require that the dominant term for the lower bound of
\eta^{*}
is
\Upxi
as we have an upper bound for
\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}
given in (
8.13
). Therefore, by Lemma
8.2
, if we can show that

(8.33)

\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}\left|\Upxi\right|^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\geq 4\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}\left|\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right|_{0}^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime},

then we have

(8.34)

\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}+\Upxi\right|^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}\left|\Upxi\right|^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

To show that (
8.33
) holds, by applying (
8.13
), we observe that

(8.35)

4\int_{v_{0}}^{v}\left|\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right|_{0}^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq 4(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})V^{2}(\tilde{u})e^{2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}.

Showing
4(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})V^{2}(\tilde{u})e^{2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\leq\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}\left|\Upxi\right|^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}
requires more work. Together with (
7.106
) and (
7.133
), for all
v\in[v_{0},v(\tilde{u})]
, we first write

(8.36)

\displaystyle\Upxi(\tilde{u},v)

\displaystyle=e^{\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})+\int^{\tilde{u}}_{0}J_{5}(u^{\prime},v)-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\left(\psi(0,v)+J_{13}(\tilde{u},v)\right).

Since
J_{5}
and
A_{u}
are real-valued, we can write

(8.37)

\displaystyle|\Upxi|^{2}(\tilde{u},v)

\displaystyle=e^{2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})+2\int^{\tilde{u}}_{0}J_{5}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\left|\psi(0,v)+J_{13}(\tilde{u},v)\right|^{2}

with

(8.38)

J_{13}(\tilde{u},v):=\int^{\tilde{u}}_{0}e^{-\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})-\int^{u^{\prime\prime}}_{0}J_{5}(u^{\prime},v)-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}A_{u}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\left(J_{4}(u^{\prime\prime},v)+J_{6}(u^{\prime\prime},v)\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}.

Integrating (
8.37
) along the incoming null curve
u=\tilde{u}
, we thus have

(8.39)

\displaystyle\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}\left|\Upxi\right|^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=e^{2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}e^{2\int^{\tilde{u}}_{0}J_{5}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}|\psi(0,v^{\prime})+J_{13}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})|^{2}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Next, for each
v^{\prime}\in[v_{0},v(\tilde{u})]
, we consider the decomposition

(8.40)

\int^{\tilde{u}}_{0}|J_{5}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}=\int^{\tilde{u}}_{u_{2}}|J_{5}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\int^{u_{2}}_{0}|J_{5}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

By the fact that
J_{5}
is continuous on
[0,u_{2}]
, we have

(8.41)

C_{55}(u_{2}):=\sup_{v^{\prime}\in[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]}\int^{u_{2}}_{0}|J_{5}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}<+\infty.

We then repeat the computations in Lemma
7.11
up to (
7.177
) to obtain

(8.42)

\int^{\tilde{u}}_{u_{2}}|J_{5}|(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\log(3).

Consequently, this implies that

(8.43)

e^{2\int^{\tilde{u}}_{0}J_{5}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\geq\frac{1}{9}e^{-2C_{55}}.

Back to (
8.39
), we thus derive

(8.44)

\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}\left|\Upxi\right|^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\geq\frac{e^{-2C_{55}}}{9}e^{2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}|\psi(0,v^{\prime})+J_{13}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})|^{2}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

To provide a lower bound for
\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}|\psi(0,v^{\prime})+J_{13}(u,v^{\prime})|^{2}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}
in (
8.44
), we first attempt to show that

(8.45)

\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}|\psi|^{2}(0,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\geq 4\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}|J_{13}|^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Referring to the expression of
|J_{13}|
in (
8.38
), together with (
8.41
),(
8.42
), for each
v^{\prime}\in[v_{0},v(\tilde{u})]
, we obtain

(8.46)

\displaystyle|J_{13}|(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\leq 3e^{C_{55}}\int^{\tilde{u}}_{0}\left|e^{-\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})}\right|\left(|J_{4}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})+|J_{6}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}.

Note that
\gamma_{0}(u)
is not necessarily non-negative for the charged case and thus necessitates the term
\left|e^{-\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})}\right|
to remain in (
8.46
). To estimate the integral on the right of (
8.46
), we first note that

(8.47)

C_{56}:=\sup_{u\in[0,v_{0})}V(u)<+\infty

because
V(u)\rightarrow 0
as
u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
and
I(u)
is continuous on any compact subsets of
[0,v_{0})
. We continue to estimate

(8.48)

\displaystyle\int_{u_{2}}^{\tilde{u}}\left|e^{-\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})}\right|\left(|J_{4}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})+|J_{6}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{u_{2}}^{\tilde{u}}\left(|J_{4}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})+|J_{6}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8C_{45}e^{\left(\frac{15}{4}+\frac{3}{2}\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))^{\frac{1}{2}}+8C_{49}C_{56}e^{\left(\frac{19}{4}+\frac{3}{2}\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}\delta(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Here we have employed (
8.13
) and similar arguments used to derive (
7.175
) and (
7.176
) in Lemma
7.11
.
To proceed, we impose

(8.49)

\delta(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))\leq\min\left\{\frac{e^{-\left(\frac{19}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}}{256C_{45}^{2}},\frac{e^{-\left(\frac{23}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}}{256C_{49}^{2}C_{56}^{2}}\right\}.

As a result, from (
8.48
), we obtain

(8.50)

\int_{u_{2}}^{\tilde{u}}\left|e^{-\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})}\right|\left(|J_{4}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})+|J_{6}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}\leq e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}.

On the other hand, for the contribution of the integral from
u=0
to
u=u_{2}
, we have

(8.51)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{u_{2}}\left|e^{-\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})}\right|\left(|J_{4}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})+|J_{6}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{57}\int^{u_{2}}_{0}|J_{4}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})+|J_{6}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}

with

(8.52)

C_{57}(u_{2}):=\max_{u^{\prime}\in[0,u_{2}]}\left|e^{-\gamma_{0}(u^{\prime})}\right|\left(1+\max_{u^{\prime}\in[0,u_{2}]}\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}(u^{\prime})\right).

To bound
|J_{4}|
, using (
7.129
) and (
7.138
), we obtain

(8.53)

|J_{4}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})\leq\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|H_{6}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime\prime}\leq C_{58}(v^{\prime}-v_{0})

with

(8.54)

\displaystyle H_{6}:=

\displaystyle\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)\partial_{v}\phi}{r_{0}}+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)\phi}{r_{0}r(1-\mu)}+(4\pi\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}^{2})\frac{\phi(-\partial_{u}r)r^{2}\im{(\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{v}\phi)}}{r_{0}(1-\mu)}

\displaystyle+\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Q(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}r)}{r_{0}(1-\mu)}+4\pi\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\frac{Qr|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}(\partial_{u}r)\phi}{r_{0}(\partial_{v}r)(1-\mu)}

being a continuous function on
[0,u_{2}]\times[v_{0},v(\tilde{u})]
. This implies that there exists a constant
C_{58}>0
such that
|H_{6}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime\prime})\leq C_{58}
for all
(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime\prime})\in[0,u_{2}]\times[v_{0},v(\tilde{u})]
. Similarly, from (
1.31
), (
7.129
), (
7.163
), together with the bound on
|H_{6}|
by
C_{58}
, we define

(8.55)

H_{7}:=J_{9}+J_{10}+J_{12}-\mathrm{i}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{2Q\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}(1-\mu)}\right)

and observe that since
H_{7}
is a continuous function on
[0,u_{2}]\times[v_{0},v(\tilde{u})]
, there exists by a constant
C_{59}>0
such that
|H_{7}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime\prime})\leq C_{59}
for all
(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime\prime})\in[0,u_{2}]\times[v_{0},v(\tilde{u})]
. Hence, we have

(8.56)

|J_{6}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime})\leq\int^{v}_{v_{0}}|H_{6}|+|H_{7}|(u^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime\prime}\leq(C_{58}+C_{59})(v^{\prime}-v_{0}).

Combining these estimates into (
8.46
), we derive the following estimate for
|J_{13}|
:

(8.57)

|J_{13}|(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\leq 3e^{C_{55}}(e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}+C_{57}(2C_{58}+C_{59})u_{2}(v^{\prime}-v_{0}))\\
\leq C_{60}(e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}+(v^{\prime}-v_{0}))

with

(8.58)

C_{60}(u_{2}):=3e^{C_{55}}\left(1+C_{57}(2C_{58}+C_{59})u_{2}\right).

Consequently, this implies

(8.59)

\displaystyle 4\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}|J_{13}|^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 8C_{60}^{2}\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}e^{-2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}+(v^{\prime}-v_{0})^{2}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 8C_{60}^{2}\left(e^{-2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}+(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})^{2}\right)(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}).

Henceforth, by the definition of
h^{2}(v(\tilde{u}))
in (
8.4
), verifying (
8.45
) is equivalent to requiring

(8.60)

h^{2}(v(\tilde{u}))\geq 8C_{60}^{2}(e^{-2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}+(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})^{2}).

Now if (
8.60
) is true, via (
8.44
) and (
8.45
), we have

(8.61)

\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}|\Upxi|^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\geq\frac{e^{-2C_{55}}}{36}e^{2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}|\psi|^{2}(0,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

With this, via (
8.35
) and (
8.61
), validating (
8.33
) is now the same as doing so for

(8.62)

4(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})V^{2}(\tilde{u})e^{2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\leq\frac{e^{-2C_{55}}}{36}e^{2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}|\psi|^{2}(0,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Henceforth, we can instead require

(8.63)

h^{2}(v(\tilde{u}))\geq 144e^{2C_{55}}V^{2}(\tilde{u}).

Recall that (
8.33
) implies (
8.34
), which by (
8.61
), in turn implies that

(8.64)

\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}+\Upxi\right|^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\geq\frac{e^{-2C_{55}}}{144}e^{2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}|\psi|^{2}(0,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Hence by (
8.30
), we have

(8.65)

\displaystyle\eta^{*}(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi}{r(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}(1-\mu)\left(\frac{r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{16\pi C_{54}(1-\mu_{0}(0))}{27er(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))}e^{-2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int_{v_{0}}^{v(\tilde{u})}\left|\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)_{0}+\Upxi\right|^{2}(\tilde{u},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\pi C_{54}e^{-2C_{55}}(1-\mu_{0}(0))}{243er(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}|\psi|^{2}(0,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2\pi C_{54}e^{-2C_{55}}(1-\mu_{0}(0))}{729er_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}|\psi|^{2}(0,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

This would contradict (
8.28
) if

(8.66)

\displaystyle\frac{eC_{36}C_{56}}{2r_{0}(\tilde{u})}(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})\left(r_{0}(\tilde{u})+e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}}\right)+\log\left(\frac{2r_{0}(0)}{eC_{56}}\right)+\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}{r_{0}(0)}\right)+\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\pi C_{54}e^{-2C_{55}}(1-\mu_{0}(0))}{729er_{0}(\tilde{u})}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}|\psi|^{2}(0,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Equivalently, this is our last requirement for
h^{2}(v(\tilde{u}))
:

(8.67)

\displaystyle h^{2}(v(\tilde{u}))\geq C_{61}\left(r_{0}(\tilde{u})+e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}}\right)+\log\left(\frac{2r_{0}(0)}{eC_{56}}\right)+\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}{r_{0}(0)}\right)+\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})\right)\right)

with

(8.68)

C_{61}(u_{2}):=\frac{729e^{2}C_{36}C_{56}}{2\pi C_{54}e^{-2C_{55}}(1-\mu_{0}(0))}.

It now remains to check that each of the assumptions made above can be verified if we take
\tilde{u}
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
. Collecting the above assumptions and using the fact that
\delta(u,v(\tilde{u}))\leq\frac{1}{2}
for each
u\in[u_{2},\tilde{u}]
, here we list the requirements

(1)

(
7.60
):

\delta(u,v(\tilde{u}))\leq\frac{C_{33}^{2}e^{2}}{4}e^{-4\gamma_{0}(u)}\quad\mbox{for all}\,\,u\in[u_{2},\tilde{u}],

(2)

(
7.71
):

\delta(u,v(\tilde{u}))\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))e^{-\gamma_{0}(u)}}{3},\frac{e^{-\frac{9}{2}\gamma_{0}(u)}}{2C_{34}}\right\}\quad\mbox{for all}\,\,u\in[u_{2},\tilde{u}],

(3)

(
8.15
):

\delta(u,v(\tilde{u}))\leq\frac{e^{-\left(\frac{15}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u)}}{64C_{53}^{2}}\quad\mbox{for all}\,\,u\in[u_{2},\tilde{u}],

(4)

(
8.49
):

\delta(\tilde{u},v(\tilde{u}))\leq\min\left\{\frac{e^{-\left(\frac{19}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}}{256C_{45}^{2}},\frac{e^{-\left(\frac{23}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}}{256C_{49}^{2}C_{56}^{2}}\right\},

(5)

(
8.27
):

v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}\leq\frac{2r_{0}(\tilde{u})}{e^{2}C_{56}}e^{\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}=\frac{2r_{0}(0)}{e^{2}C_{56}}e^{\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})-\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)},

(6)

(
8.60
):

h^{2}(v(\tilde{u}))\geq 8C_{60}^{2}(e^{-2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}+(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})^{2}),

(7)

(
8.63
):

h^{2}(v(\tilde{u}))\geq 144e^{2C_{55}}V^{2}(\tilde{u}),

(8)

(
8.67
):

h^{2}(v(\tilde{u}))\geq C_{61}\left(r_{0}(\tilde{u})+e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}}\right)+\log\left(\frac{2r_{0}(0)}{eC_{56}}\right)+\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}{r_{0}(0)}\right)+\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})\right)\right).

Recall that each of the constants that appear above is only dependent on
u_{2}
,
\tilde{\varepsilon}
and parameter
\xi\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right)
.

Before we proceed with verifying these above assumptions, we first state a useful lemma.

Lemma 8.4
.

There exist positive constants
C_{66}
and
C_{67}
given in (
8.76
) depending only on
u_{2}
and
\tilde{\varepsilon}
, such that

(8.69)

C_{66}(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})\leq\delta(u_{2},v(\tilde{u}))\leq C_{67}(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}).

Proof.

For each
(u,v)\in[0,u_{2}]\times[v_{0},v(\tilde{u})]
, we first compute

(8.70)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\log\delta

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r_{0}}-\frac{r}{r_{0}^{2}}(\partial_{u}r_{0})}{\frac{r-r_{0}}{r_{0}}}=r\frac{\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}-\frac{\partial_{u}r_{0}}{r_{0}}}{r-r_{0}}

and

(8.71)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\log\delta(u,v)=r(u,v)\frac{\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}}{\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}}=r(u,v)\frac{\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{2\mu-1-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}}{\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}}.

Here we have used (
7.160
) for calculating
\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}\right)
. Next, note that in
[0,u_{2}]\times[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]\subset\mathcal{R}
, functions
\partial_{v}r
and
\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{2\mu-1-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)
are continuous. Since
[0,u_{2}]\times[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]
is compact and
\mu<1
, then for all
(u,v)\in[0,u_{2}]\times[v_{0},v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}]
, there exist constants
C_{62},C_{63}
,
C_{64}
,
C_{65}
, such that

(8.72)

C_{62}(u_{2})\leq\frac{\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{2\mu-1-Q^{2}/r^{2}}{1-\mu}\right)(u,v)\leq C_{63}(u_{2})

and

(8.73)

0<C_{64}(u_{2})\leq\partial_{v}r(u,v)\leq C_{65}(u_{2}).

Consequently, from (
8.71
), we have

C_{62}r(u,v)\frac{v-v_{0}}{\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}}\leq\partial_{u}\log\delta(u,v)\leq C_{63}r(u,v)\frac{v-v_{0}}{\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}},

and

-\frac{|C_{62}|}{C_{64}}r(0,v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})\leq\partial_{u}\log\delta(u,v)\leq\frac{|C_{63}|}{C_{64}}r(0,v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon}).

These imply that

(8.74)

\displaystyle-\frac{|C_{62}|}{C_{64}}r(0,v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})u_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\log\left(\frac{\delta(u_{2},v)}{\delta(0,v)}\right)\leq\frac{|C_{63}|}{C_{64}}r(0,v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})u_{2}.

By setting
v=v(\tilde{u})
, we hence obtain

(8.75)

\displaystyle C_{66}(v-v_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\delta(u_{2},v)\leq C_{67}(v-v_{0})

with

(8.76)

\displaystyle C_{66}:=e^{-\frac{|C_{62}|}{C_{64}}r(0,v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})u_{2}}v_{0}>0,\quad\text{ and }\quad C_{67}:=e^{\frac{|C_{63}|}{C_{64}}r(0,v_{0}+\tilde{\varepsilon})u_{2}}v_{0}>0.

∎

We now start to verify the assumptions (1) to (8) as listed above. For a fixed
\tilde{u}
, since
v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}\rightarrow 0
as
v(\tilde{u})\rightarrow v_{0}
, we can pick
v(\tilde{u})
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
, such that it satisfies

(8.77)

v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}=e^{-A\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})-B\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}.

We claim that by choosing
A
and
B
to be suitable constants, we can verify assumptions (1) to (5). First, note that for assumption (5) to hold, with
\tilde{u}
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
, it suffices to pick
A>-1
and
B\geq 1
. To verify assumptions (1) to (4), we conduct as follows. For a given
\tilde{u}\in[u_{2},v_{0})
, we denote a set

(8.78)

S_{2}:=\left\{u\in[u_{2},\tilde{u}]:\text{ Assumptions (1) to (4) hold for all }u^{\prime}\in[u_{2},u]\right\}.

It is clear that the set
S_{2}
is closed. To show that
S_{2}
is non-empty, it suffices to verify that
u_{2}\in S_{2}
. Observe by Lemma
8.4
and (
8.77
) that

(8.79)

\delta(u_{2},v(\tilde{u}))\leq C_{67}(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})=C_{67}e^{-A\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})-B\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}.

Henceforth, assumptions (1) and (4) hold at
u=u_{2}
if we have

(8.80)

\displaystyle C_{67}e^{-A\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})-B\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{33}^{2}e^{2}}{4}e^{-4\gamma_{0}(u_{2})},

\displaystyle C_{67}e^{-A\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})-B\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{2(1-\mu_{0}(0))e^{-\gamma_{0}(u_{2})}}{3},\frac{e^{-\frac{9}{2}\gamma_{0}(u_{2})}}{2C_{34}}\right\},

\displaystyle C_{67}e^{-A\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})-B\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{-\left(\frac{15}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u_{2})}}{64C_{53}^{2}},

\displaystyle C_{67}e^{-A\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})-B\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\frac{e^{-\left(\frac{19}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u_{2})}}{256C_{45}^{2}},\frac{e^{-\left(\frac{23}{2}+3\xi\right)\gamma_{0}(u_{2})}}{256C_{49}^{2}C_{56}^{2}}\right\}.

These are true by picking
\tilde{u}
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
, since the left sides of the inequalities depend on
\tilde{u}
and they go to
0
as
\tilde{u}\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}
, while the right sides of the inequalities do not depend on
\tilde{u}
. Hence, we prove
u_{2}\in S_{2}
. We proceed to prove that
S_{2}
is open. Pick an arbitrary
u_{5}\in S_{2}
. We then have assumptions (1) to (4) hold for all
u\in[u_{2},u_{5}]
. Next, choose an arbitrary
u_{6}\in[u_{2},u_{5}]
. Analogously to (
8.25
), we get

(8.81)

\displaystyle\delta(u_{6},v(\tilde{u}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r_{0}(u_{6})}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}(\partial_{v}r)(u_{6},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=\frac{1}{r_{0}(u_{6})}\int^{v(\tilde{u})}_{v_{0}}(\partial_{v}r)(0,v^{\prime})e^{-\tilde{\gamma}(u_{6},v^{\prime})-\gamma(u_{2},v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{eC_{56}e^{-\gamma_{0}(u_{6})}}{2r_{0}(u_{6})}(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})\leq\frac{eC_{56}e^{-\gamma_{0}(u_{2})}}{2r_{0}(\tilde{u})}(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}).

Consequently, by (
8.77
), this implies

(8.82)

\displaystyle\delta(u_{6},v(\tilde{u}))\leq\frac{eC_{56}e^{-\gamma_{0}(u_{2})}}{2r_{0}(\tilde{u})}(v(\tilde{u})-v_{0})=\frac{eC_{56}e^{-\gamma_{0}(u_{2})}}{2r_{0}(0)}e^{-A\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})-(B-1)\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}.

Since
u_{6}
is arbitrary and the above estimate is uniform in
u_{6}
, we hence show that for all
u\in[u_{2},u_{5}]
, we have

(8.83)

\displaystyle\delta(u,v(\tilde{u}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{eC_{56}e^{-\gamma_{0}(u_{2})}}{2r_{0}(0)}e^{-A\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})-(B-1)\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}.

Henceforth, if we pick
A>\frac{23}{2}+3\xi
and
B\geq 1
corresponding to the most singular term among assumptions (1) to (4), then for
\tilde{u}
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
, we now obtain better estimates for assumptions (1) to (4) for all
u\in[u_{2},u_{5}]
. In other words, we show that
S_{2}
is open since
u_{5}
is an arbitrary element in
[u_{2},\tilde{u}]
. This thus implies that
S_{2}=[u_{2},\tilde{u}]
. Therefore, assumptions (1) to (4) indeed hold for all
u\in[u_{2},\tilde{u}]
. With
\xi\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right)
, choosing
A=14
and
B=1
satisfies all the requirements.

It now remains to verify assumptions (6) to (8). Plugging in our choice

v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}=e^{-14\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})-\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)},

we obtain

(8.84)

8C_{60}\left(e^{-2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}+e^{-28\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})-2\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}\right)\leq 16C_{60}e^{-2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}

and

(8.85)

\displaystyle C_{61}\left(r_{0}(\tilde{u})+e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{v(\tilde{u})-v_{0}}\right)+\log\left(\frac{2r_{0}(0)}{eC_{56}}\right)+\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}{r_{0}(0)}\right)+\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{61}\left(r_{0}(\tilde{u})+e^{-\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\left(\log\left(\frac{2r_{0}(0)}{eC_{56}}\right)+15\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{61}\left(r_{0}(0)e^{-\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}+e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\left(\log\left(\frac{2r_{0}(0)}{eC_{56}}\right)+15\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{61}\left(r_{0}(0)+\log\left(\frac{2r_{0}(0)}{eC_{56}}\right)+15\right)\max\left\{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})},e^{-\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}\right\},

where in (
8.85
) we use the fact that
\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}\leq 1
for
\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})\geq 0
.

Hence, to fulfill assumptions (6) to (8), it suffices to impose

(8.86)

h^{2}(v(\tilde{u}))\geq 16C_{60}e^{-2\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})},

(8.87)

h^{2}(v(\tilde{u}))\geq 144e^{2C_{55}}V^{2}(\tilde{u}),

and

(8.88)

h^{2}(v(\tilde{u}))\geq C_{61}\left(r_{0}(0)+\log\left(\frac{2r_{0}(0)}{eC_{56}}\right)+15\right)\cdot\max\left\{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})},e^{-\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}\right\}.

Observe that, for
\tilde{u}
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
, the three competing vanishing terms are
V^{2}(\tilde{u}),e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}
and
e^{-\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}
. Henceforth, to verify assumptions (6) to (8), for a
\tilde{u}
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
, it suffices to require that

(8.89)

\limsup_{\tilde{u}\rightarrow v_{0}}\frac{h^{2}(v(\tilde{u}))}{\left(V(\tilde{u})+e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}(\tilde{u})}+e^{-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{0}(0)}{r_{0}(\tilde{u})}\right)}\right)^{2}}=\infty.

This is exactly the condition (
8.8
) stated in Theorem
8.1
and we thus conclude the proof of Theorem
8.1
.
∎

9.
Exceptional Set and Weak Cosmic Censorship

In this section, we prove that the subset of BV initial data, which lead to the incompleteness of future null infinity, are non-generic.

As mentioned in the introduction, we prescribe quantity
\alpha
along
C_{0}^{+}
and we have

(9.1)

\alpha=\phi+\frac{\theta}{\partial_{v}r}\quad\mbox{ with }\quad\theta=r\partial_{v}\phi.

Note that if both
\phi
and
\theta
are of bounded variation along
C_{0}^{+}
, so is
\alpha
. Aligned with
[
19
]
, we define the total variation of
\phi
along
C_{0}^{+}
as

(9.2)

TV_{\{0\}\times(0,\infty)}[\phi]=2\int_{0}^{\infty}|\theta|(0,v^{\prime})\frac{\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}}{v^{\prime}}.

For notational simplicity, we denote

(9.3)

\vartheta(v):=\theta(0,v).

In this paper, we consider initial data set of
\vartheta
with
\vartheta\in BV([0,\infty),\mathrm{d}v)\cap L^{1}\left((0,\infty),\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{v}\right)
. The main conclusion of this section is

Theorem 9.1
.

(Exceptional Set.) For the spherically symmetric Einstein-Maxwell-charged scalar field system, within its initial data set
BV([0,\infty),\mathrm{d}v)\cap L^{1}\left((0,\infty),\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{v}\right)
prescribed along
C_{0}^{+}
, we have an exceptional set
\mathcal{E}
and it satisfies the following properties:

-

for each
\vartheta\notin\mathcal{E}
, the evolutional spacetime arising from
\vartheta
possesses a complete future null infinity;

-

for every
\vartheta\in\mathcal{E}
, there is a complex
2
-dimensional linear space
\Pi_{\vartheta}
belonging to the initial data set and each element in
\Pi_{\vartheta}\setminus\{\vartheta\}
does not belong to
\mathcal{E}
, i.e.,

(9.4)

\left(\Pi_{\vartheta}\setminus\{\vartheta\}\right)\bigcap\mathcal{E}=\emptyset.

Moreover, if
\vartheta,\vartheta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}
and
\vartheta\neq\vartheta^{\prime}
, then we have

(9.5)

\Pi_{\vartheta}\bigcap\Pi_{\vartheta^{\prime}}=\emptyset.

Remark 14
.

Theorem
9.1
implies that the exceptional set
\mathcal{E}
has complex codimension
2
instability in the
BV([0,\infty),\mathrm{d}v)\cap L^{1}\left((0,\infty),\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{v}\right)
initial data set.

Proof.

We define the set
\mathcal{E}
to be the union of initial datum along
C_{0}^{+}
, which does not satisfy the initial-data conditions in Theorem
7.1
and in Theorem
8.1
. Suppose that
\vartheta\in\mathcal{E}
. Then by Theorem
7.1
and Theorem
8.1
, we have

(9.6)

\left|I(u)-\vartheta(v_{0})\right|\rightarrow 0\quad\mbox{ as }\quad u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}

and

(9.7)

\limsup_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}\frac{h(v(u))}{g(v(u))}<+\infty

with
v(u),h(v),g(v)
defined in (
8.5
), (
8.4
), (
8.6
), respectively. We then choose two real-valued non-negative functions
f_{1},f_{2}\in L^{1}\left((0,\infty),\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{v}\right)
. For
f_{1}
, we further require it to vanish on
[0,v_{0})
, to be absolutely continuous on
[v_{0},\infty)
, and to satisfy

(9.8)

\lim_{v\rightarrow v_{0}^{+}}f_{1}(v)=1.

For
f_{2}
, we choose it to be absolutely continuous on
[0,\infty)
, vanishing on
[0,v_{0}]
, and with property

(9.9)

\limsup_{v\rightarrow v_{0}^{+}}\frac{1}{g(v)^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{v-v_{0}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\left(\frac{v^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\right)^{2}f_{2}^{2}(v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right)=\infty.

With
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
, we then consider the following initial data

(9.10)

\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}=\vartheta+\lambda_{1}f_{1}+\lambda_{2}f_{2}.

Since
\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}
and
\vartheta
coincides for
v\in[0,v_{0})
, the corresponding solutions coincide in
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{0})\setminus\{v=v_{0}\}
. This thus permits a common definition of
\gamma_{0}
and
I(u)
as described in (
7.1
) and (
7.6
) for both
\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}
and
\vartheta
. Applying (
9.6
) and (
9.8
), taking limits as
v\rightarrow v_{0}^{+}
while respecting (
8.5
), these yield

(9.11)

\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}(v_{0})=\lim_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}I(u)+\lambda_{1}.

If
\lambda_{1}\neq 0
, then we have
\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}(v_{0})\neq\lim_{u\rightarrow 0^{-}}I(u)
. By Theorem
7.1
, we get
\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}\notin\mathcal{E}
.

If
\lambda_{1}=0
and
\lambda_{2}\neq 0
, we encounter the scenario

(9.12)

\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}(v_{0})=\lim_{u\rightarrow v_{0}^{-}}I(u)=\vartheta(v_{0})

and for
v\in(v_{0},\infty)
we have

(9.13)

\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}(v)=\vartheta(v)+\lambda_{2}f_{2}(v).

Recalling (
7.107
), we then get

(9.14)

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}(v)^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{2e^{-2\gamma_{0}(0)}}{v-v_{0}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\left|\frac{v^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}(v^{\prime})-\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}(v_{0})\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2e^{-2\gamma_{0}(0)}}{v-v_{0}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\left|\lambda_{2}\left(\frac{v^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\right)f_{2}(v^{\prime})-\left(\frac{v^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\vartheta(v^{\prime})-\vartheta(v_{0})\right)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

By (
8.11
), we hence derive

(9.15)

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{h}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}(v)^{2}}{g(v)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{g(v)^{2}}\frac{2e^{-2\gamma_{0}(0)}}{v-v_{0}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\left|\frac{v^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}(v^{\prime})-\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}(v_{0})\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{2}e^{-2\gamma_{0}(0)}\frac{1}{g(v)^{2}}\frac{1}{v-v_{0}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\left(\frac{v^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\right)^{2}f_{2}^{2}(v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}-2e^{-2\gamma_{0}(0)}\frac{1}{g(v)^{2}}\frac{1}{v-v_{0}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\left|\frac{v^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\vartheta(v^{\prime})-\vartheta(v_{0})\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{2}e^{-2\gamma_{0}(0)}\frac{1}{g(v)^{2}}\frac{1}{v-v_{0}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\left(\frac{v^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\right)^{2}f_{2}^{2}(v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}-2e^{-2\gamma_{0}(0)}\frac{h(v)^{2}}{g(v)^{2}}.

Applying (
9.7
) and (
9.9
), inequality (
9.15
) further implies that

(9.16)

\limsup_{v\rightarrow v_{0}^{+}}\frac{\tilde{h}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}(v)^{2}}{g(v)^{2}}=\infty.

Theorem
8.1
then applies to
\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}
and hence we demonstrate
\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}\notin\mathcal{E}
.

We then proceed to prove that the complex
2
-dimensional linear subspace
\Pi_{\vartheta}
defined by

(9.17)

\Pi_{\vartheta}:=\{\vartheta_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}:(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\}

intersects
\mathcal{E}
only at
(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})=(0,0)
, which corresponds to
\vartheta
itself. Suppose that
\vartheta,\vartheta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}
and
\Pi_{\vartheta},\Pi_{\vartheta^{\prime}}
intersect at

(9.18)

\tilde{\vartheta}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}=\tilde{\vartheta}^{\prime}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})},

then by the construction of
f_{1},f_{2}
, we have

\vartheta=\vartheta^{\prime}
on
[0,v_{0})
and hence we obtain the same functions
\gamma_{0}(u),I(u),g(v)
. Employing (
9.6
), we also get

(9.19)

\vartheta(v_{0})=\vartheta^{\prime}(v_{0})=\lim_{u\rightarrow 0^{-}}I(u).

By (
9.10
) and (
9.18
) with
v=v_{0}
, we then obtain

(9.20)

\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{1}^{\prime}.

Equation (
9.18
) also implies

(9.21)

\vartheta-\vartheta^{\prime}=(\lambda_{2}^{\prime}-\lambda_{2})f_{2}.

For each
v\in[v_{0},\infty)
, this gives

(9.22)

\left|\frac{v}{v_{0}}\vartheta(v)-\vartheta(0)-\left(\frac{v}{v_{0}}\vartheta^{\prime}(v)-\vartheta^{\prime}(0)\right)\right|^{2}=\left(\frac{v}{v_{0}}\right)^{2}|\lambda_{2}^{\prime}-\lambda_{2}|^{2}f_{2}^{2}(v).

Recall the first line of (
9.14
), for
\vartheta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}
, we have

(9.23)

h^{\prime}(v):=\frac{2e^{-2\gamma_{0}(0)}}{v-v_{0}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\left|\frac{v^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\vartheta^{\prime}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}(v^{\prime})-\vartheta^{\prime}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}(v_{0})\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Together with (
9.22
), it then implies that

(9.24)

\displaystyle|\lambda_{2}^{\prime}-\lambda_{2}|^{2}\frac{1}{g(v)^{2}}\frac{1}{v-v_{0}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\left(\frac{v^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\right)^{2}f_{2}^{2}(v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{g(v)^{2}}\frac{1}{v-v_{0}}\int^{v}_{v_{0}}\left|\frac{v}{v_{0}}\vartheta(v)-\vartheta(0)-\left(\frac{v}{v_{0}}\vartheta^{\prime}(v)-\vartheta^{\prime}(0)\right)\right|^{2}(v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq e^{2\gamma_{0}(0)}\left(\frac{h(v)^{2}}{g(v)^{2}}+\frac{h^{\prime}(v)^{2}}{g(v)^{2}}\right).

Note that (
9.7
) implies

(9.25)

\limsup_{v\rightarrow v_{0}^{+}}\frac{h(v)}{g(v)}<+\infty\quad\text{ and }\quad\limsup_{v\rightarrow v_{0}^{+}}\frac{h^{\prime}(v)}{g(v)}<+\infty.

Back to (
9.24
), utilizing (
9.9
), (
9.25
) and letting
v\rightarrow v_{0}^{+}
, we then conclude that
\lambda_{2}=\lambda_{2}^{\prime}
. With (
9.21
), this implies that
\vartheta=\vartheta^{\prime}
. Hence, we prove that
\Pi_{\vartheta}\bigcap\Pi_{\vartheta^{\prime}}=\emptyset
unless
\vartheta=\vartheta^{\prime}
.

It remains to prove that, once the initial-data conditions for Theorem
7.1
(first instability theorem) or Theorem
8.1
(second instability theorem) are satisfied and a trapped surface is formed, then the corresponding dynamical spacetime is blessed with a complete future null infinity. In
[
27
]
Dafermos identified six conditions:
\mathbf{A}^{\prime},\mathbf{B}^{\prime},\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\prime},\mathbf{\Delta}^{\prime},\mathbf{E}^{\prime},\mathbf{\Sigma T}^{\prime}
and proved that, for spherically symmetric Einstein field equations, once these six conditions are fulfilled, then the existence of a trapped surface would imply the completeness of future null infinity. For spherically symmetric Einstein-Maxwell-charged scalar field system, condition
\mathbf{\Sigma T}^{\prime}
(a local extension principle at
r(u,v)>0
) was proved in Section of
[
35
]
. With asymptotically flat initial data, in our setting, conditions
\mathbf{A}^{\prime},\mathbf{B}^{\prime},\mathbf{\Delta}^{\prime},\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
are also automatically satisfied. It remains to meet the condition
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\prime}
(positive energy condition), i.e.,
T_{uu}\geq 0,T_{vv}\geq 0,T_{uv}\geq 0
. Meanwhile, for our charged system, a direct calculation gives

(9.26)

T_{uu}=|D_{u}\phi|^{2}\geq 0,\quad T_{vv}=|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}\geq 0,\quad T_{uv}=\frac{Q^{2}}{16\pi r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\geq 0,

which verifies condition
\mathbf{B}^{\prime}
. Therefore, for the generic scenarios (when Theorem
7.1
or Theorem
8.1
is activated), we conclude the completeness of future null infinity. Hence the weak cosmic censorship for the spherically symmetric Einstein-Maxwell-charged scalar field system is proved.

∎

Appendix A
Alternative Proof of the Almost Scale-Critical Trapped-Surface-Formation Criteria for the Einstein-(Real) Scalar Field System

For the spherically symmetric Einstein-(real) scalar field system, by setting
Q\equiv 0
in the system (
1.11
) to (
1.19
), we get

(A.1)

r\partial_{v}\partial_{u}r+\partial_{v}r\partial_{u}r=-\frac{\Omega^{2}}{4},

(A.2)

\partial_{u}(\Omega^{-2}\partial_{u}r)=-4\pi r\Omega^{-2}|\partial_{u}\phi|^{2},

(A.3)

\partial_{v}(\Omega^{-2}\partial_{v}r)=-4\pi r\Omega^{-2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2},

(A.4)

r\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\phi+\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi+\partial_{v}r\partial_{u}\phi=0.

Define

m(u,v):=\frac{r}{2}\cdot(1+4\Omega^{-2}\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r)(u,v)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mu(u,v):=\frac{2m(u,v)}{r(u,v)}.

Analogously to (
1.19
), (
1.20
), (
1.26
), we deduce

(A.5)

\partial_{v}\partial_{u}r=\frac{1}{r}\left(\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right)\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r,

(A.6)

\partial_{u}\left(\frac{1-\mu}{\partial_{v}r}\right)=\frac{4\pi r(\partial_{u}\phi)^{2}(1-\mu)}{(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)},

(A.7)

\partial_{v}m=\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)(\partial_{v}\phi)^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}.

Furthermore, for any coordinate patch
[u_{0},v_{0})\times[v_{1},v_{2}]
with
u_{0}<v_{0}
and
v_{1}<v_{2}
, we can define

\delta(u):=\frac{r_{2}(u)-r_{1}(u)}{r_{1}(u)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\eta(u):=\frac{2(m_{2}(u)-m_{1}(u))}{r_{2}(u)}

and let
\delta_{0}:=\delta(u_{0})
and
\eta_{0}:=\eta(u_{0}).

Adopting the proof strategy in Theorem
4.1
, we give a shorter new proof of the below theorem, which was first achieved by Christodoulou in
[
18
]
.

\mathcal{O}
\Gamma
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
C_{0}^{+}
\mathcal{B}_{0}
\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}
v_{1}
\mathcal{A}
R^{\prime}
v_{2}
u=u_{0}
u=u_{*}
\mathcal{O}
\Gamma
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
C_{0}^{+}
\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}
v_{1}
\mathcal{A}
R^{\prime}
v_{2}
u=u_{0}
u=u_{*}

Figure 14.
Diagram for the proof of Theorem
A.1
. The rectangle region
R^{\prime}
used in the proof below is indicated as above. The apparent horizon
\mathcal{A}
is represented with a dotted curve. The scenario on the left corresponds to
|\mathcal{B}_{0}|\geq v_{2}-v_{1}
, while the scenario on the right corresponds to
|\mathcal{B}_{0}|<v_{2}-v_{1}
, and
\mathcal{B}_{0}
is allowed to be an empty set.

Theorem A.1
.

Let
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}=(v_{1},v_{1})
be the future ending point of
\Gamma
as portrayed in Figure
14
. Let
v_{2}>v_{1}
and consider the future-directed incoming null curve along
v=v_{2}
. With
\delta_{0}\in\left(0,1\right)
, along
u=u_{0}
if we have

(A.8)

\eta_{0}>\frac{2\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{0}}\right),

then there exists
u_{*}\in(u_{0},v_{0})
along
v=v_{2}
in the future of
C_{0}^{+}
, such that a MOTS or a trapped surface is guaranteed to form along
[u_{0},u_{*}]\times\{v_{2}\}
.

Proof.

Let
u_{*}\in[u_{0},v_{1})
be an arbitrary constant such that
(u_{*},v_{2})\in\mathcal{Q}
. Denote
R^{\prime}:=[u_{0},u_{*}]\times[v_{1},v_{2}]
and define
x(u):=\frac{r_{2}(u)}{r_{2}(u_{0})}
. We will show that condition (
A.8
) along
u=u_{0}
guarantees the existence of a MOTS or a trapped surface in
R^{\prime}
. Below, we present a mechanism in the regular (untrapped) region, that would lead to a MOTS or a trapped surface formation in
R^{\prime}
.

If there is already a MOTS or a trapped surface in
R^{\prime}
, then we are done. Otherwise, assuming the absence of a MOTS or a trapped surface in
R^{\prime}
, we proceed to compute

(A.9)

\frac{\mathrm{d}\eta}{\mathrm{d}x}=\frac{\frac{\mathrm{d}\eta}{\mathrm{d}u}}{\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{\mathrm{d}u}}=\frac{\partial_{u}\left(\frac{2(m_{2}-m_{1})}{r_{2}}\right)}{\frac{\partial_{u}r_{2}}{r_{2}(u_{0})}}=-\frac{\eta}{x}-\frac{2}{x(\partial_{u}r_{2})}\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}m\;\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

By (
A.7
), we have

(A.10)

\displaystyle-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}m=

\displaystyle-\partial_{u}\left(\frac{2\pi r^{2}(1-\mu)(\partial_{v}\phi)^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}\right)=-2\pi\left(\partial_{u}\left(\frac{(1-\mu)}{\partial_{v}r}\right)r^{2}(\partial_{v}\phi)^{2}+\left(\frac{2(1-\mu)}{\partial_{v}r}\right)r(\partial_{v}\phi)\partial_{u}(r\partial_{v}\phi)\right)

\displaystyle=8\pi^{2}r^{3}(1-\mu)\left((\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)\frac{(\partial_{u}\phi)^{2}(\partial_{v}\phi)^{2}}{(\partial_{u}r)^{2}(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}-\frac{1}{2\pi r^{2}}(\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)\frac{(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}\phi)}{(\partial_{v}r)(\partial_{u}r)}\right).

With an elementary inequality
-x^{2}+ax\leq\frac{a^{2}}{4}
, we then derive

(A.11)

\displaystyle\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}m\;\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}8\pi^{2}r^{3}(1-\mu)(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)\left(-\frac{(\partial_{u}\phi)^{2}(\partial_{v}\phi)^{2}}{(\partial_{u}r)^{2}(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}+\frac{1}{2\pi r^{2}}\frac{(\partial_{v}\phi)(\partial_{u}\phi)}{(\partial_{v}r)(\partial_{u}r)}\right)\;\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}8\pi^{2}r^{3}(1-\mu)(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)\left(\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}r^{4}}\right)\;\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)_{2}}{2}\int^{v_{2}}_{v_{1}}\frac{\partial_{v}r}{r}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)_{2}}{2}\log\left(1+\frac{r_{2}(u)-r_{1}(u)}{r_{1}(u)}\right)\leq\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)_{2}}{2}\delta(u).

In (
A.11
), we use the fact that
\partial_{v}(-\partial_{u}r)\geq 0
in
R^{\prime}
. Furthermore, for all
(u,v)\in R^{\prime}
, we also have

(A.12)

\partial_{v}r(u,v)\leq\partial_{v}r(u_{0},v).

This further implies that, for all
u\in[u_{0},u_{*}]
, we get

(A.13)

r_{2}(u)-r_{1}(u)\leq r_{2}(u_{0})-r_{1}(u_{0}).

For
\delta(u)
, we hence derive

(A.14)

\displaystyle\delta(u)=\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}-1

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{r_{2}-(r_{2}-r_{1})}\leq\frac{r_{2}(u_{0})-r_{1}(u_{0})}{r_{2}(u)-(r_{2}(u_{0})-r_{1}(u_{0}))}\leq\frac{\delta(u_{0})}{\frac{r_{2}(u)}{r_{1}(u_{0})}-\delta(u_{0})}=\frac{\delta_{0}}{x(u)(1+\delta_{0})-\delta_{0}}.

Returning to (
A.9
), we obtain

(A.15)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\eta}{\mathrm{d}x}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\eta}{x}+\frac{\delta(u)}{x}\leq-\frac{\eta}{x}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{x(x(1+\delta_{0})-\delta_{0})},

which simplifies to

(A.16)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}(x\eta)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta_{0}}{(x(1+\delta_{0})-\delta_{0})}.

Integrating from any given
x\in\left(x(u_{*}),1\right)
to
1
and using the fact that
\eta(x)\leq\mu_{2}(x)
, we arrive at

\eta_{0}-x\eta(x)\leq\int^{1}_{x}\frac{\delta_{0}}{(s(1+\delta_{0})-\delta_{0})}\mathrm{d}s

and

(A.17)

\displaystyle\mu_{2}(x)\geq\eta(x)\geq\frac{1}{x}\left(\eta_{0}-\frac{\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}\log\left(\frac{1}{x(1+\delta_{0})-\delta_{0}}\right)\right).

Next, we consider the following two scenarios:

•

Scenario 1:
|\mathcal{B}_{0}|\geq v_{2}-v_{1}
. This corresponds to the illustration on the left of Figure
A.1
. For this case, we are allowed to set
u_{*}
to be arbitrarily close to
v_{1}
. Note that at
(u_{*},v_{2})
we have

(A.18)

x(u_{*})\leq\frac{r_{1}(u_{*})}{r_{2}(u_{0})}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{1+\frac{r_{2}(u_{0})-r_{1}(u_{0})}{r_{1}(u_{0})}}=\frac{r_{1}(u_{*})}{r_{2}(u_{0})}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}.

Employing (
A.18
) and the fact that
r_{1}(u_{*})\rightarrow 0
as
u_{*}\rightarrow v_{1}^{-}
, we obtain
x(u_{*})\leq\frac{p\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}
for any
p>1
.

•

Scenario 2:
|\mathcal{B}_{0}|<v_{2}-v_{1}
. This case is illustrated in the right picture of Figure
A.1
. As seen from the picture, when discussing the point
(u_{*},v_{2})
, we may not be able to pick
u_{*}
to be arbitrarily close to
v_{1}
due to the spacelike nature of
\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}
. Nonetheless,
r_{2}(u)\rightarrow 0
as
(u,v_{2})
approaches any point on
\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}
. Thus, if we choose
u_{*}
along
v=v_{2}
to be sufficiently close to
\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}
, we then get
x(u_{*})\leq\frac{p\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}
for any
p>1
.

In both cases, since
x(u)
is a decreasing continuous function on
[u_{0},u_{*}]
, there exists
\tilde{u}(k)
such that
x(\tilde{u}(k))=\frac{k\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}
for any
k>1
. We then evaluate (
A.17
) at this point
(\tilde{u}(k),v_{2})
and get

(A.19)

\mu(\tilde{u}(k),v_{2})\geq\frac{1}{k}\left(\eta_{0}\left(\frac{1+\delta_{0}}{\delta_{0}}\right)-\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{0}}\right)+\log(k-1)\right).

Hence, we would have
\mu(\tilde{u}(k),v_{2})\geq 1
if

(A.20)

\eta_{0}\left(\frac{1+\delta_{0}}{\delta_{0}}\right)-\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{0}}\right)\geq k-\log\left(k-1\right).

Observe that, with
k>1
, the lower bound in (
A.20
) is attained at
k=2
. Hence, we have
\mu(\tilde{u}(2),v_{2})\geq 1
if we request

(A.21)

\eta_{0}\geq\frac{2\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{0}}\right).

It is exactly the requirement in (
A.8
) and this concludes the proof of Theorem
A.1
.
∎

Appendix B
C^{1}
Extension Theorem with Doppler Exponent
\gamma
for the Einstein-(Real) Scalar Field System

For the spherically symmetric Einstein-(real) scalar field system, we have the same set of equations from (
A.1
) to (
A.7
). In addition, we have

(B.1)

\partial_{u}\mu=-\frac{4\pi r(1-\mu)|\partial_{u}\phi|^{2}}{-\partial_{u}r}+\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\mu

and denote

(B.2)

\alpha:=\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r},\quad\quad\alpha^{\prime}:=\frac{\partial_{v}\alpha}{\partial_{v}r}.

We define the
Doppler exponent
in the (real) scalar field system to be

(B.3)

\gamma(u,v):=\int^{u}_{0}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

In particular, its partial derivative with respect to
v
is given by

(B.4)

\partial_{v}\gamma(u,v)=\int_{0}^{u}\frac{\partial_{v}r\partial_{u}r}{r^{2}}\frac{1}{1-\mu}\left(2\mu-4\pi\frac{r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

if interchanging between the derivative and integral is allowed. Furthermore, we have the following
Doppler identity

(B.5)

(\partial_{v}r)(0,v)=(\partial_{v}r)(u,v)e^{\gamma(u,v)}.

For the spherically symmetric Einstein-(real) scalar field system below, we proceed to prove

Theorem B.1
.

(
C^{1}
Extension Theorem with Doppler Exponent
\gamma
.) We prescribe
C^{1}
initial data
\alpha
along
C_{0}^{+}
. Let
v_{*}>0
and assume that for every
\hat{v}\in(0,v_{*})
, a
C^{1}
solution exists on
\mathcal{D}(0,\hat{v})
. If it obeys

(B.6)

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})}\gamma<+\infty,

then a
C^{1}
solution can be extended to
\mathcal{D}(0,\tilde{v})
with
\tilde{v}>v_{*}
.

Proof.
By the hypothesis, there is a positive constant
G
such that

(B.7)

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})}\gamma(u,v;0)<G.

For each
u\in[0,v_{*})
, we define the quantity

(B.8)

B^{\prime}(u):=\sup_{v\in[u,v_{*}]}|\alpha^{\prime}|(u,v).

We will proceed to show that this quantity remains bounded uniformly in
u
, which in turn implies

(B.9)

B^{\prime}:=\sup_{u\in[0,v_{*})}\sup_{v\in[u,v_{*}]}|\alpha^{\prime}|(u,v)=\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})}|\alpha^{\prime}|<+\infty

and the solution extends as a
C^{1}
solution up to
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})
.

Estimates for
B^{\prime}(u)
.
To estimate
B^{\prime}(u)
