, we adopt the following strategy. We first compute
\partial_{u}\alpha
and get

(B.10)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\alpha^{\prime}=2\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\alpha^{\prime}+3\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r^{2}}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)+4\pi\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r^{2}}\frac{1}{1-\mu}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}.

Henceforth, for any
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})
, we obtain

(B.11)

|\alpha^{\prime}|(u,v)\leq E^{\prime}(v_{*})+\sum_{i=1}^{3}|I_{i}|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\quad\text{ with }\quad E^{\prime}(v_{*}):=\sup_{\{0\}\times[0,v_{*}]}|\alpha^{\prime}|

and
|I_{i}|
satisfying the following estimates:

(B.12)

|I_{1}|(u,v)\leq 2\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)|\alpha^{\prime}|\left(\frac{1}{r}\right)\left(\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(B.13)

|I_{2}|(u,v)\leq 3\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(B.14)

|I_{3}|(u,v)\leq 4\pi\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}\right)\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

We continue by obtaining key estimates for the following terms:

\frac{1}{1-\mu},\,|\alpha^{\prime}|,\,\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r},\,\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}\mbox{ for all }(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*}).

This is done by employing the following method for each of these terms:

\frac{1}{1-\mu}
:

By utilizing (
B.1
), we compute

\partial_{u}\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}\right)=\frac{\partial_{u}\mu}{(1-\mu)^{2}}=\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}\left(-\frac{4\pi r|\partial_{u}\phi|^{2}}{(-\partial_{u}r)}+\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right).

Together with
\partial_{u}r<0
, we obtain

(B.15)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\mu(u,v)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\mu(0,v)}\exp\left({\int^{u}_{0}\left(-\frac{4\pi r|\partial_{u}\phi|^{2}}{(-\partial_{u}r)}+\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\mu(0,v)}\exp\left({\int^{u}_{0}\left(\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}}\right).

Employing the definition of
\gamma
in (
B.3
) and
\mu_{*}
in (
2.15
), we hence get

(B.16)

\frac{1}{1-\mu}(u,v)\leq\frac{1}{1-\mu_{*}}e^{G}.

|\alpha^{\prime}|
:

By the definition of
B^{\prime}(u)
in (
B.8
), for each
(u,v)\in\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})
, we have

(B.17)

|\alpha^{\prime}|(u,v)\leq B^{\prime}(u).

\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}
:

Applying the definition of
\alpha
in (
B.2
) and
r\phi|_{\Gamma}=0
, we can rewrite
\phi
as

(B.18)

\phi(u,v)=\frac{1}{r}\int^{v}_{u}(\alpha\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime},

which in turn implies that

(B.19)

\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v)=\alpha(u,v)-\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}(\alpha\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

Using the fact that
r(u,u)=r|_{\Gamma}=0
and (
B.8
), we obtain

(B.20)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\alpha(u,v)-\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}(\alpha\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\left|\alpha(u,v)-\alpha(u,v^{\prime})\right|\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\left(\int^{v}_{v^{\prime}}\left|\partial_{v}\alpha(u,v^{\prime\prime})\right|\mathrm{d}v^{\prime\prime}\right)\partial_{v}r(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int^{v}_{u}\left(\int^{v}_{v^{\prime}}B^{\prime}(u)(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime\prime}\right)(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=\frac{B^{\prime}(u)r(u,v)}{2}.

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}:

For this term, we proceed with a different strategy as follows. Observe that

(B.21)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}=\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}-\phi\right)^{3}=3\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\partial_{v}(r\phi)}{\partial_{v}r}-\phi\right)

\displaystyle=3\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{2}\left(\alpha^{\prime}\partial_{v}r-\partial_{v}\phi\right)=3\alpha^{\prime}\frac{r^{2}(\partial_{v}\phi)^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{3\partial_{v}r}{r}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}.

This then implies

(B.22)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(r^{3}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}\right)=3r^{2}\partial_{v}r\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}+r^{3}\left(3\alpha^{\prime}\frac{r^{2}(\partial_{v}\phi)^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{3\partial_{v}r}{r}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}\right)=3\alpha^{\prime}r^{3}\left(\frac{r^{2}(\partial_{v}\phi)^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}\right).

Since
r^{3}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}|_{\Gamma}=0
, together with (
B.20
), equation (
A.7
),
\partial_{v}r\geq 0,\partial_{v}m\geq 0
, we thus obtain

(B.23)

\displaystyle r^{3}\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}

\displaystyle(u,v)\leq\int_{u}^{v}\left|\partial_{v}\left(r^{3}\left(\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}\right)\right|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq 3B^{\prime}(u)r^{3}(u,v)\int_{u}^{v}\frac{r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2\pi}B^{\prime}(u)r^{3}(u,v)\int^{v}_{u}\frac{\partial_{v}m}{1-\mu}(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\frac{3e^{G}}{\pi(1-\mu_{*})}B^{\prime}(u)r^{4}(u,v)\mu(u,v),

which in turn implies

(B.24)

\left(\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}\right)^{3}(u,v)\leq C_{3}^{\prime}B^{\prime}(u)r(u,v)\mu(u,v)

with the constant
C_{3}^{\prime}
given by

(B.25)

C_{3}^{\prime}:=\frac{3e^{G}}{\pi(1-\mu_{*})}.

Employing these estimates, we can now obtain an estimate for each of the terms
|I_{i}|
as follows. For
I_{1}
, we have

(B.26)

\displaystyle|I_{1}|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

For
I_{2}
, by (
B.17
), we deduce that

(B.27)

\displaystyle|I_{2}|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq 3\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r^{2}}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\left|\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\frac{3}{2}\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Last but not least, for
I_{3}
, we obtain

(B.28)

\displaystyle|I_{3}|(u,v)

\displaystyle\leq 4\pi\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r^{2}}\frac{1}{1-\mu}\left|\frac{r\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}\right|^{3}(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq 4\pi C_{3}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{u}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Consequently, by substituting these estimates back to (
B.11
), we derive

(B.29)

|\alpha^{\prime}|(u,v)\leq E^{\prime}(v_{*})+\left(\frac{7}{2}+4\pi C_{3}^{\prime}\right)\int_{0}^{u}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},v)B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

For each
u
, we pick
\tilde{v}\in[u,v_{*}]
such that the supremum for (
B.8
) is attained at
(u,\tilde{v})
. Since (
B.29
) is true for all
v\in[u,v_{*}]
, we deduce that

(B.30)

B^{\prime}(u)\leq E^{\prime}(v_{*})+\left(\frac{7}{2}+4\pi C_{3}^{\prime}\right)\int_{0}^{u}\left(\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}\right)(u^{\prime},\tilde{v})B^{\prime}(u^{\prime})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

Applying Grönwall’s inequality, we hence prove

(B.31)

\displaystyle B^{\prime}(u)\leq E^{\prime}(v_{*})\exp\left(\left(\frac{7}{2}+4\pi C_{3}^{\prime}\right)\int^{u}_{0}\frac{-\partial_{u}r}{r}\frac{\mu}{1-\mu}(u^{\prime},\tilde{v})\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\right)\leq E^{\prime}(v_{*})\exp\left(\left(\frac{7}{2}+4\pi C_{3}^{\prime}\right)G\right).

Observe that the upper bound obtained in (
B.31
) is uniform in
u
and
\tilde{v}
. Therefore, from (
B.9
), we conclude

(B.32)

B^{\prime}\leq E^{\prime}(v_{*})\exp\left(\left(\frac{7}{2}+4\pi C_{3}^{\prime}\right)G\right)<+\infty,

which in turn implies that the solution extends as a
C^{1}
solution up to
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*})
.

Extension Beyond Boundary.

Next, we demonstrate that there exists some
\xi^{\prime}>0
, such that the solution extends as a
C^{1}
solution to
\mathcal{D}(0,v_{*}+\xi^{\prime})
. To do so, we estimate the change in
\gamma
across a rectangular region
\{(u,v):u\in[0,v_{*}],v\in[v_{*},v_{*}+\xi]\}
for some
\xi>0
. An illustration of the set-up is in Figure
8
.

For any given
u_{1}\in[0,v_{*})
, we consider the rectangular strip given by

(B.33)

U_{\xi}(u_{1})=[0,u_{1})\times[v_{*},v_{*}+\xi].

Similar to (
B.9
), we define

(B.34)

\overline{B^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{1}):=\sup_{U_{\xi}(u_{1})}(|\alpha^{\prime}|).

In addition, we denote

(B.35)

\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)=\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*})\cup U_{\xi}(u_{1}).

From the first part of the proof, we have that the quantity
B^{\prime}
in (
B.9
) is bounded, independent of
u_{1}
, and obeys the following estimates from (
B.32
):

(B.36)

B^{\prime}(u)\leq E^{\prime}(v_{*})\exp\left(\left(\frac{7}{2}+4\pi C_{3}^{\prime}(G)\right)G\right)=:C_{12}(G,v_{*}).

We remark that
C_{3}^{\prime}
is defined in (
B.36
) and the newly defined
C_{12}
depends only on
v_{*}
and
G
. Fix some
\hat{\xi}>0
. We then have the following lemma:

Lemma B.2
.

For any
u_{1}\in(0,v_{*})
and
\xi\in(0,\hat{\xi}]
, if
\gamma
satisfies

(B.37)

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)}\gamma\leq 3G,

then we have

(B.38)

\overline{B^{\prime}_{\xi}}(u_{1})\leq\max\{C_{12}(G,v_{*}),C_{12}(3G,v_{*}+\hat{\xi})\}=:B_{2}.

The proof of this lemma follows in the same fashion as for deriving an estimate for
B^{\prime}(u)
, noting that the bounds established in the region
U_{\xi}(u_{1})
would depend on the initial data along
\{0\}\times[v_{*},v_{*}+\xi]
and the uniform upper bound for
\gamma
is now
3G
, independent of
u_{1}
and
\xi
.

With the above lemma, we continue to prove that the
C^{1}
solution extends beyond the domain of dependence
\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*})
. First, we define

(B.39)

u_{2}=\sup\left\{u_{1}\in[0,v_{*}):\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)}\gamma\leq 3G\right\}.

By the definition of
u_{2}
, we then have either
u_{2}=v_{*}
or

(B.40)

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)}\gamma=3G

is attained at some
u_{2}\in[u_{0},v_{*})
. We now show that for
\xi
sufficiently small, the latter will not happen. Suppose for a contradiction that the latter happens. Subsequently, set
u_{1}=u_{2}
as in the hypothesis of Lemma
B.2
. Analogous to the proof of Lemma
B.2
, in the region
U_{\xi}(u_{2})
, we first obtain

(B.41)

\frac{|\partial_{v}\phi|}{\partial_{v}r}(u,v)\leq\frac{B_{2}}{2}.

We can derive an estimate for
m(u,v)
using (
B.41
) as follows. By (
A.7
) and
m|_{\Gamma}=0
, these imply

(B.42)

\displaystyle m(u,v)\leq 2\pi\int^{v}_{u}\frac{r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq\frac{\pi B_{2}^{2}}{2}\int^{v}_{u}r^{2}(\partial_{v}r)(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}=\frac{\pi B_{2}^{2}}{6}r^{3}(u,v)

and hence

(B.43)

\mu(u,v)\leq\frac{\pi B_{2}^{2}}{3}r^{2}(u,v).

In addition, from (
B.16
) but with
G
replaced by
3G
, we have

(B.44)

\frac{1}{1-\mu}(u,v)\leq\frac{1}{1-\mu_{*}}e^{3G}.

With these estimates, we recall the derivative of
\gamma
with respect to
v
from (
B.4
)

(B.45)

\partial_{v}\gamma(u,v)=\int_{0}^{u}\frac{\partial_{v}r\partial_{u}r}{r^{2}}\frac{1}{1-\mu}\left(2\mu-4\pi\frac{r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}\right)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

By (
B.43
), (
B.44
) and the Doppler identity (
B.5
),
we deduce that

(B.46)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{u}\frac{\partial_{v}r\partial_{u}r}{r^{2}}\frac{1}{1-\mu}\left|2\mu-4\pi\frac{r^{2}|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{(\partial_{v}r)^{2}}\right|(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{5\pi}{3(1-\mu_{*})}e^{3G}B_{2}^{2}\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)(\partial_{v}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\leq\frac{5\pi}{6(1-\mu_{*})}e^{3G}B_{2}^{2}\int^{u}_{0}(-\partial_{u}r)(u^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\;\frac{5\pi}{6(1-\mu_{*})}e^{3G}B_{2}^{2}r(0,v)=\frac{5\pi}{12(1-\mu_{*})}e^{3G}B_{2}^{2}v\leq\frac{5\pi}{12(1-\mu_{*})}e^{3G}B_{2}^{2}(v_{*}+\hat{\xi}).

The estimate in (
B.46
) implies that the expression in (
B.45
) is valid by dominated convergence theorem. For each
(u,v)\in U_{\xi}(u_{2})
, we then establish

(B.47)

\displaystyle\gamma(u,v)\leq\gamma(u,v_{*})+\int_{v}^{v_{*}}|\partial_{v}\gamma|(u,v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq G+\frac{5\pi}{12(1-\mu_{*})}e^{3G}B_{2}^{2}(v_{*}+\hat{\xi})\xi.

If we pick

(B.48)

\xi:=\min\left\{\hat{\xi},\frac{12G(1-\mu_{*})}{5\pi e^{3G}B_{2}^{2}(v_{*}+\hat{\xi})}\right\},

we arrive at

\sup_{\mathcal{D}(0,u_{1},v_{*}+\xi)}\gamma\leq 2G
, contradicting (
B.40
).

Thus, for each
u_{1}\in(u_{0},v_{*})
, the hypothesis of Lemma
B.2
is verified. This then implies that (
B.38
) holds and hence
\sup_{\mathcal{D}(u_{0},u_{1},v_{*}+\Delta v)}|\alpha^{\prime}|
remains bounded as
u_{1}\rightarrow v_{*}
. Therefore, we conclude that the solution extends as a
C^{1}
solution up to the closure of
\mathcal{D}(u_{0},v_{*},v_{*}+\xi)
. In particular, with
C^{1}
initial data prescribed along
\{v_{*}\}\times[v_{*},v_{*}+\xi]
, we show that the solution further extends as a
C^{1}
solution to
\mathcal{D}(v_{*},v_{*}+\xi^{\prime})
for some
\xi^{\prime}\in(0,\xi]
. This completes the proof of Theorem
B.1
.
∎
